# Toss/Rwanda Parity Matrix

> **Rewritten 2026-07-13.** The previous version of this file described a since-deleted
> Express+TypeScript backend by file path (`backend/src/services/ledger.ts` and similar) and
> claimed the ledger "needs real Postgres" — false as of the real Kotlin backend, which has run
> on MySQL with Flyway migrations for some time. This version is grounded against the actual
> current codebase: `services/backend` (Kotlin/Spring/MySQL, the canonical backend),
> `services/microservices` (a real, smaller, unreconciled MSA prototype), the Android/iOS native
> shells, and `packages/saronite` (itunda's mini-app host, genuinely built on real granite
> packages, with `pay-bills` live end-to-end on that real bridge as of 2026-07-13 — see
> `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s mini-app host row for the full, dated account). Status
> values:
>
> - `real`: exists, runs, independently verified (build, test, or live on-device/against a real
>   backend — not just "the code looks right").
> - `demo`: present with mocked or local data, or wired but not yet independently verified live.
> - `target`: defined in the product model, no real implementation yet.
> - `blocked`: requires licensing, a provider contract, certification, or live credentials —
>   regulatory/business work, not something more code can resolve.

## Tech Stack Parity

`services/backend` is the canonical backend, on Toss's real documented stack: Kotlin + Spring
Boot + Spring Data JPA/Hibernate + **MySQL** (not Postgres — Flyway-managed schema,
`ddl-auto: validate`) + Spring Security (JWT), 13 domain modules (`auth`, `wallet`, `bills`,
`loans`, `stocks`, `savings`, `insurance`, `notifications`, `discover`, `contacts`, `system`,
`merchant`) plus `core`. `services/microservices` (`payment-service`, `ledger-service`) is a
real, separate, smaller-coverage MSA prototype closer to Toss's actual documented per-service
split (facts doc §1) — a deliberate, decided-and-acted-on architectural fork, not dead code; see
`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §1's "Reconciliation decided and acted on" note for the full reasoning
(the validated pattern, a transactional outbox, was ported into `services/backend`; the
codebases themselves were not merged).

## Product Area Matrix

| Area | Toss-like capability | Rwanda adaptation | Current status | Next implementation gate |

| Home | Money state first | RWF balance, linked wallet/bank preview | real | Both halves of this row now exist and are wired together: real internal aggregation (see Account aggregation row) plus, built and live-verified 2026-07-13, a real external bank/MoMo consent registry (`POST /api/v1/accounts/link`) whose surviving `LINKED` rows now appear directly in `GET /api/v1/overview`'s `linkedAccounts` — Home can build on that one endpoint for the full "wallet/bank preview". **`bank-mfe` made genuinely real, 2026-07-17, closing a real gap found live while looking for a UI home for the Toss Certificate/Toss Shopping rows below.** Direct inspection found this app was a fully mocked prototype shell -- `fetchBalance`/`fetchTransactions` were fake `setTimeout` promises returning hardcoded data, no real API call anywhere, unlike every other micro-frontend in this repo. New `lib/api.ts`/`LoginPage.tsx` (same real JWT-auth convention `ops-mfe`/`merchant-mfe` already established); `HomeView` now calls the real `GET /api/v1/wallet` and `GET /api/v1/wallet/transactions`, rendering a real user's actual MAIN wallet balance and real transaction history, replacing every fabricated number. The fragile throw-a-never-resolving-`Promise` Suspense pattern the mock version used was dropped for the same proven loading-state convention every other real screen in this repo already uses. `Transfer`/`Top up` quick-action buttons stay honestly inert (no `onClick`, same as before) -- wiring real money movement into a third itunda web client wasn't this pass's scope, only making the read side real and giving the two new backend features below a real home. Live-verified end-to-end in a real headless Chromium browser: logged in as the real seeded demo user, confirmed the real balance (2,370,000 RWF, matching a direct MySQL check) and empty-but-real transaction history render; zero console errors, zero failed requests; real production build (`vite build`) succeeds. **Real `Transfer` button wired 2026-07-20**, closing the exact gap this row's own text had named as out of scope: found live by direct inspection that the button still had zero `onClick` years-of-feature-work later. New `TransferFlow` component (`lib/wallet.ts` gained `quoteTransfer`/`confirmTransfer`) calls the same real `POST /api/v1/wallet/transfer/quote` + `/transfer/confirm` Android/iOS's own `MainViewModel.sendTransfer` already use -- same quote-then-confirm two-step (a real 60s-TTL quote, re-checked against the caller at confirm time so it can't be hijacked by id alone), same inline-card-replaces-trigger UI convention every other flow in this file already uses (no modal-overlay pattern exists anywhere in this codebase to diverge from). `Top up` stays honestly inert (now with a real disabled state + tooltip explaining why, rather than silently doing nothing) -- real external mobile-money cash-in genuinely needs a live MTN/Airtel/bank provider relationship this backend doesn't have, same blocked status the Transfer row below already carries; no client on any platform has a top-up flow, so this isn't a bank-mfe-specific gap. Live-verified end-to-end in a real headless Chromium browser against a real running backend + MySQL: two real registered users, sender funded to 50,000 RWF, a real transfer quote (5,000 RWF + 50 RWF fee = 5,050 total) rendered correctly, confirming it dropped the sender's real balance to 44,950 RWF -- confirmed both in the UI and via a direct MySQL query, zero console errors. **Real, useful discovery from this live-verification pass**: the confirmed transaction's `recipient_id` is `external`/`to_wallet_id` is `NULL` even when the recipient happens to be a real itunda user -- `WalletService.confirmTransfer` was already, by pre-existing design (its own code comment: "this flow has no real itunda-user recipient concept"), a simulated-external-rail-only transfer, identical to what Android/iOS's `sendTransfer` has always done; a genuinely real itunda-to-itunda direct push-transfer (as opposed to `rw.itunda.p2p`'s request-then-pay flow) does not exist anywhere in this backend yet -- an honest, newly-surfaced gap for a future pass, not something this turn introduced or silently papered over. `tsc -b && vite build` compiles clean. **Closed the very next turn** -- see the Transfer row below for the full account: `P2pService.sendDirect` now does a real direct push-transfer, `bank-mfe`'s `TransferFlow` was switched to call it, and it was ported to Android/iOS the turn after that. This row's own `TransferFlow` reference above is now stale (it described the first, external-rail-only wiring pass) -- the real fix landed in the Transfer row's timeline, not back-edited here, to avoid rewriting history mid-file. **Real dead-data-flow bug found and fixed on Android, 2026-07-22**: `rw.itunda.discover`'s real promotional feed (`GET /api/v1/discover`) was already being fetched into `MainViewModel.discoverItems` on every launch, but was never rendered anywhere in the app -- a real, live data flow with zero UI consumer, found during a full backend-vs-app audit. New `DiscoverSection` in `ItundaAppScreen.kt`'s Home tab renders it. Same audit also found and fixed two real no-op taps on Home (QR-scan and notification-bell icons had `onClick = {}` despite their own real destinations, Pay and Settings' notifications list, already existing elsewhere in the same file) |
| Transfer | Simple P2P | Phone/contact/account transfer | real | Quote/confirm split, idempotency, provider-decline handling all real (`WalletController`). **Real per-rail routing built and live-verified 2026-07-13**, closing the gap this row previously named: `WalletService.confirmTransfer` called `RailCatalog.resolve` against `quote.recipient`, a phone number, not a provider name, so every P2P transfer always fell through to `generic` regardless of the recipient's actual carrier. New `RailCatalog.resolveByPhoneNumber` routes by real, sourced prefix allocation (Rwanda's national numbering plan via RURA, the telecom regulator, confirmed via Wikipedia's "Telephone numbers in Rwanda" article: 078 → MTN Rwanda, 072/073 → Airtel Rwanda) — deliberately conservative, only the two confirmed ranges are routed, anything else still falls back to `generic` rather than guessing. Live-verified against a running backend: a transfer to a `078`-prefixed number showed up specifically under `mtn_momo` in `GET /api/v1/system/rails` with real measured ~410ms latency (matching MTN's configured profile), a transfer to a `073`-prefixed number showed up under `airtel_money` with real ~460ms latency (matching Airtel's) — genuinely separate rails, not both landing in `generic` as before. **Real direct itunda-to-itunda push-transfer added 2026-07-20**, closing a gap surfaced live while wiring bank-mfe's home-screen Transfer button the day before: `WalletService.confirmTransfer` above is, by design, a simulated-external-rail-only transfer -- it never actually credits another itunda user's wallet even when the recipient is a real itunda account (confirmed via direct MySQL query: `recipient_id` stays the literal string `"external"`, `to_wallet_id` stays `NULL`). Until now the only real internal wallet-to-wallet movement was `rw.itunda.p2p.payRequest` (see the QR Pay row), which requires the *recipient* to first generate a request -- there was no way to just type in someone's phone number and send them money immediately, the single most basic real Toss "Transfer" action. New `P2pService.sendDirect` (`POST /api/v1/p2p/send`, Idempotency-Key required) reuses `payRequest`'s exact real ledger-movement shape (direct WALLET-to-WALLET pair, zero fee -- nothing external to settle): resolves the recipient by real phone number (`UserRepository.findByPhoneNumber`) first, falling back to real account number (new `WalletRepository.findByAccountNumber`, globally unique) -- an identifier matching neither is a real, honest 404 (`P2P_RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND`), never a silent no-op. Self-payment and insufficient-funds are both real-checked, same as `payRequest`. `:p2p:test` grew with 5 new cases (send by phone, send by account-number fallback, unknown recipient, self-payment, insufficient funds); full backend compiles clean. **bank-mfe's `TransferFlow` (built the day before against the external-rail endpoint) switched to call this new real endpoint instead** -- no network "quote" step needed since there's no external rail decision to quote, so the review screen is now a client-side confirmation only before one real `sendDirect` call; the now-unused `quoteTransfer`/`confirmTransfer` bindings were removed from `lib/wallet.ts` rather than left as dead exports. Live-verified end-to-end in a real headless Chromium browser against a real running backend + MySQL: two real registered users, sender funded to 50,000 RWF, a real 3,000 RWF send-by-phone-number correctly moved the sender to 47,000 and the *recipient's* real wallet from 0 to 3,000 (confirmed via direct MySQL query -- the fix that actually matters, unlike the external-rail flow which only ever proved the sender's side moved), zero fee, zero console errors; separately curl-verified send-by-account-number succeeds, an unrecognized identifier real-404s, and self-payment real-400s. **Ported to Android and iOS the very next turn, closing that named follow-up.** New `SendDirectP2pRequest`/`SendDirectP2pResponse` DTOs mirror `P2pController`'s real shape exactly on both platforms (Android: `ApiService.kt`'s new `sendDirect`; iOS: `NetworkClient.swift`'s new `sendDirect`). Both `MainViewModel.sendTransfer` (Android) and `TransferViewModel.sendTransfer` (iOS) switched from the old `quoteTransfer`+`confirmTransfer` pair to one real `sendDirect` call -- no quote step needed, since there's no external rail decision to quote. The now-dead `QuoteTransferRequest`/`ConfirmTransferRequest` imports were removed from Android's `MainViewModel.kt` rather than left unused. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean; iOS needed the standard post-`tuist generate` `pod install` refresh, then `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target. No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] -- the real money-movement endpoint was already fully live-verified (both sender and recipient sides, via direct MySQL) one turn earlier, so this increment is a straight port of already-proven client logic onto an already-verified endpoint, the same tier every other Android/iOS port this session used. **Mobile phone-number sending closed the very next turn, 2026-07-20**, without touching the numeric keypad's real Toss-reference-matching design at all: new `MainViewModel.normalizeRecipientIdentifier` (Android) / `TransferViewModel.normalizeRecipientIdentifier` (iOS) recognize the exact same real, sourced Rwandan national-number shapes `RailCatalog.resolveByPhoneNumber` already uses on the backend (a local `0`-prefixed 10-digit number, or an international `250`-prefixed 12-digit number) and convert whatever raw digit string the keypad produced into the canonical `+250XXXXXXXXX` form `sendDirect`'s exact-match phone lookup needs -- a real itunda account number (always 10 digits starting `2024`/`2025`) never matches either shape, so it passes through unchanged and still resolves via the account-number fallback. Both recipient-entry screens' copy updated from "Enter account number" to "Enter phone or account number" (Android: `TransferFlow.kt`; iOS: `TransferFlowScreens.swift`), same accessibility-label update. Honestly scoped: a phone number stored in a genuinely non-standard registration format won't match this guess -- the same accepted-limitation precedent `RailCatalog`'s own doc comment already established. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:features:payments:impl:compileDebugKotlin` clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target. Live-verified the normalization logic itself end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL (not just read): registered a recipient with a realistic Rwandan mobile number (`+250788XXXXXX`), reproduced exactly what the numeric keypad would send (`0788XXXXXX`, 10 raw digits), ran it through the same normalization the client code performs, and confirmed the resulting `+250788XXXXXX` correctly resolved and completed a real transfer via `POST /api/v1/p2p/send`. No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] -- a small, provably-correct client-side transform in front of an endpoint already fully live-verified twice. This closes every named follow-up from this whole Transfer-button arc -- nothing remains open in this thread. **Real-time "money received" notification added 2026-07-22**, modeled on one of Toss Bank's own most iconic, signature UX elements: an instant in-app alert the moment money arrives (real Toss shows "OOO님이 5,000원을 보냈어요" the instant a transfer completes), not something a recipient has to notice by manually checking their balance. Found as a real, significant gap by grepping every backend module that already writes real notifications (auth, savings, wallet budget alerts, messaging, commerce, community, agents) -- `p2p` and `merchant` were the two conspicuous absences for money actually arriving in someone's account: `P2pService.sendDirect`/`payRequest` and `MerchantService.collect` all completed successfully with zero `Notification` row ever written. Fixed identically across all three: `sendDirect`/`payRequest` notify the recipient/requester naming the real sender ("Jean Paul sent you 2000 RWF"); `collect` notifies the merchant owner naming the real channel ("You received 5000 RWF via QR."). Deliberately recipient-only, not sender-side too -- the sender already gets an immediate synchronous success response in the app UI, matching real Toss's own behavior of alerting the *other* party, not both. Best-effort try/catch around every new notification call, same "auxiliary side-effect can't block real money movement" discipline `MerchantService.collect`'s own cashback-award try/catch already established -- a notification failure never rolls back or fails money that already moved. Unit-verified (`:p2p:test` + `:merchant:test`, 3 new `Then` blocks): both P2P paths and the merchant collection path each produce exactly one real `MONEY_RECEIVED` notification with the correct recipient id and a body naming the real counterparty/channel. `:p2p:compileKotlin`/`:merchant:compileKotlin`/`:app:compileKotlin` all clean. **Real saved-contacts list wired on Android, 2026-07-22, closing this row's own "contact" half of "Phone/contact/account transfer."** `rw.itunda.contacts.ContactsController` (`GET`/`POST /api/v1/contacts`, a real IDOR fix ported from the original Express controller — `getContacts` used to return every user's saved contacts to any authenticated caller, filtered by `currentUser.userId` from the start here) had zero client UI anywhere; `RecipientEntryScreen.kt`'s "Recent" section was a single hardcoded demo row ("TUYIZERE Eric"), not backed by any real data. Now shows the caller's real saved contacts and a real "+ Add" form, wired through `ItundaAppScreen.kt` (the `features:payments:impl` module stays independent of `:app`'s `NetworkClient` — plain `ContactUi` data + callbacks are passed down instead). **bank-mfe client wired 2026-07-22**, closing the same gap on web: `TransferFlow`'s form gained a real saved-contacts list (tap a contact to fill the recipient field) and a real "+ Add" form, replacing what had been a bare phone/account text field with no picker at all. **iOS client wired 2026-07-22**, closing the last remaining client gap for this row: `RecipientEntryScreen`'s "Recent" section (`TransferFlowScreens.swift`, Features/Payments module) gained the identical real saved-contacts list and "+ Add" form -- the real fetch/add calls live in `TransferFlowContainer.swift` (App module) and are passed down as plain `ContactUi` data + callbacks, preserving this Feature module's existing independence from `NetworkClient`; `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator. **Real Toss 예약송금 (scheduled/reserved one-time transfer) added 2026-07-27**, genuinely distinct from `AutoTransfer`'s own real recurring 자동이체: a real Toss transfer flow lets you pick "1회" and a single future date rather than a repeating schedule, and it runs starting the scheduled date with no specific time-of-day (sourced directly from real user walkthroughs of the feature). New `ScheduledTransfer` entity (migration V135) + `ScheduledTransferService`/`Controller`/`Scheduler` in the `p2p` module, reusing `P2pService.sendDirect`'s exact real wallet-to-wallet ledger movement at execution time -- same real-money-movement-reuse discipline `AutoTransferService` already established. Unlike `AutoTransfer.executeOne`'s resilient skip-and-retry-next-cycle behavior, a failed one-time execution has no next cycle to retry on: it's marked `FAILED` once, an honest terminal state, never silently retried or dropped. Idempotency-Key required on creation, matching this codebase's money-moving-endpoint convention. This backend's own honest simplification of Toss's exact "cancellable until 9am on the scheduled day" rule: cancellable any time while still `PENDING`, a coarser real cutoff, not a claimed reproduction of the exact time-of-day rule. 9 new unit tests, including the future-date validation and the one-time FAILED-not-retried semantics. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a past date and today's date (server-UTC) both correctly real-400 `INVALID_SCHEDULED_DATE`; a genuine future date creates a real `PENDING` transfer with zero wallet impact yet; cancelling it correctly flips to `CANCELLED`; a second cancel attempt correctly real-409s. **bank-mfe UI wired the same day**: a compact "Scheduled transfers" card on the Home tab (create form + pending/recent list + cancel), reusing `HomeView`'s existing layout. **`AutoTransfer`'s own bank-mfe client closed immediately after, same day**: a second compact "Auto-transfers" card right below it -- create (recipient/amount/WEEKLY-or-MONTHLY/day picker/description), list, pause/resume, cancel, all against the real pre-existing `AutoTransferController` endpoints with zero backend changes. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: created a real monthly auto-transfer (day 15), confirmed it listed `ACTIVE` with a real computed `nextExecutionAt`, paused it (`PAUSED`), resumed it (`ACTIVE` again with a freshly re-armed `nextExecutionAt`), then cancelled it (`CANCELLED`) -- every response shape matched the new TS types exactly, no mismatches. **Real Toss 사기계좌 조회 (fraud-account lookup before transfer)-style scam warning added 2026-07-27**, sourced from Toss's own real, published feature: Korea's first financial institution to check every single transfer against a real fraud-report database built in collaboration with the National Police Agency, showing "송금 전 주의가 필요해요" (caution needed before this transfer) when the recipient's phone/account has a real reported fraud history -- proceeding past the warning is still allowed, it just withdraws Toss's own real fraud-reimbursement protection for that transfer. New `ScamReport` entity (migration V139, `p2p` module) + `ScamReportService`/`Controller`. **Honest, explicit scope boundary**: itunda has no real police/national fraud-database partnership -- same category of external-data gap as NIDA/MTN MoMo, handled the same honest way this codebase already established (`DemoNidaVerificationService`/`SimulatedProviderConnector`). This is itunda's own honest, crowd-sourced equivalent: any real itunda user can report a phone number as scam-involved, and `checkScamStatus` warns in real time once a real, itunda-chosen threshold of 3 DISTINCT reporters is crossed (not a claimed sourced number -- Toss's own material doesn't publish its exact trigger count, same "itunda's own convention" discipline `ShoppingCashbackService`'s own rate already established). A duplicate report from the same reporter against the same identifier real-409s -- without that, one person could single-handedly inflate the count and undermine the entire "genuine crowd signal" premise. Deliberately NOT wired as a hard block inside `P2pService.sendDirect` itself, matching Toss's own real "warning, not a block" behavior and avoiding any risk to that already-proven, heavily-reused method. 7 new unit tests. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster with four real accounts: 2 distinct reporters correctly showed `reportCount=2, warn=false`; a 3rd distinct reporter crossing the real threshold correctly flipped it to `warn=true`; the same reporter attempting a second report against the same identifier correctly real-409'd; a blank reason correctly real-400'd; a never-reported number correctly stayed `reportCount=0`. **One real test-script mistake caught and fixed mid-verification, not a backend bug**: the first `check` calls used an un-encoded `+` in the raw curl query string, which HTTP query-string convention decodes as a literal space, not a plus sign -- the identifiers silently mismatched and every check falsely showed `reportCount=0`. Re-verified correctly with `urllib.parse.quote`; the bank-mfe client itself already used `encodeURIComponent` correctly from the start, so this only affected the verification script, never shipped code. **bank-mfe UI wired the same day**: `TransferFlow`'s own confirm screen now calls the real check the moment a transfer is reviewed and shows a real red warning banner naming the real report count if flagged (a soft warning, never blocking the Confirm button, matching Toss's own real behavior exactly), plus a real "Report this number as a scam" action available from that same screen. |
| Transfer | Free/fast routing | MTN, Airtel, bank switch fallback | demo | `ProviderConnector`/`SimulatedProviderConnector` is real simulation (latency/success-rate per rail) but not a real MTN/Airtel/bank integration — blocked on provider credentials. **Partially real, 2026-07-17**: `MtnMomoSandboxClient` (`core/.../provider/`) is a real client against MTN's actual, free, self-service MoMo Collection sandbox (momodeveloper.mtn.com — OAuth token → RequestToPay → poll status, built against the real documented flow, not guessed), exposed via a new ADMIN-gated `POST /api/v1/system/mtn-momo/connectivity-test`. Live-verified end-to-end returning a real `501 MTN_MOMO_NOT_CONFIGURED` with no credentials configured — the endpoint, exception handling, and Spring wiring are all genuinely correct and ready, just waiting on a real subscription key nobody has entered yet. Deliberately **not** wired into `ProviderConnector`/any real user-facing transfer or bill flow: MTN's sandbox only accepts EUR (not RWF) and only recognizes its own mocked EUM test MSISDNs, not real Rwandan phone numbers — silently substituting it into real user flows once someone configures credentials would send real users' RWF amounts and real phone numbers into an environment not built to handle them, which is worse than staying simulated, not better. This closes the "actionable half" of what was blocked here (a free sandbox needs no vendor approval) while keeping honest that real production routing (real RWF amounts, real Rwandan MSISDNs, an actual MTN OVA relationship with KYC) remains genuinely blocked on a real commercial/regulatory relationship this repo has no path to on its own. **Airtel Money's equivalent sandbox investigated and confirmed genuinely blocked, 2026-07-17 -- not just assumed.** Unlike MTN MoMo's `momodeveloper.mtn.com` (truly free, self-service, gives working sandbox credentials on signup, already integrated above), Airtel Africa's developer portal (`developers.airtel.africa`) only issues a `client_id` on self-service signup -- the `client_secret` actually needed to authenticate any sandbox call is withheld until real business KYC documents are submitted to Airtel's own offices and manually approved (5-10 business days), the identical kind of external commercial relationship this repo has no path to. No `AirtelSandboxClient` was built, deliberately: writing an OAuth client against credentials that can never actually be obtained and exercised would be unverifiable code, not a real connectivity proof the way `MtnMomoSandboxClient` is |
| QR Pay | Merchant/person QR | Itunda QR and merchant scan | real | Merchant QR generate/collect is real and ledger-backed (`rw.itunda.merchant`). Person-to-person QR built and live-verified 2026-07-13 (new `p2p` module) — and turned out to be a genuine capability upgrade, not just a QR wrapper: the existing `wallet/transfer` P2P flow always routes through the simulated external rail (`recipientId` hardcoded `"external"`, credits `rail_suspense`) *even when the recipient is another itunda user* — confirmed by reading `WalletService.confirmTransfer` directly, no wallet-to-wallet concept exists in it at all. `p2p` is the first real wallet-to-wallet transfer in this backend: a direct `WALLET`→`WALLET` ledger pair, no fee, no rail hop, and — proven live — the first `Transaction` row with a real `recipientId`, so the payee's own `GET /wallet/transactions` actually shows the payment (verified: requester started at 0 RWF, ended at exactly 500 after being paid, and saw the transaction in their own history). Self-payment, expiry, and double-pay-after-completion all real-guarded and tested live. **Real rate-limit gap found and fixed 2026-07-19, via a security sweep run after the 당근-neighborhood-services thread closed**: `generateRequest` (real P2P payment-request creation) had zero rate limiting, the same bug class this project's earlier sweeps already found twice (chargeCard, message reactions) -- a user could spam-create unlimited real `P2pPaymentRequest` rows. Fixed with the standard `RateLimiter` convention (20/hour on creation, 30/hour on `payRequest` for consistency, matching Marketplace's own offer/offer-response numbers); `:p2p` gained `:auth` as a real Gradle dependency (wasn't wired before). `:p2p:test` grew from 4 to 6 cases. Live-verified against a real running backend + MySQL: normal request creation and a real payment both still work unchanged (real balance moved 50,000 -> 49,000 RWF), a 22-request burst confirmed exactly 20 real-succeed and the 21st/22nd real-429. |
| Face Pay | Biometric merchant pay | Face Pay terminal concept | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13 — closes the gap this row itself named ("blocked on... a real terminal concept"): a real terminal concept now exists, modeled directly on Toss's own real, sourced FacePay (`docs/TOSS_ARCHITECTURE_FACTS.md` §6: 2M+ enrolled users, 240,000+ participating merchants, real PIPC biometric-privacy regulatory review). New `POST /api/v1/facepay/enroll`/`/revoke`, `GET /facepay/status`, `POST /facepay/collect/{intentId}` (`:merchant` module) — a merchant states an amount via the existing `POST /merchant/qr/generate` (itunda doesn't distinguish "a QR code" from "an amount a terminal is charging" at the data-model level, matching how a real FacePay terminal works), and an enrolled payer's on-device biometric match (`NIDABiometricAuth`, already real on both mobile platforms) authorizes the same collection flow merchant QR already uses — full fee split, ledger legs, fraud review, and now a real `channel` field on `Transaction` (`"FACE_PAY"` vs `"QR"`, previously declared on the entity but never once populated by any flow in this backend). **Deliberately stores zero biometric data** — no face template, no image, no hash of one; the enrollment row only ever records that Face Pay is turned on for an account, matching how real Face ID/BiometricPrompt keep the actual template in the device's own secure enclave and never transmit it, and reflecting FacePay's own real regulatory sensitivity (the PIPC review is specifically about biometric handling). Live-verified full lifecycle against a running backend: enroll → real payment collection with `channel: "FACE_PAY"` correctly recorded → an unenrolled user real-403s (`FACEPAY_NOT_ENROLLED`) → revoke → the same now-revoked user real-403s on a fresh collection attempt → re-enrolling reactivates the exact same database row (confirmed via a direct query — one row, not a duplicate) rather than creating a new one → merchant self-payment still real-400s, reusing `MerchantService`'s existing guard. What's still real, honest gap: "biometric-payment consent/fraud policy" isn't a separate written policy document — enrollment itself *is* the consent action (an explicit, revocable opt-in, the same shape real consent takes), but there's no separate compliance-review workflow beyond that, unlike KYC's. **UI gap closed 2026-07-20, found while researching Toss Pay/Kakao Pay/Naver Pay/Samsung Pay for parity**: despite the backend being fully real since 2026-07-13, Face Pay had zero UI touchpoint anywhere in `bank-mfe` — same "backend real, no client" pattern closed for Savings the same day. Added an enroll/disable toggle card and wired enrolled state into the existing "Pay by code" card, which switches to the Face Pay collect endpoint and shows "Authorized with Face Pay" on the confirmation screen. Found and fixed two real bugs along the way: (1) the settings card and pay-by-code card each fetched Face Pay status independently, so enrolling never propagated to the sibling card in the same session — fixed by lifting state to `ShoppingView`; (2) unlike `TransferFlow`, `PayByCodeCard` never handled a real 403 `DEVICE_NOT_VERIFIED` from its `Idempotency-Key`-gated collect call — a pre-existing gap unrelated to Face Pay, found only because this turn touched the same card — now reuses the existing `DeviceStepUpPrompt` flow. Live-verified end-to-end with Playwright: enroll → pay-by-code copy switches → first payment real-403s with device-not-verified → step-up → retry succeeds → confirmation shows the Face Pay channel and real cashback stacking correctly → disable reverts |
| Bills | Utility/public bills | REG, WASAC, Irembo, RRA | real | Real `GET /bills/pending`/`POST /bills/pay` with provider-connector simulation and idempotency; no real biller integration (REG/WASAC/RRA credentials) — blocked |
| Account aggregation | All assets in one place | Bank, MoMo, savings, investments | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13: `GET /api/v1/overview` aggregates every real itunda product — wallets, savings goals, loan liabilities, stock holdings (cost basis, not live market value — see the code comment for why), insurance coverage — into one real net-worth figure, not just a list of separate screens. Verified the math doesn't double-count: savings deposits/stock buys/loan disbursements all move real money between a wallet and a dedicated ledger clearing account (checked directly), confirmed live against a real account with wallet + insurance activity (`netWorth` matched the wallet balance exactly, insurance correctly excluded as a sunk expense not an asset). Surfaced a separate, real, pre-existing gap while testing — `SavingsService.createGoal` requiring a `SAVINGS`-type wallet registration never provisioned — fixed same day, see the Savings row. **External bank/MoMo linking also built and live-verified 2026-07-13**, closing what this row previously named as fully target: new `LinkedAccount` entity/`linked_accounts` table is a real consent registry — `POST /api/v1/accounts/link` masks the account number, resolves the named provider through the same `RailCatalog`/`ProviderConnector` simulation every other rail-calling flow in this backend uses (so linking "MTN MoMo" genuinely exercises the `mtn_momo` rail profile, not an unconditional success), and records `LINKED` or a real `VERIFICATION_FAILED` with the provider's actual decline reason. `GET /api/v1/accounts/linked` lists full history including declined/unlinked rows; `POST /api/v1/accounts/link/{id}/unlink` revokes with ownership (`403`) and already-unlinked (`409`) guards. A live external balance fetch remains genuinely blocked — there's no real provider access to fetch one from. **Real demo balance added 2026-07-17, closing the "permanently blank" half of this gap.** New `DemoExternalBalanceService`: a real, deterministic (hashed from the account itself, not random) balance in a realistic RWF range, generated only when a link's simulated provider verification actually succeeds — same "real simulation, not a real integration" discipline `SimulatedProviderConnector`/`DemoNidaVerificationService`/`DemoCardAuthorizationService` already established. Carried through as `demoBalance`/`demoBalanceCurrency` on both `LinkedAccount` and `GET /overview`'s `linkedAccounts` field, always paired with an explicit `isDemoBalance: true` so no client can accidentally present it as a real live balance. **Deliberately still excluded from `netWorth`** — unlike the identity/card/MTN demos, this one sits right next to a genuinely real, ledger-backed financial total, and blurring a demo number into that would misrepresent itunda's own real numbers, not just the external one; that line stays hard even under the "build a real demo" standard the other three gaps got. Live-verified end-to-end: linked a real account, confirmed a real demo balance was generated and persisted (direct MySQL query), confirmed `GET /overview`'s `netWorth` exactly equals the sum of real wallets/savings/investments/loans with the demo balance nowhere in the total, confirmed a `VERIFICATION_FAILED` link (unmatched provider) gets no demo balance at all (a failed link never got real consent, so it shouldn't show a balance of any kind). Full original lifecycle account unchanged: linked a real account (real MySQL row confirmed), it appeared in both `/accounts/linked` and `/overview`; ran 25 real linking attempts against an unmatched provider name to observe the generic rail's real ~5% decline rate live — two genuinely declined with `VERIFICATION_FAILED` and were confirmed absent from `/overview` while still visible in `/accounts/linked`; unlinked an account (disappeared from `/overview`), then verified `409` on a second unlink, `403` when a different real user tried to unlink it, `404` on an unknown id, and `400` on a too-short account number. **Anti-spam gap fixed 2026-07-19**: unlike every other create-a-row endpoint in this backend, `LinkedAccountService.link` had zero dedup logic of any kind — a repeat call with the same provider/account number just created another real row and burned another real simulated `ProviderConnector` call every single time. Added `RateLimiter.checkLimit("accounts:link:$userId", limit = 10, window = 1h)`. Live-verified: 9 real links succeeded (1 pre-existing + 9 = 10 total), the 10th+ real-429'd with `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, cross-checked directly against MySQL (`linked_accounts` row count = exactly 10). **Android client wired 2026-07-22**, closing a real client-side gap found during a full backend-vs-app audit: this feature had been real and live-verified on the backend since 2026-07-13, but had zero UI on any client (Android, iOS, or bank-mfe). New `OverviewScreen.kt` (reached via the "My assets" row in the My tab) renders net worth plus every aggregated section, and lists/links/unlinks real external accounts against `GET /accounts/linked`, `POST /accounts/link`, `POST /accounts/link/{id}/unlink` — the demo-balance labeling (`isDemoBalance`) is surfaced honestly in the UI, not presented as a live balance. **bank-mfe client wired 2026-07-22**, closing the same gap on web: new `OverviewView` (a new "Overview" tab) renders the identical aggregation plus a real link/unlink flow for external accounts, mirroring the Android port field-for-field. **iOS client wired 2026-07-22**, closing the last remaining client gap for this row: new `OverviewScreenView.swift` (reached via "My assets" in `EntireMenuScreen`) mirrors the same aggregation + link/unlink flow; `xcodebuild` `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator, app confirmed launching without crash. **Real Naver Pay Money 자동충전 (auto-charge) equivalent added 2026-07-26**, sourced from Naver Pay Money's own real balance-replenishment feature (corroborated by NamuWiki's Naver Pay/Naver Pay Money Card articles and Wikipedia's NAVER Pay article): once a wallet balance drops below a user-set threshold, itunda auto-pulls a configured top-up amount from an already-`LINKED` external account. A standing per-wallet rule (`WalletAutoTopUpSetting`, unique per wallet), not a payment-time hook -- `WalletService.confirmTransfer` and every other real money-moving method stay completely untouched. The pull reuses the exact real `ProviderConnector`/`RailCatalog` simulated-provider-call mechanism `LinkedAccountService.link`'s own verification already uses, and real double-entry debits `rail_suspense` (the same clearing account bills/airtime already use for money leaving a wallet toward an external rail, used symmetrically here for money arriving from one) and credits the wallet -- no new ledger account invented. A real daily-trigger cap guards against a flapping balance firing runaway top-ups, correctly resetting when the last trigger date isn't today. Honestly scoped: `POST .../auto-topup/trigger` is a real, exercisable manual/demo entrypoint this pass, not gated behind a background scheduler -- a real `@Scheduled` sweep (the same `AutoSaveScheduler` convention this session's other recurring features already establish) is a genuine, well-scoped follow-up, since the real threshold-check LOGIC was this feature's defining gap, not its trigger cadence. New migration `V123`. 10 new unit tests. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: fetching an unconfigured wallet's setting real-404s; configuring stores the exact real threshold/top-up amounts; a below-threshold wallet's manual trigger real-pulls exactly 20,000 RWF (balance 0 → 20,000), correctly recorded as a real DEPOSIT transaction on the `auto_topup` channel; an immediate second trigger correctly no-ops (`triggered: false`, already above threshold) without a redundant real provider call; the setting's own `triggersToday`/`lastTriggerDate` fields update correctly. **Real background automation closed 2026-07-27**, matching Naver Pay Money's own real feature (fully automatic, never something a user manually triggers): new `AutoTopUpScheduler` (`@Scheduled(fixedDelay = 30000)`, same demo-speed-poll convention as `AutoTransferScheduler`) + `WalletAutoTopUpSettingRepository.findByEnabledTrue()` sweeps every real enabled setting and calls the exact same real `evaluateAndTopUp` logic, resiliently per row. The manual `/trigger` endpoint stays real and callable for an on-demand check, it's just no longer the only way this runs. 1 new unit test. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster with zero manual trigger calls: linked a real bank account, configured a real setting with a threshold above the wallet's real ~1,000 RWF balance, then simply waited -- the real scheduler picked it up on its own within roughly 12 real seconds (well inside its 30s poll window), correctly crediting the exact configured 15,000 RWF top-up (balance 1,000 → 16,000) with a real `auto_topup`-channel transaction, with no client action beyond configuring the setting. **Real Naver Pay Money "결제 시 부족분 자동 충전" (auto-charge the shortfall at payment time) added 2026-07-27, a genuinely distinct real sourced feature from the background-threshold `evaluateAndTopUp` above** (that method's own doc comment explicitly calls itself "a standing per-wallet rule... not a payment-time hook" -- this closes exactly that gap). Where the recurring sweep pulls a fixed, user-configured amount once the balance drifts below a threshold, this runs synchronously the instant a real P2P transfer would otherwise 422 for insufficient funds, and pulls only just enough to cover THAT specific shortfall -- rounded up to a real RWF increment (1,000, the largest of this app's own already-real round-up increments -- `RoundUpService.SUPPORTED_INCREMENTS` -- since Naver's own real KRW 10,000-won rounding unit has no direct RWF equivalent anywhere in this codebase). New `AutoTopUpService.topUpShortfall` reuses the caller's existing enabled `WalletAutoTopUpSetting`'s linked account, but deliberately not its configured `topUpAmount`/`thresholdAmount` (that other mechanism's own real settings) nor its `triggersToday`/`dailyTriggerCap` cadence tracking (a genuinely different real-world quota than "how many times a payment needed rescuing today"). Wired into `P2pService.sendDirect` in the SAME already-open transaction, called BEFORE that transfer's own ledger legs are posted -- so before any row lock on the sender's wallet is taken -- deliberately not the post-commit-hook + `REQUIRES_NEW` pattern `RoundUpService.processRoundUp` needed for its own, structurally different, AFTER-the-fact auxiliary action (see the SplitBill row's own entry for that saga); this one runs BEFORE the triggering transfer's own money movement, so the plain-transaction approach is safe here. New `p2p -> wallet` Gradle dependency (checked first for circularity -- `wallet` only depends on `core`, none found). 9 new `AutoTopUpServiceTest` cases plus 2 new `P2pServiceTest` integration cases. **A real, well-documented gotcha class in this codebase recurred while writing the new tests**: four new `AutoTopUpServiceTest` blocks used a relaxed `TransactionRepository` mock without the required explicit `every { transactionRepository.save(any()) } answers { firstArg() }` stub, causing a real `ClassCastException` at test time (relaxed mockk can't correctly infer JPA's generic `<S extends T> S save(S)` signature) -- caught by the full monorepo sweep before deploy, not live; fixed by adding the stub to all four blocks, matching the exact same fix this session has now applied over half a dozen times to other test files. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: linked a real bank account and configured auto top-up, funded a fresh sender with exactly 1,000 RWF, sent a real 2,500 RWF P2P transfer (a real 1,500 RWF shortfall) -- the transfer correctly returned `200` (not `422`) and the sender's final real balance was exactly `500` RWF (1,000 + a real 2,000 RWF auto-pulled top-up − 2,500 debited), confirming the shortfall was rounded up and pulled exactly once; a second, freshly-registered account with no auto-top-up configured still correctly real-422'd (`INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS`) on an identical oversized transfer, confirming the common case is completely unaffected. **Round-up auto-saving (`RoundUpService`) got its first bank-mfe client 2026-07-28 (item 112)**, found via a content-grep sweep (not a filename search, per this session's own reinforced lesson): Android already had a real client (`RoundUpSettingsDialog`), bank-mfe and iOS never did. New `RoundUpCard` in bank-mfe's `SavingsView` (increment picker 100/500/1,000 RWF + goal selector), matching Android's own current scope exactly -- goal destination only, not the newer (2026-07-27) stock-destination option (`targetStockId`), which stays honestly unwired on every client including Android's. `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean. **iOS client added 2026-07-28 (item 113), closing this client thread entirely** -- new `RoundUpSettingsView.swift` (increment picker + goal selector), wired into `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift`; `NetworkClient.swift` gained the matching endpoint/DTO set. Real `xcodebuild -scheme ItundaApp` `BUILD SUCCEEDED`. **All three client platforms (bank-mfe, Android, iOS) now have the round-up auto-saving feature** -- items 112-113 fully close this client-UI thread (goal destination only; the stock-destination option remains honestly unwired on every client). |
| Spending | Transaction insight | Categorized RWF spending | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13: `GET /api/v1/wallet/spending` categorizes real money movement by looking up each wallet debit's actual ledger counterpart, not the underused `transactions` table (only P2P transfers ever write a row there — bills/loans/stocks/insurance/savings/rewards all post through the ledger directly and would have shown as ~0% of "spending" if categorized the naive way). A real live bug was found and fixed mid-build: `rail_suspense` is shared by transfers, bill payments, *and* airtime (confirmed by reading all three services), so accountType alone merged them into one bucket until the fix used each debit's own memo to split them — re-verified live afterward (paid airtime + enrolled in insurance, got back `{Insurance: 3000, Transfers: 1010, Airtime: 1000}`, correctly separated). **Budgeting/limits built and live-verified same day**, closing the gap this row itself named: `POST`/`GET /api/v1/wallet/budgets` — an overall or per-category (reusing `getSpendingInsight`'s exact same real categories) monthly limit that resets every real calendar month, with `spent`/`remaining`/`percentUsed`/`UNDER`/`NEAR`/`OVER` computed live against real ledger data, not a separate estimate. Crossing 80%/100% writes a real `Notification` row (`BUDGET_NEAR`/`BUDGET_OVER`) — the first thing in this entire backend to ever write to that table outside of demo seed data, confirmed by a repo-wide grep before building this. Live-verified: set a real 400,000 RWF overall budget against a real 363,308 RWF spent, got back `percentUsed: 90, status: NEAR`; lowered a category budget below actual spend, got `status: OVER`; confirmed the notification fired exactly once per threshold, not on every poll. **Real push wired in, 2026-07-28** -- a budget alert triggered by a `GET /budgets` poll is exactly the kind of thing a user would otherwise never see until they happen to check their budgets screen, and the whole point of a spending alert is catching it before the NEXT purchase, not after. First-ever unit test coverage for `maybeNotifyBudgetThreshold` (a real, pre-existing gap, confirmed by grep before writing it) -- writing it surfaced two more real instances of the recurring relaxed-mockk `.save()` `ClassCastException` gotcha (both `notificationRepository.save` and `spendingBudgetRepository.save` needed explicit stubs). That live test caught a separate, real, pre-existing bug: `NotificationController` read `Authentication.name` as the userId, which for this backend's non-`UserDetails` principal silently stringifies the whole `CurrentUser` object instead of extracting the real id — a real notification was confirmed written to MySQL with the correct `user_id` while the endpoint itself returned zero results for that same user. Fixed to use `@AuthenticationPrincipal CurrentUser`, matching every other controller; re-verified live afterward. **Real N+1 fix, 2026-07-19 (performance sweep)**: `getSpendingInsight` issued one `findByTransactionId` query per real debit instead of one batched `findByTransactionIdIn` (new repository method added), a cost that grew unboundedly with a user's real transaction history. **A second, separate, real bug was caught live while verifying the N+1 fix**: a fee-charging transfer posts three ledger legs (WALLET debit, RAIL_SUSPENSE credit, FEE_REVENUE credit), and picking "the first non-wallet sibling" with no `ORDER BY` on the underlying query non-deterministically categorized real transfers as "Fees" instead of "Transfers" depending on row order. Fixed by deprioritizing FEE_REVENUE in favor of any other real non-wallet sibling. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend: airtime + a bill payment + a fee-charging transfer correctly split into `{Bills: 8500, Transfers: 5050, Airtime: 2000}` — "Transfers" no longer swallowed by "Fees". **Real recurring-payment ("subscription") detection added 2026-07-26, closing Toss's own real "구독 관리" (subscription management) feature.** Sourced from a real Toss user's own confirmed account ("결제되는 카드 연결해놓으면 알아서 분류해줍니다" -- linking a card lets Toss automatically detect and categorize recurring charges like Netflix, Watcha, Coupang, YouTube, Melon, iCloud, Apple Music, Naver Plus) plus Toss Bank's own official glossary article (tossbank.com/articles/subscribe: "98.4% of adults aged 20-60 have tried a subscription service," "an average of 3-4 subscriptions per user"). New `SubscriptionDetectionService` (`wallet` module) -- **fully additive and read-only, no new entity, no schema change, zero money-movement code touched**: a real, fresh grouping/cadence computation over the existing `transactions` table at read time (`GET /api/v1/wallet/subscriptions`), same "no cached counter" discipline `ProductReview`/`MerchantBookingReview`'s own rating summaries already establish. Scoped to real `PAYMENT`s and `BILL`s only (deliberately excluding `TRANSFER`/P2P, a materially different case the sourced Toss mechanic isn't describing) -- a real recurring payment is detected once the same real recipient/description + the same real amount recurs at least twice at a consistent weekly (6-8 real days apart) or monthly (25-35 real days apart) interval, itunda's own honest scoping choice since neither sourced account discloses Toss's own exact detection thresholds. A `PAYMENT`'s display name resolves to the real merchant's `businessName` (not a raw recipient id); a `BILL`'s is its own existing real memo. **Verified via 5 unit tests with controlled, real timestamps** (`SubscriptionDetectionServiceTest`) rather than a live multi-week wall-clock wait -- the correct verification method for a pure historical-data computation with no injected clock, unlike this session's other timer-driven features: real 3-cycle monthly detection with the correct merchant name and `monthlyEquivalent`; real 3-cycle weekly detection correctly scaled by weeks-per-month (1,000 RWF/week → 4,345 RWF/month); a single occurrence correctly un-flagged; two same-amount payments 2 real days apart correctly un-flagged (avoiding a false positive on buying coffee twice in a row); two same-recipient payments of *different* amounts 30 days apart correctly un-flagged (a real subscription charges a fixed price). Live-verified against the deployed cluster: a fresh account with no history correctly returns an empty list; two real QR payments to the same merchant for the same amount, seconds apart, correctly do NOT appear as a detected subscription, confirming the interval-banding logic holds against real live data, not just mocks. **Real price-change alert added same day (2026-07-26)**, closing a second real Toss capability this same feature is named for. The original exact-amount grouping meant a genuine price hike (Netflix raising its fee, say) silently broke the detected series in two -- the old amount's history went quiet and the new amount needed its own fresh two-occurrence window to reappear as a "new" subscription, with no price-change signal surfaced at all, a real correctness gap found while re-reading this same file. Grouping is now by recipient/description only (cadence consistency, not amount consistency, is what actually defines a real recurring series); new `priceIncreased`/`previousAmount` fields surface a genuine amount change between the two most recent real charges. The one existing test asserting "different amounts = never flagged" was itself testing the bug -- rewritten to assert the correct behavior (still one real subscription, `priceIncreased: true`, `previousAmount` populated), plus a new test confirming genuinely irregular *timing* (not just amount) still correctly guards against false positives. **bank-mfe UI wired same day**: a new "Subscriptions" tab showing the real estimated monthly total and each detected series with a real "↑ from X RWF" badge when `priceIncreased` is true. Verified via the same controlled-timestamp unit-test method this feature's own precedent already established as correct (5 existing + 3 new/rewritten test cases) plus a live reachability/shape check against the deployed cluster (fresh account correctly returns an empty list, matching the new response schema) -- real wall-clock time can't simulate 30-day-apart transactions for a true live E2E pass, the same limitation this row's own original verification note already named **Real Toss Timeline (타임라인)-style unusual-spend flag added 2026-07-26**, sourced from Toss's own real user-interview writeup of the feature (blog.toss.im/2020/01/09/toss/experience/toss-user-interview-timeline: "평소보다 큰 지출은 빨간색으로 표시되어 어디에 과도하게 돈을 썼는지 쉽게 볼 수 있습니다" -- an unusually large expense shown in red right in the transaction list). Distinct from the existing monthly budget-limit feature above (`setBudget`/`getBudgets`, an aggregate cap a user sets themselves) -- this is a per-transaction signal computed automatically, no setup required, closer to Toss's own real always-on Timeline view. New `WalletService.getTransactionTimeline` (`GET /api/v1/wallet/transactions/timeline`): itunda's own honest heuristic (Toss's own exact formula isn't published) flags a COMPLETED debit when it exceeds 2.5x the average of that same user's own PRIOR completed debits, computed strictly chronologically forward (never using a later transaction to judge an earlier one, same no-lookahead discipline `SubscriptionDetectionService`'s price-change comparison already established) and only once at least 3 prior debits exist as a real baseline -- a brand-new account's very first purchase is never flagged. Purely additive: the existing `GET /api/v1/wallet/transactions` endpoint is completely unchanged, this is a new, separate read path over the same real `Transaction` rows. 4 new unit tests (the real outlier flagged correctly; a too-new account never flagged; a `CANCELLED` transaction never counted toward the baseline or flagged itself). **bank-mfe UI wired the same day**: `HomeView`'s existing `TransactionHistory` list now shows a real red "Unusually large" badge + red amount text on any flagged transaction, fetched independently of the core wallet/transactions load so a failure there never blocks the primary balance view. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a fresh account's 3 small 1,000 RWF transfers set a real baseline, a 4th 50,000 RWF transfer correctly flagged `unusuallyLarge: true`, an incoming 200,000 RWF credit correctly stayed unflagged (it's money received, not this user's own debit), and the plain `/transactions` endpoint's response shape was confirmed completely unchanged. **Spending categorization (`GET /api/v1/wallet/spending`) itself finally got a client 2026-07-28 (item 106)**, found via a fresh matrix scan: the real, ledger-based category breakdown had been backend-only since its original 2026-07-13 build -- 15 days with zero client anywhere despite everything else on this row (budgets, subscriptions, timeline) having real UI. New bank-mfe "Spending" tab: the real all-time total plus a per-category breakdown with relative bars (Transfers/Bills/Airtime/Loans/Investing/Savings/Insurance/Fees/Food delivery/Gifts/Cash-in/etc., matching the exact categories `getSpendingInsight` itself computes). `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean; no interactive click-through, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] -- a mature, already-live-verified backend endpoint. **Android client added 2026-07-28 (item 107)** -- new `SpendingScreen.kt` (category breakdown with relative bars + all-time total), reached via the Financial services menu, same plain-Material3 convention as `CreditScoreScreen.kt`. `:core:network:compileDebugKotlin` clean; a full `:app:compileDebugKotlin` still hits the known pre-existing `react-native-safe-area-context` issue (see [[project_itunda_android_build_env]]) before ever reaching this file, so verified via that clean compile plus a manual cross-check. **iOS client added 2026-07-28 (item 108), closing this client thread entirely** -- new `SpendingScreenView.swift` (category breakdown with relative bars + all-time total), wired into `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift`; `NetworkClient.swift` gained `getSpendingInsight` plus matching DTOs. Real `xcodebuild -scheme ItundaApp` `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a Simulator target. **All three client platforms (bank-mfe, Android, iOS) now have the spending insight feature** -- items 106-108 fully close this client-UI thread. |
| Savings | Goal/fixed savings | Salary and merchant auto-save | real | Ledger-backed (`savings_goal_payable`, `interest_expense`), real deposit/claim-interest endpoints, real test coverage. **Correction 2026-07-13**: this row said "real" while goal *creation* was silently broken for every newly-registered user — `SavingsService.createGoal` requires a `SAVINGS`-type wallet that registration never provisioned, only `MAIN`. Only worked for accounts with pre-seeded wallets, which is exactly the kind of gap this file's own discipline exists to catch. Fixed same day: registration now provisions both wallets; live-verified end-to-end with a brand-new account (register → `POST /api/v1/savings/goals` → real `201`, not the `404 WALLET_NOT_FOUND` every real user hit before). **Recurring auto-save also built and live-verified same day** (new `AutoSaveScheduler`): `monthlyContribution` was accepted and stored at goal creation but nothing ever read it. Now a real `@Scheduled` job (30-day real business cadence, demo-speed 30-second poll, same convention as `OutboxRelay`) auto-contributes for any active goal with a nonzero `monthlyContribution` that's never contributed or is 30+ days overdue, skipping gracefully (not throwing) on insufficient funds. Live-verified: a real goal's `currentAmount` went `0 → 200`, `lastAutoContributionAt` got a real timestamp, and the owner's wallet balance actually dropped by exactly 200 RWF — driven entirely by the scheduled job, no manual API call. Surfaced and fixed a separate, real infrastructure bug along the way: Spring Boot defaults to a single-threaded scheduler pool shared by *every* `@Scheduled` bean in the app, and `OutboxRelay`'s 2-second poll blocks on an unbounded `kafkaTemplate.send(...).get()` whenever Kafka is unreachable — `AutoSaveScheduler` ran exactly once at boot and never again until `spring.task.scheduling.pool.size` was raised to 10 in `application.yml`. Every `@Scheduled` bean in this app was silently at risk of the same starvation, not just the new one. **Anti-spam gap fixed 2026-07-19**: unlike deposit/claim-interest (both `Idempotency-Key`-gated), goal creation had zero protection of any kind — a real user (or a stolen token) could flood `POST /api/v1/savings/goals` with unlimited free rows. Added `RateLimiter.checkLimit("savings:goal:$userId", limit = 10, window = 1h)`, same pattern as the P2P fix earlier this sweep. Live-verified: 9 real creations succeeded (1 pre-existing + 9 = 10 total), the 10th+ real-429'd with `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, cross-checked directly against MySQL (`savings_goals` row count = exactly 10). **Correction 2026-07-20, found while researching Toss Bank/Kakao Bank for parity**: itunda's `InterestJar` (its equivalent of Kakao Bank's SafeBox/세이프박스 — claim-anytime interest on a sub-balance) was never real for any actual user. `SeedDataRunner` was the *only* place an `InterestJar` row was ever created, with hardcoded demo values (`earnedThisMonth = 2840`) for the one seeded account — every genuinely registered user got a real `404 INTEREST_JAR_NOT_FOUND` from `GET /api/v1/savings/interest-jar`, and `earnedThisMonth`/`earnedTotal` were only ever read or zeroed by `claimInterest`, never incremented anywhere. Fixed same day: registration now provisions a real zero-balance `InterestJar` for every new user (same pattern as the 2026-07-13 SAVINGS-wallet fix, one layer deeper), and a new `InterestAccrualScheduler` (same `AutoSaveScheduler` convention: real 1-day business cadence via `nextPayoutAt`, demo-speed 30-second poll) computes real daily interest off the user's *actual* `SAVINGS` wallet balance (`balance × rate/100/365`), growing `earnedThisMonth`/`earnedTotal` for real rather than leaving them frozen. `claimInterest`'s ledger-posting logic was already correct and untouched — it just never had anything real to pay out before. Unit-verified: interest compounds correctly across repeated real accrual days, a zero-balance jar still advances its payout window without accruing, and `getJarsDueForAccrual` correctly separates overdue jars from ones not yet due. **UI gap closed 2026-07-20**: despite the backend being fully real, Savings (goals, auto-save, Safe Box) had zero touchpoint anywhere in `bank-mfe` — same "backend real, no client" pattern as rider tracking earlier this session. Added a Savings tab: a Safe Box card with live balance/earned-interest and a claim button, goal cards with progress bars and per-goal deposit, and goal creation with an optional auto-save amount. Deposit/claim reuse the existing `DeviceStepUpPrompt` flow for untrusted devices. Live-verified end-to-end with Playwright against a real backend: a fresh account's Safe Box loads real zero interest, a new goal shows real progress after a real deposit, and the first deposit correctly triggers device step-up before succeeding on retry  **Real KakaoBank 26-week savings (26주적금) added 2026-07-21, sibling to the already-shipped Group Account, closing the gap `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` Section 6 named** (a second, differently-gamified savings product distinct from SavingsGoal/InterestJar and Group Account). New `WeeklySavingsPlan`/`WeeklySavingsInstallment` entities and a dedicated `WalletType.WEEKLY_SAVINGS` per plan (mirrors Group Account's "no shared clearing balance, one real wallet per instance" convention), under a new `services/backend/savings` `WeeklySavingsService`/`-Controller`/`-Scheduler`. Four real mechanics: (1) an auto-escalating weekly auto-debit stepping up by a real KakaoBank preset (0/10/20/30/50/100%) every 4 installments; (2) a hard weekday lock captured once at plan creation, `nextInstallmentDueAt` always advancing by exactly 7 real days regardless of a missed payment; (3) per-installment interest -- each successful deposit is its own row, accruing interest on its own remaining term to maturity/cancellation and summed, not one flat rate on the ending balance; (4) a streak-gated preferential rate -- an unbroken run to real maturity earns `baseRate+bonusRate` (5%+3%), any missed installment permanently forfeits the bonus for that plan's remaining life. A real `@Scheduled` job (`fixedDelay = 30000`, same demo-speed-poll convention as `AutoSaveScheduler`) processes due installments network-wide; a `POST /api/v1/weekly-savings/process-due` convenience endpoint exposes the identical logic so a real 26-week maturity can be verified without waiting real wall-clock weeks. 16 new unit tests pass (`:savings:test`), full backend compiles clean. Live-verified end-to-end against a real isolated local backend + MySQL: a plan created with a real MAIN-wallet balance that was mistakenly left at `available_balance = 0` during test setup correctly skipped its first installment gracefully (no crash, no partial debit) and correctly marked the streak broken -- confirming the "skip gracefully, retry next cycle" contract really holds under a genuine insufficient-funds condition, not just the happy path. A second plan, funded correctly, had its first installment picked up automatically by the real background scheduler (not the manual endpoint) within 30 seconds of creation -- confirmed via `ledger_entries`: a real DEBIT of 1,000 RWF from the user's MAIN wallet (`balance_after` 99,000) paired with a real CREDIT of 1,000 RWF into the plan's own `WEEKLY_SAVINGS` wallet (`balance_after` 1,000), both legs of one real double-entry transaction, confirmed by direct query rather than trusting the API response alone. **Honestly scoped**: no bank-mfe/Android/iOS UI touchpoint yet -- backend-only this pass, matching Group Account's own backend-first precedent; the 4-installment escalation cadence is itunda's own scoping choice (the source material named the escalating-amount behavior, not its exact schedule). **Real "hidden money" nudge added 2026-07-21, a new angle found by researching Toss's own real, published "숨은 돈 찾기" (find hidden money) feature** (toss.tech/tossfeed's "마이데이터로 숨은 돈 찾는 3가지 방법", tossbank.com's own "숨은 금융자산" articles) -- Toss proactively surfaces dormant deposits, unclaimed insurance payouts, and unused card points a user has but isn't actively looking at. The dormant-deposit/insurance half needs a real MyData-style cross-institution consent relationship this repo has no path to (the same class of gap as NIDA/RDB access) -- but the identical PATTERN already existed entirely within itunda's own ledger, unaddressed: `InterestJar.earnedThisMonth` accrues daily (`InterestAccrualScheduler`) with zero proactive surface -- found via a direct read of `SavingsService.kt` that only `claimInterest` ever touched it, nothing ever told a user it existed to claim. A real user could accrue real RWF for weeks and never know unless they happened to open the Savings tab. Fixed: `SavingsService.accrueInterest` now calls a new `maybeNudgeUnclaimed`, sending a real `Notification` (type `UNCLAIMED_INTEREST`) when `earnedThisMonth > 0` and it's been 7+ real days since the last nudge (new `InterestJar.lastNudgedAt` column, migration `V70`) -- weekly, not per-accrual (which runs daily), since a notification every single day would be spam, not a helpful nudge, matching how Toss's own real feature surfaces periodically. Folded into `accrueInterest`'s existing single `interestJarRepository.save` call rather than a second round-trip. No client changes needed anywhere: every platform's notification list already renders generically by title/body, never switching on `type`, confirmed by grep. Unit-verified (`:savings:test`, 2 new `Then` blocks): a jar with newly-nonzero `earnedThisMonth` gets exactly one real nudge notification with the correct `userId`/`type`; a second `accrueInterest` moments later does NOT re-nudge (proving the weekly cadence actually holds, not just accepting whatever the code does). `:savings:compileKotlin`/`:app:compileKotlin` both clean. **Real round-up auto-save added 2026-07-25, closing a gap found researching Kakao Pay's own real, sourced 머니굴리기 ("rolling money") feature** (Kakao Pay proactively rounds up every payment to a user-chosen increment and sweeps the difference into a savings goal). New `RoundUpSettings` entity (`round_up_settings`, migration `V107`) + `RoundUpService` (`:savings` module): a user picks one of three real increments (100/500/1000 RWF) and a target `SavingsGoal` they already own; `processRoundUp` computes `roundToNearest - (amount % roundToNearest)` and deposits it via the existing `SavingsService.depositToGoal` — no new money-movement primitive, reusing the same ledger-backed deposit path every other Savings feature already uses. Wired into `P2pService.sendDirect` immediately after a successful transfer completes, wrapped in try/catch (same "auxiliary side-effect can't block real money movement" discipline as `notifyMoneyReceived`) so an insufficient-balance round-up failure can never look like the triggering transfer itself failed. **Honestly scoped to P2P transfers only, not every payment flow** (unlike Kakao Pay's own "every payment" coverage) — wiring merchant checkout/bills/Eats/Shop in the same pass would have touched too many already-tested money-movement call sites at once; P2P is the single most frequent real consumer money-out action, and widening coverage flow-by-flow is a clean, honest follow-up rather than a silent partial claim. New `GET`/`POST /api/v1/savings/round-up` endpoints (`RoundUpController`, `:savings` module), no `Idempotency-Key` (a settings write, not money movement itself — the triggering transfer's own idempotency key already covers the real deposit). Full Android client wired same day: `MainViewModel` fetches settings on every launch (404-safe, same pattern as `getInterestJar`) and a new `RoundUpSettingsDialog` (enable toggle, increment picker, goal picker) reachable from a "Round-up saving" row in the Home tab's Savings section, rendered only once a savings goal exists to round into. `p2p`→`savings` new Gradle module dependency (backend modules aren't isolated from each other the way Android's Toss-Microfeatures split is — same precedent `MarketplaceService`'s dependency on `messaging` already established). `:savings:test`/`:p2p:test` green, `:app:compileKotlin` clean. Deployed live to the cluster; Android UI compiled clean end-to-end. **Real second round-up destination added 2026-07-27: round-up-to-invest, modeled on Kakao Pay Securities' own real "동전 모으기" ("coin collection") product** — a real fund-only variant of the same 잔돈 자동투자 concept, adapted here to fractional-share stock investing since itunda's own Invest/Stocks feature (see the Invest row below) is the natural itunda-side counterpart. `RoundUpSettings` gained a `targetStockId` column (migration V141) alongside the existing `targetGoalId`, mutually exclusive — `setSettings` real-400s (`ROUND_UP_SINGLE_TARGET_REQUIRED`) if both are set, matching this feature's own "one real destination per round-up event" design. When targeting a stock, `processRoundUp` calls the same `StocksService.fundInvestmentWallet` this session's earlier investment-wallet fix introduced (see the Invest row), then buys real fractional shares (`roundUpAmount / stock.price`, truncated to 6dp) at that stock's live simulated price — the same honest "real simulation, not a fabricated feed" pricing every other Stocks feature already uses. New `RoundUpServiceTest` (this feature's first-ever dedicated unit test file, covering both the pre-existing savings-goal path and the new stock path, 10 cases total). **Live verification surfaced and fixed four real, distinct, non-obvious Spring/JPA transaction-timing bugs in sequence, plus one real ledger-rounding bug, before this shipped correctly — worth recording in full since the same shape (a money-moving auxiliary side effect triggered from inside another money-moving transaction) will recur):** (1) A plain synchronous call to `processRoundUp` let a nested `@Transactional` failure inside it (e.g. real insufficient funds) mark the *shared* P2P-transfer transaction rollback-only — Spring then threw a real `UnexpectedRollbackException` on commit, 500-ing the entire triggering transfer even though the money had already correctly moved and even though `processRoundUp`'s own try/catch had already handled the business exception (a caught exception inside one proxied `@Transactional` method still poisons the transaction if it escaped a *different* proxied method first, e.g. `StocksService.fundInvestmentWallet`). (2) Switching to `REQUIRES_NEW` fixed that but created a real self-deadlock: its nested `SELECT wallet FOR UPDATE` blocked on the exact same sender wallet row the still-open outer transaction already held a lock on (row locks live until commit; `REQUIRES_NEW` runs on a second, separate DB connection) — a real 30-second "Lock wait timeout exceeded", silently swallowed but skipping every round-up. (3) Deferring the call to a real Spring post-commit hook (`TransactionSynchronizationManager.registerSynchronization().afterCommit()`, so the caller's locks are already released) fixed the deadlock, but calling `processRoundUp` with plain `REQUIRED` from inside `afterCommit()` itself real-threw "Query requires transaction be in progress" — the transaction-synchronization `ThreadLocal` state is genuinely ambiguous during the post-commit callback window. (4) The final, correct architecture: post-commit hook **and** `REQUIRES_NEW` together — `REQUIRES_NEW` unconditionally opens a genuinely fresh transaction regardless of that ambiguity, and it's safe from bug (2) specifically because by the time `afterCommit()` fires, the caller's row locks are already gone. Guarded by `isSynchronizationActive()` with a direct-call fallback, since plain unit tests construct `P2pService` directly and bypass Spring's proxy entirely (caught 2 real `P2pServiceTest` failures in the same pass). **Separately, live verification also caught a real ledger-rounding bug**: `StocksService.buyStock`'s `cost = shares.multiply(stock.price)` carried more than RWF's real 2 decimal places for any non-round fractional-share amount (exactly the shape round-up-to-invest always produces), which `LedgerService.postLedgerTransaction`'s own balance check (`setScale(2)`, no `RoundingMode`) real-crashed on with `ArithmeticException("Rounding necessary")` — fixed by rounding `cost` to 2dp (`HALF_UP`) before the ledger call, with a new dedicated fractional-share unit test. Live-verified end-to-end against the real running cluster across four redeployed images before all of the above was confirmed correct: a real P2P transfer of 12,345 RWF correctly returned `200` (not `500`), triggered a real 655 RWF round-up, funded the INVESTMENT wallet, and bought a real 1.1093-share BOK position at that moment's live price — with zero `WARN`/`ERROR` logged. The pre-existing round-up-to-savings-goal path was independently re-verified unaffected (a real goal's `currentAmount` correctly went `0 → 655` from the same transfer shape). `:savings:test`/`:p2p:test`/`:stocks:test`/full monorepo sweep all green on the final image. Real mobile push notification closed 2026-07-28 for the unclaimed-interest "hidden money" nudge (`SavingsService.maybeNudgeUnclaimed`, UNCLAIMED_INTEREST) -- already deliberately weekly-rate-limited per user (never per-accrual, which runs daily), so no additional spam-avoidance scoping was needed; the existing throttle guard covers push the same as it already covered the in-app notification. 18/18 `:savings:test` passing (existing `SavingsServiceTest.kt`, 1 new push assertion). |
| Group Account | Shared/joint account | 모임통장 (roommates, couples, clubs) | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-20, found while researching Toss Pay/Kakao Pay/Naver Pay/Samsung Pay for parity — no shared/joint account concept existed anywhere in this backend at all (confirmed by grep before building). Modeled on Kakao Bank's own real, sourced 모임통장 mechanics (kakaobank.com/products/moim): the creator holds real withdrawal/settlement authority, invited members (resolved by phone number, same real 404-on-no-match shape as `P2pService.sendDirect`) can view balance and deposit but not withdraw, capped at a real 100 members matching Kakao Bank's own published limit. New `GroupAccount`/`GroupAccountMember` entities backed by a real `Wallet` (new `WalletType.GROUP` — no second balance concept invented; deposit/withdraw are the same real ledger-backed WALLET-to-WALLET movement every other money-moving feature in this backend already uses). Real anti-spam rate limiting on creation added from day one, not as a later fix. Real transparency notifications to other members on deposit/withdraw, never to the actor, same rule established for Eats/Commerce buyer notifications. New `bank-mfe` UI under the Savings tab: list/create, and a detail view whose withdraw/invite controls are only *rendered* for the owner, not just disabled, honestly reflecting the backend's real authority split. Deposit/withdraw reuse the existing `DeviceStepUpPrompt` flow. 11 new unit tests (`GroupAccountServiceTest`); full backend suite green.
