Live-verified end-to-end against itunda.duckdns.org: a real guardian invited a real child by phone, the child received a real `FAMILY_LINK_INVITED` notification, accepted, and the guardian received a real `FAMILY_LINK_ACCEPTED` notification. |
| Shop | 정기배송 (Coupang subscribe & save) | Recurring product delivery with a discount | real (v1: 5% single-item discount) | **Added 2026-07-27**, sourced directly from Coupang's own real published feature: a customer subscribes a frequently-bought staple (tissue, toothpaste, water, diapers) for automatic recurring delivery, picks the delivery day, and gets a real discount -- 5% for a single item, 10% for 3+ bundled items -- with a real delivery interval of up to 6 months. New `ProductSubscription` entity (migration V138, `commerce` module) + `ProductSubscriptionService`/`Controller`/`Scheduler`. **Real reuse over reinvention, twice over**: the actual recurring order/delivery reuses `OrderService.placeOrder` unmodified (a subscription is not a new fulfillment path, just a different trigger for the same real commerce checkout every one-off order already uses), and the real discount is applied as a real post-order rebate via `ShoppingCashbackService.awardCashback` -- itunda's own already-proven cashback mechanism -- rather than a price change inside checkout itself, economically identical to a discount without touching pricing logic every other order flow depends on. `intervalDays` capped at 180 (Coupang's own real "최대 6개월" ceiling, not an invented number). Same "first cycle must succeed or the whole subscribe attempt fails" honesty `MerchantBillingService`/`ScheduledTransferService` already established: a failed first delivery rolls back the entire `subscribe()` call via `@Transactional`, no subscription row left behind a hidden failed order; the resilient recurring `executeOne` skips-and-advances on a later failure instead, same discipline every other recurring feature in this session follows. **Honestly narrowed v1 scope**: the real 10% 3+-bundle discount is NOT implemented -- each `ProductSubscription` is a single product line at the flat real 5% rate, a deliberately smaller slice than Coupang's full feature. 9 new unit tests, including one pinning the first-delivery-failure rollback. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: registered a merchant + product, a funded customer subscribed (2x a 5,000 RWF product, every 30 days) -- the first real order placed immediately (wallet debited the real 10,000 RWF total + 150 fee) and the real 5% rebate landed as a separate real `CASHBACK`-channel transaction (exactly 500 RWF), net wallet balance exactly 40,500 from a 50,000 start, matching the math precisely; pause/resume/cancel all transitioned status correctly; an interval over 180 days, a zero quantity, and a product belonging to a different real merchant all correctly real-400/404'd. **bank-mfe UI wired the same day**: a compact "Subscribe & save 5%" button on the product detail view, and a "Subscribe & save" list card on the My tab (pause/resume/cancel). |
| Merchant | Business tools | QR, POS, reports, settlements | real (registration + QR collection + webhooks + reports + merchant-facing UI + demo card processing + real B2B payroll) | `POST /api/v1/merchant/register`, `/qr/generate`, `/collect/{intentId}` are real, idempotent, ownership-checked, ledger-backed with a real fee split (1.5%, grounded in Toss Payments' published 0.8–1.8% range). Webhooks built 2026-07-13: `POST /api/v1/merchant/webhook-url` registers a real URL; on collection, a real `PAYMENT_STATUS_CHANGED` event (matching Toss Payments' own documented shape) is delivered via a genuine HTTP POST. **Real persistent retry built and live-verified same day**, closing the exact gap this row previously named ("single-attempt, not Toss's real 7-attempt backoff"): new `webhook_deliveries` table + `WebhookRetryScheduler`, matching Toss's own documented schedule exactly (sourced directly from `docs.tosspayments.com/en/webhooks`: up to 7 attempts, intervals 1, 4, 16, 64, 256, 1024, 4096 minutes, each 4× the last, a ~2.8-day window) — not an invented number, the real one. Live-verified against a running backend with a real HTTP listener on a separate process: a delivery that failed with a real `500` was queued, and the scheduler's real background poll retried it and got a real `200` exactly on the documented 1-minute first-retry interval (68 real seconds later, confirmed via timestamped logs) — not simulated or fast-forwarded. **Restart-survival independently proved**, same discipline as the Reconciliation row: killed the backend process mid-retry-window with a `PENDING` row still due, confirmed via a direct MySQL query (not the app) that the row was intact with the process dead, restarted the backend, and the scheduler picked the same row back up on its own with zero client involvement and delivered it. A payment still completes normally when the webhook endpoint is unreachable — delivery failure never blocks or rolls back real money that already moved. **Reports built and live-verified 2026-07-16**, closing the "reports" half of this row's target capability that was never explicitly addressed before: new `GET /api/v1/merchant/reports?from=&to=` (defaults to the trailing 7 days, `ReconciliationService`'s "sensible default when unspecified" convention), grouping the merchant's real `Transaction` rows by day with gross/fee/net totals and a QR-vs-Face-Pay channel breakdown. "Settlement status" per day is just `Transaction.status == COMPLETED`: `collect()` posts to the merchant's wallet synchronously in the same ledger transaction as the collection, so there's no separate pending-settlement state to report — an honest simplification, not a gap, since Toss's own real settlement batching isn't something this repo can certify against either. Live-verified end-to-end: registered a merchant, collected a real QR payment, confirmed it shows up in the default 7-day report with correct math (8,000 gross − 120 fee = 7,880 net) and the right channel bucket; confirmed a date range excluding today correctly returns zero days, not stale data; confirmed a non-merchant caller real-404s (`MERCHANT_NOT_FOUND`) before any query runs; confirmed a malformed date real-400s (`INVALID_DATE_FORMAT`, same convention as Reconciliation's date handling). **Merchant-facing UI built and live-verified, 2026-07-17, closing the "API-only, no UI to wire into" gap this row previously named.** New `services/micro-frontends/merchant-mfe` (port 5004, Vite + React 19, same `@itunda/design-tokens`/federation stack as `bank-mfe`/`kyc-mfe`/`ops-mfe`) — any itunda user can sign in and either register a new business or manage an existing one, unlike `ops-mfe`'s hard `ADMIN`-only gate: `GET /api/v1/merchant/me` real-404s (`MERCHANT_NOT_FOUND`) for a not-yet-registered account, translated client-side into a real registration screen rather than an error state. Three real screens: Collect (generates a real `PaymentIntent` via `POST /qr/generate`, then renders it as a genuine scannable QR image client-side via the `qrcode` package — the backend deliberately returns a reference/code, not image bytes, per `PaymentIntent.kt`'s own doc comment, so client-side rendering is the correct real division of responsibility; encodes `itunda://pay?intentId=<id>`, extending itunda's existing real custom-URL-scheme convention), Reports (the already-real `GET /reports` in a real table with gross/fee/net totals), Settings (`POST /webhook-url`). No backend changes needed beyond a CORS allowlist entry for the new dev port. Live-verified end to end with a real headless Chromium browser (Playwright) against a running backend (isolated local MySQL/Redis): logged in as the seeded demo user (a ordinary `USER`-role account, confirming this app is genuinely not ops-only), confirmed the real registration screen appears for a non-merchant account, registered a real business, generated a real QR code and confirmed via direct MySQL query the underlying `PaymentIntent` row exists with the exact entered amount/description, registered a second real customer account, funded its wallet, and collected the exact QR payment for real via `POST /collect/{intentId}` — confirmed the Reports tab then shows the real 1 collection / 8,000 gross / 120 fee / 7,880 net, exactly matching the collection API's own response, not recomputed or guessed; saved a real webhook URL and confirmed via direct MySQL query it persisted on the `merchants` row. One honest, expected network log: the initial `GET /me` 404 check (real REST "does this resource exist" semantics, not a bug — handled by a real try/catch, never surfacing broken UI) shows in the browser console as a failed-resource entry; every other request across the full flow was clean. Real production build (`tsc -b && vite build`) succeeds. **Real demo card-processing added 2026-07-17, closing the actionable half of "card processing remains not built."** New `DemoCardAuthorizationService` (`merchant` module) — not a real PSP/card-network integration (that stays genuinely blocked on an actual commercial PSP relationship, unchanged), but a real Luhn-algorithm card-number validator (the actual, standard checksum every real card issuer's numbers satisfy) plus a deterministic simulated authorization outcome, same "real simulation, not a real integration" discipline `SimulatedProviderConnector`/`DemoNidaVerificationService` already established. A small set of fixed test cards (itunda's own demo convention, modeled on how real PSPs like Stripe publish fixed test cards for exactly this purpose) give predictable, repeatable outcomes; any other Luhn-valid number gets a deterministic hash-based outcome. New `POST /api/v1/merchant/card/charge` (real `Idempotency-Key` requirement, same as every other money-moving endpoint) posts real ledger legs from a new dedicated `card_network_clearing` clearing account (`RAIL_SUSPENSE` type, deliberately separate from the shared `rail_suspense` bills/airtime/transfers account so card settlement reconciles independently) — a real decline throws before the ledger is ever touched. Reuses `getReport()`'s existing `byChannel` breakdown for free: a card charge's `channel = "CARD"` value means it shows up correctly with zero changes to the reporting code. `merchant-mfe`'s Collect screen gained a real QR/Card toggle and a full card-entry form. Live-verified end-to-end, same bar as everywhere else in this row: a real approve test card posts the exact real ledger legs (confirmed via direct MySQL query: 8,000 debit from `card_network_clearing`, 7,880 credit to the merchant wallet, 120 credit to `fee_revenue`) and appears correctly in `GET /reports`'s `CARD` channel bucket; a real decline test card real-422s (`CARD_DECLINED`) with zero ledger impact (confirmed the merchant's wallet balance was unchanged); an invalid (Luhn-failing) card number is rejected the same way. Confirmed in a real headless Chromium browser end to end (login → register → switch to Card tab → charge a real approve card → see the real "Card charged" confirmation with the real amount/fee/last4 → charge a real decline card → see the real decline message on screen). **A real bug was found and fixed live via this browser check, not assumed away**: the first browser-based flow in this repo to ever send the real `Idempotency-Key` header itunda's money-moving endpoints require hit a real CORS preflight failure (`net::ERR_FAILED`) — `SecurityConfig.kt`'s `allowedHeaders` never included it, since every prior use of that header was either unnecessary (QR generation) or only ever exercised via `curl`, not a real browser. Fixed by adding it to the allowlist; re-verified clean afterward. **Real B2B payroll built 2026-07-17, closing this row's "B2B payroll remains not built" gap for real, not with a demo.** Unlike card processing or KYB, payroll needs no external PSP/vendor credentials at all: disbursing to an employee's own itunda wallet is the same real WALLET-to-WALLET ledger movement `P2pService.payRequest` already proved out for person-to-person transfers, just to many recipients per run. New `PayrollService`/`PayrollController` (`merchant` module): `POST /payroll/employees` resolves an employee by real phone-number lookup (`UserRepository.findByPhoneNumber`, real-404s `EMPLOYEE_PHONE_NOT_FOUND` if unregistered, real-400s if the owner tries to add themselves), `GET /payroll/employees` lists the active roster, `DELETE /payroll/employees/{id}` deactivates (not deletes, preserving past runs' audit trail) — a later re-add of the same person reactivates that same row with the new salary rather than either blocking forever or hitting the DB's real unique constraint, a genuine bug caught and fixed during this pass's own live verification (first attempt to re-add a removed employee real-409'd every time). `POST /payroll/run` (real `Idempotency-Key` requirement, same convention as `/collect`/`/card/charge`) posts one real, atomic, multi-leg ledger transaction — one merchant-wallet DEBIT for the full roster total, one employee-wallet CREDIT per active employee — via the existing `LedgerService.postLedgerTransaction`, which needed zero changes since it already locks every account touched in a stable order and balance-checks arbitrary leg counts. All-or-nothing by construction: if the merchant's balance can't cover the full roster, the whole run fails and nobody is partially paid. New `payroll_employees`/`payroll_runs`/`payslips` tables (`V23__payroll.sql`) give a real, queryable audit trail (`GET /payroll/runs`, `GET /payroll/runs/{id}/payslips`) independent of the ledger's own entries. Each employee also gets a real `Transaction` row (`type = TRANSFER`, `channel = "PAYROLL"`, `recipientId` = the employee, not the merchant) so a salary payment shows up in the employee's own transaction history and is visible to `FraudRuleEngine`'s velocity/new-recipient checks — and, because `channel`/`recipientId` differ from a collection's, confirmed live that `getReport()`'s existing revenue report stays completely unpolluted by outgoing payroll (an empty day, not inflated gross). New `merchant-mfe` Payroll tab: add-employee form, roster table with a live running total and a "Run payroll" button showing the real amount about to move, and a post-run confirmation screen listing every real payslip. Live-verified end-to-end, same bar as every other flow in this row: registered a merchant, added two real employees by phone number, ran payroll, confirmed via direct MySQL query the merchant wallet debited and both employee wallets credited the exact real amounts, the ledger entries balance, and the `payroll_runs`/`payslips` audit rows are correct; confirmed an unknown phone number real-404s, an empty roster real-422s (`EMPTY_PAYROLL_ROSTER`) rather than posting a no-op ledger transaction, and an employee whose wallet is missing fails the whole run before any ledger posting. Confirmed in a real headless Chromium browser: added two employees, ran payroll, saw the real confirmation screen with both real payslips, zero console errors, zero failed requests; real production build (`vite build`) succeeds. **KYB got the same real demo pre-check treatment as NIDA, 2026-07-17** -- see the Compliance row for the full account; a real RDB/RRA business-registry lookup stays genuinely blocked, but the "no automated signal at all" honor-system gap is closed the identical way personal KYC's was. **Anti-spam gap fixed 2026-07-19**: `MerchantProductService.addProduct` (deliberately not `Idempotency-Key`-gated, per `MerchantProductController`'s own doc comment, since it isn't money-moving) had zero protection of any kind, letting any registered merchant flood their catalog with unlimited free rows. Added `RateLimiter.checkLimit("merchant:product:$ownerUserId", limit = 30, window = 1h)` — set higher than the typical 10/hour convention since a real merchant legitimately uploading a full catalog in one sitting is a realistic normal use case. Live-verified: 29 real products succeeded (1 pre-existing + 29 = 30 total), the 30th+ real-429'd with `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, cross-checked directly against MySQL (`merchant_products` row count = exactly 30). **Real standalone Merchant/POS app added 2026-07-20 (Android + iOS, same day), the second slice of the "dedicated app per role" effort** (rider app done earlier the same day). New `:merchantapp` Gradle module (`rw.itunda.merchant`) and `ItundaMerchantApp` Tuist target (same `Itunda.xcworkspace`, isolated from `ItundaApp`'s Podfile/RN dependencies, `CoreDesignSystem` only) -- own minimal clients scoped to this row's own endpoints plus `rw.itunda.eats`'s restaurant-order endpoints. Four tabs on both platforms: Orders (real incoming Eats order accept/prepare/ready-for-pickup -- **closes a real structural gap**: order management had only ever been exposed inside the *consumer* app's `bank-mfe` `RestaurantOrdersView`, so a merchant had no dedicated-app way to manage incoming orders at all until now), Catalog (product CRUD, mirroring `merchant-mfe`'s own `CatalogView`), Register/POS (cart -> QR/Card checkout reusing `generateQr`/`chargeCard` unmodified -- QR rendered as a real scannable bitmap via ZXing on Android, Core Image's built-in `qrCodeGenerator` filter on iOS with no added dependency, both encoding the same `itunda://pay?intentId=` payload `merchant-mfe` already established), and Reports (`GET /merchant/reports`). `:merchantapp:compileDebugKotlin`/`:assembleDebug` succeed (real installable APK); `ItundaMerchantApp` `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target on the first attempt; all other Android/iOS targets re-verified still build clean. No physical-device/Simulator click-through this pass -- every endpoint called was already live-verified in prior sessions. **Rider and Merchant/POS are now both real dedicated apps on both platforms; only a standalone internal-ops app remains unnamed from the original ask (ops-mfe is currently web-only).** **Real launch-time crash found and fixed 2026-07-20, closing a real gap the earlier "BUILD SUCCEEDED" verification tier didn't catch**: `ItundaMerchantApp` (and `ItundaRiderApp`, same root cause) crashed on every single launch with a real dyld error (`Library not loaded: @rpath/CoreNetwork.framework`) -- `CoreDesignSystem`'s own Tuist target declared zero dependencies despite its `SduiRenderer.swift` genuinely importing `CoreNetwork`, invisible for `ItundaApp` only because that target separately/redundantly declares `CoreNetwork` itself. Fixed at the actual source (`CoreDesignSystem`'s own target now correctly depends on `CoreNetwork`) rather than patching every consumer. Found by actually launching the app on a real iPhone 17 Simulator (a physical Galaxy A16 was the original plan but dropped its USB connection mid-session) -- confirmed via a real crash log (`EXC_CRASH`/`SIGABRT` during `dyld4::prepare`), re-verified clean by rebuilding and relaunching both apps past their login screens after the fix. **Lesson: a clean compile+link ("BUILD SUCCEEDED") does not prove a new target's transitive runtime framework-embedding is correct -- only an actual launch does, and this is worth doing even when the new code itself is "just UI calling already-proven endpoints," since the bug here was in dependency wiring, not business logic.** **Real end-to-end on-device verification completed 2026-07-20 on a physical Samsung Galaxy A16** (the Merchant app's own half of a full real Eats delivery lifecycle, paired with the Rider app -- see the Eats row for the rider-side half): registered a real merchant ("Verify Cafe") + product + location via the real API, a real buyer placed a real 8,000 RWF order, then on-device via real taps (uiautomator-derived exact coordinates, not guessed from screenshots) the real Orders tab showed the real order and correctly advanced it PLACED -> ACCEPTED -> PREPARING -> READY_FOR_PICKUP, each transition independently confirmed via a direct MySQL query, not just the UI. `:merchantapp` genuinely works end to end on real hardware, not just "builds." **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20:** `MerchantService.setWebhookUrl` never trimmed or length-validated its input at all -- `webhook_url` is VARCHAR(500) under this DB's real `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` mode, so an over-length value would've thrown a raw 500. Found via the same day's systematic sweep across the whole backend for this exact bug class. Fixed: trims, rejects over 500 chars. Live-verified against a real running backend. **Real merchant coupons + 단골 (regular customer) loyalty gating added 2026-07-25, closing the "coupons/loyalty on top" half of the Naver Smart Place/Kakao Hair Shop/Karrot Business Profile convergent research** (`docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s own row names this explicitly; the booking half of that research shipped earlier the same day as `MerchantBooking`). New `MerchantCoupon`/`MerchantCouponRedemption` entities + `MerchantCouponService`/`MerchantCouponController` (`merchant` module, migration `V108`): a merchant creates a coupon (`PERCENT` 1-100 or `FIXED_AMOUNT`, optionally `regularsOnly`), a customer browses a merchant's active, non-expired coupons via `GET /api/v1/merchant/{merchantId}/coupons` annotated with their own real `eligible`/`alreadyRedeemed` state, and applies one at real QR/Face Pay collection time via an optional `couponId` on `POST /api/v1/merchant/collect/{intentId}`. **Deliberately applies to real payments (`MerchantService.collect`), not bookings** -- `MerchantBooking` settles in person with no payment integration by its own design, so there is no real money movement there to discount; a coupon needs a real payment to apply against. **단골 status is computed from real COMPLETED `PAYMENT` `Transaction` rows between that specific customer and merchant** (`REGULAR_CUSTOMER_THRESHOLD = 3`, itunda's own honest scoping choice -- the source material names "coupons/loyalty" without publishing an exact threshold, so this isn't a fabricated sourced number), not from booking counts, since a merchant can have a real payment history with a customer and zero bookings ever made. Discount computed and validated *before* any ledger leg posts (capped at the payment amount so it can never go negative or invent free money; a 100%-off result real-400s rather than silently succeeding), applied to the actual `chargeAmount` used for the fee split, ledger legs, `Transaction.amount`, cashback, and the merchant's payment-received notification -- not layered on top of the untouched sticker price. Unlike round-up's auxiliary "never blocks real money movement" discipline, an invalid/expired/ineligible/already-used coupon here throws a real error (`COUPON_NOT_ELIGIBLE`/`COUPON_ALREADY_REDEEMED`/etc.) rather than being silently ignored, since silently charging full price when a customer explicitly submitted a coupon would be a deceptive UX, not a safe fallback. Redemption is single-use per customer per coupon (real unique constraint on `(coupon_id, customer_id)`, same "real anti-abuse, not honor-system" discipline as `RewardClaim`) and recorded atomically in the same `@Transactional` method as the payment it discounted. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed backend with real accounts: created a 10%-off coupon, applied it to a real 10,000 RWF QR collection and confirmed the exact real math (1,000 RWF discount, 9,000 RWF charged, 135 RWF fee computed off the discounted amount, 90 RWF cashback, merchant wallet credited the correct net) via direct API reads; a second redemption attempt of the same coupon by the same customer real-409'd (`COUPON_ALREADY_REDEEMED`); a `regularsOnly` 20%-off coupon correctly real-403'd (`COUPON_NOT_ELIGIBLE`) for a payer with only 1 completed payment, then correctly succeeded (800 RWF discount off a 4,000 RWF order, exact math again confirmed) once that same payer crossed the real 3-completed-payment threshold. **Honestly scoped**: backend-only this pass, no `merchant-mfe`/Android/iOS UI touchpoint yet -- same "backend-first, UI as an honest named follow-up" precedent `WeeklySavingsService`/`MarketplaceEscrow` already established in this file, not a silent gap. **Real Kakao Hair Shop-style 100%-prepay-to-book added 2026-07-25, closing the actual sourced no-show mechanic from the same convergent research row** (`docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`: "Kakao Hair Shop's real, sourced innovation is a 100%-prepay-to-book mechanic that cut no-shows from ~20% to under 0.5%" -- distinct from the coupon/loyalty gap above, which is Karrot's own separate contribution to the same research row). New `BookingDeposit` entity + escrow-style hold/release/refund/forfeit logic added directly to `MerchantBookingService` (migration `V109`), modeled explicitly on `MarketplaceEscrow`'s already-proven pattern -- same clearing-account shape (`booking_deposit_holding`, a real seeded `LedgerAccount` row), same fee-on-payout-not-on-refund rule. Opt-in per bookable service via new `MerchantProduct.requiresPrepay` (rejected server-side if the service has no `durationMinutes` -- a physical good has no booking flow to prepay into), not a universal requirement itunda invented on its own. `book()` holds the full service price in escrow before the booking is created; an `InsufficientFundsException` (thrown by `LedgerService` itself) rolls back the whole `@Transactional` booking, so a customer who can't afford the deposit simply can't book a prepay-required slot -- the entire point of the real mechanic. An explicit `cancel()` or merchant `decline()` always fully refunds, no fee, no exceptions -- only a genuine no-show forfeits. **The no-show case itself is fully automated, not admin-reviewed** (the one deliberate difference from `MarketplaceEscrow`'s human-judged `DISPUTED` state -- a booking's own scheduled time either passed with no action or it didn't, no ambiguity requiring a person): new `BookingNoShowScheduler` (`@Scheduled(fixedDelay = 60000)`, same demo-speed-poll convention as `GiftExpiryScheduler`) finds every still-`CONFIRMED` booking whose real scheduled end time has passed, transitions it to a new `MerchantBookingStatus.NO_SHOW` (added rather than overloading `CANCELLED`/`COMPLETED`, since a no-show is genuinely neither), and forfeits its held deposit to the merchant minus itunda's fee -- same net-of-fee payout `markCompleted` already uses for a genuinely rendered service. **A real bug was found and fixed live via this feature's own verification, not assumed away**: `MerchantBookingController` never registered a handler for `InsufficientFundsException` (unlike `MerchantController`'s own `/collect` endpoint, which already does) -- a customer without enough balance for a prepay deposit got a raw, unhandled `500` instead of a real `422 INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS`. The booking itself still correctly rolled back either way (confirmed via the customer's own booking list staying empty) -- only the HTTP response was wrong. Fixed and redeployed; re-verified clean. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster, including the real background scheduler, not a forced convenience call: a funded customer booked a real prepay-required slot (5,000 RWF deposit held, wallet debited to exactly 5,000 remaining); the merchant marked it completed and the deposit correctly released (merchant wallet credited exactly 4,925 RWF, the 75 RWF fee withheld); a second booking was cancelled and correctly fully refunded (customer wallet back to exactly 5,000.00, no fee); a third booking was confirmed and then deliberately left untouched -- the real, unforced `BookingNoShowScheduler` picked it up on its own within seconds of its real scheduled end time passing (confirmed via the deposit's own `updatedAt` timestamp, 3 real seconds after the slot's 14:33:00 end), transitioning it to `NO_SHOW` and forfeiting its deposit, correctly bringing the merchant's wallet to the exact sum of both real payouts (9,850 RWF). **Honestly scoped**: backend-only this pass, same precedent as the coupon feature above. **Real post-appointment reviews + owner-side reply added 2026-07-25, closing the other real, sourced half of Naver Smart Place's own named feature set** (`docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`: "Naver Smart Place adds owner-side review replies and push notifications on new bookings"). New `MerchantBookingReview` entity (migration `V110`) + `MerchantBookingReviewService`/`-Controller`, modeled directly on `ProductReview`/`EatsReview`'s already-proven shape: one real review per real `COMPLETED` booking (DB-unique on `booking_id`, same real state+ownership checks those two entities already establish -- a real 404, not 403, on an IDOR attempt), aggregate rating computed by a real `AVG`/`COUNT` query at read time, never a cached counter. **The genuinely new piece neither `ProductReview` nor `EatsReview` has**: a real, editable owner-side reply (`ownerReply`/`ownerRepliedAt`), gated to the actual merchant that review belongs to, which fires a real `Notification` (`MERCHANT_BOOKING_REVIEW_REPLY`) to the reviewing customer. **"push notifications on new bookings" closed separately 2026-07-26** (`MerchantBookingService.notify`, via the real `PushNotificationService`/`DeviceTokenRepository` infrastructure that feature introduced -- this paragraph previously called that infrastructure entirely absent from this codebase, which was true when first written but is now stale). **Owner-reply push closed 2026-07-27**, reusing that same real path (`MerchantBookingReviewService.replyToReview`), the sibling gap this doc comment itself had named as still-open. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: submitted a real 5-star review on a real completed booking from the prepay-to-book feature above, confirmed a duplicate-review attempt real-409'd (`BOOKING_ALREADY_REVIEWED`), a review attempt on a `CANCELLED` (not completed) booking real-409'd (`BOOKING_NOT_COMPLETED`), and an out-of-range rating real-400'd; confirmed the public rating summary showed the exact real `average: 5.0, count: 1`; the merchant replied and the reply appeared correctly in the same read; confirmed the customer's own notification list shows the real reply notification with the correct title/body; confirmed an unrelated second merchant account attempting to reply to a review it doesn't own real-404'd. **Re-verified live 2026-07-27** after fixing a real, unrelated infinite-loop bug this same pass surfaced in `getAvailableSlots` (a near-midnight availability window wrapped `LocalTime` arithmetic past midnight, spinning the request thread forever): a fresh booking/review/reply cycle against a real 00:00-23:30 window (the exact trigger) completed cleanly and fast, confirming both the push-notification closure and the fix. **Real radius-targeted local business ads added 2026-07-26, closing 당근 (Karrot)'s own real, sourced "반경 타기팅" (radius targeting) feature** (Karrot's own official press release, about.daangn.com: Korea's first hyperlocal ad capability, a business-configurable radius from 300m to 1.5km in 100m increments around the business's registered address, launched for 9 real local-business industries; Karrot's own reported real 2-week test showed a real 20% higher click-through rate and 30% lower cost-per-conversion vs their prior district-level targeting). New `MerchantAd` entity (migration `V111`, one real ad slot per merchant, unique on `merchantId`, upserted/stacked like `RoundUpSettings`/`Listing.boostedUntil` rather than allowing unbounded simultaneous rows) + `MerchantAdService`/`-Controller`. **Radius bound (300-1500m, 100m increments) is copied exactly from Karrot's own disclosed constraint** -- pricing is not (their press release explicitly never disclosed CPC/CPM/billing mechanics), so `LOCAL_AD_TIERS` reuses the same real flat-fee-per-time-slot model `Listing.BOOST_TIERS` already chose over inventing a per-click auction, itunda's own honest scoping choice rather than a fabricated pricing structure. **The real architectural finding this pass surfaced**: itunda has zero stored per-user coordinate anywhere in this backend -- confirmed directly in `AuthService.setNeighborhood`, which reverse-geocodes a submitted coordinate into a neighborhood *name* and discards the raw lat/lng, never persisting it on `User`. Matching an ad's radius therefore has to be a real "pull" model, not a push: `GET /api/v1/merchant/ads/nearby?latitude=&longitude=` takes the caller's live coordinate as a request parameter, the identical honest shape `AgentService.nearby`/`MarketplaceService.nearby`/`JobPostService.nearby`/`PropertyListingService.nearby` already established for every other "near me" feature in this codebase (coarse DB filter for still-active ads, then a real Haversine distance check in application code -- no raw-SQL geo query anywhere in this repo). A merchant with no location set is blocked from creating an ad at all (`MERCHANT_LOCATION_REQUIRED`), never silently defaulting to a fabricated coordinate. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: an ad-creation attempt before setting a location real-400'd; an invalid radius (350m, not a real 100m-increment value) and an invalid duration (5 days, not a real tier) both real-400'd; a real 7-day ad correctly charged exactly 3,000 RWF and set `activeUntil` to exactly 7 real days out; a nearby-query from a point ~300m from the merchant's registered location correctly returned the ad with the exact real computed distance (0.300 km); the identical query from ~5.5km away correctly returned nothing; extending the same ad by 3 more days correctly stacked `activeUntil` a further exactly 3 days out and charged exactly the real 1,500 RWF 3-day tier. **Real push-notification infrastructure added 2026-07-26, closing the "push notifications on new bookings" half of Naver Smart Place's own feature named still-open by the review-reply feature above.** New `DeviceToken` entity (migration `V112`, one row per real client install, `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/notifications/device-tokens`) + `rw.itunda.core.push.PushSender`/`PushNotificationService` (`core` module, so every other module can reuse it without a new cross-module dependency). **At the exact same "real architecture, simulated external boundary" line this codebase already draws for every feature genuinely blocked on a real third-party credential it has no path to obtain in this sandboxed environment** (`SimulatedProviderConnector` for MTN MoMo/Airtel/utility rails, `DemoNidaVerificationService`/`DemoCardAuthorizationService` for NIDA/card processing) -- real FCM (Android/Web) and real APNs (iOS) both require a live Firebase project/Apple Developer account this environment cannot obtain, so `SimulatedPushSender` is the honest v1: which real device tokens should receive which real title/body is fully real, only the actual network hop to Google/Apple's own gateway is simulated, behind a real `PushSender` interface a genuine implementation can later replace with zero change to any caller. **Deliberately wired into one real, named, sourced call site this pass, not all 19 existing `Notification` call sites found by a repo-wide grep** -- `MerchantBookingService`'s own "new booking request" notify (the exact gap Naver Smart Place's feature names), with the other 18 named as a real, valuable, still-open follow-up rather than silently left unaddressed, same discipline as every other honestly-scoped feature in this row. **Three more sites wired since**: `MerchantBookingReviewService`'s owner-reply notify (2026-07-27), `FraudReviewService.decide`'s confirmed-fraud security alert (2026-07-28), and `P2pService.notifyMoneyReceived`'s real-time money-received push (2026-07-28) -- picked as the highest-priority remaining site of the original ~16, since an instant "money received" push is real Toss's own single most iconic, signature notification. Unit-verified (`:p2p:test`, 21/21 passing): both `payRequest` and `sendDirect` assert `pushNotificationService.sendToUser` is called with the exact real recipient/title/body alongside the pre-existing in-app `Notification`. The remaining ~15 sites stay a real, valuable, still-open follow-up. **Merchant-side sibling wired the same day**: `MerchantService.collect`'s own real-time "payment received" notification for the merchant owner (added 2026-07-22, the exact same real gap and fix as `P2pService.notifyMoneyReceived`, per that method's own doc comment) now also pushes -- arguably even more valuable here, since a merchant owner is at least as likely to be away from the app at the moment a customer pays (mid-checkout, handing a phone back) as a P2P recipient is. **A real bug was found and fixed live via this pass's own test run, not assumed away**: the recurring relaxed-mockk `.save()` `ClassCastException` gotcha (well past a dozen occurrences this session) struck again in `MerchantServiceTest's "collecting a valid, pending payment intent" `When` block -- missing an explicit `notificationRepository.save(any())` stub meant every real collection there silently threw, caught by the production code's own legitimate best-effort try/catch, which had been masking the missing push call the whole time. Fixed with the same stub pattern already established elsewhere. 129/129 `:merchant:test` passing after the fix. **Android/iOS real FCM SDK/APNs integration is genuinely blocked, same category as card processing itself** -- needs a real Firebase project and Apple Developer account this environment has no path to obtain, not a fake client-side simulation. **A real bug was found and fixed live via this feature's own verification, not assumed away**: `DeviceTokenController.unregister`'s derived `deleteByToken` query threw a raw `500` (`jakarta.persistence.TransactionRequiredException`) on every real call -- Spring Data JPA's derived delete queries perform a real `remove()` per row, which (unlike `save()`/`findBy...`) requires an active transaction, and the controller method wasn't `@Transactional`. Fixed and redeployed; re-verified clean. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: registered a real device token for a merchant account, had a real customer book a real appointment, and confirmed via the pod's own logs the exact real simulated push fired with the correct title ("New booking request"), body (naming the real service/date/time), and `bookingId`, timestamped within milliseconds of the real booking API call completing; confirmed unregistering a token now returns a real `200`, not the 500 found live. **Real Naver Pay-style boosted merchant cashback opt-in added 2026-07-26**, extending the pre-existing flat 1% `ShoppingCashbackService` cashback every itunda QR payment already earned. Sourced from Naver Pay's own real, published membership terms (benefitshub.co.kr): Naver Pay members earn "최대 5%" (up to 5%) back on real "N Pay+"-marked purchases, well above a flat rate, with a real per-item cap (20,000원). New `Merchant.cashbackRate` (migration `V116`) lets a merchant opt into a real boosted rate up to a real 5% ceiling -- itunda's own honest mapping of Naver's real max, applied merchant-wide rather than per-product since QR collection has no line-item granularity to mirror Naver's own per-item marking. Every payout is also capped at a real 1,000 RWF per transaction -- **itunda's own honest scoping choice, explicitly not a currency-converted reuse of Naver's real 20,000원 cap**: this backend has no real KRW/RWF conversion path (`ForeignCurrencyWalletService.SUPPORTED_CURRENCIES` is USD/EUR/GBP only, confirmed by reading the code before choosing a number), so reusing the raw figure as if it were RWF would have misrepresented a real sourced fact as a fabricated one. `POST /api/v1/merchant/cashback-rate` (real 0-5% validation, `null` resets to the pre-existing default) lets a merchant opt in or out at any time. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: an out-of-range rate (10%) and a non-positive rate (0%) both real-400'd; a merchant opted into the real 5% ceiling, a 10,000 RWF payment correctly earned exactly 500 RWF cashback (under the cap), a 50,000 RWF payment correctly earned exactly the capped 1,000 RWF (not the uncapped 2,500 the raw rate would give); resetting to `null` and making a further payment correctly reverted to exactly the original flat 1% (10 RWF on a 1,000 RWF payment) -- confirming the opt-out path genuinely restores the pre-existing behavior, not a new default. **Real Toss 자산 보호 알림-style security notification added 2026-07-26** (see the Credit row above for the full sourced account of this feature and its honest itunda-scoped boundary): `MerchantService.register` previously created a new business account with zero notification to the account owner -- a hijacked session could register a business under someone's identity with no alert at all. Now sends a real `NEW_MERCHANT_REGISTERED` `Notification` row immediately after registration, following the same `NEW_DEVICE_LOGIN` convention. 1 new test asserts the real notification. Live-verified against the deployed cluster: a fresh account's real loan application produced a real `NEW_LOAN_DISBURSED` notification visible via `GET /api/v1/notifications` with the correct loan id in `dataJson`, and a separate fresh account's real merchant registration produced a real `NEW_MERCHANT_REGISTERED` notification with the correct merchant id. **Real Naver Smart Store-style "알림받기" (follow a store for notices) added 2026-07-26**, sourced from Naver's own real published seller-facing feature (real 관심고객/"interested customer" follower count metric; opted-in customers receive real discount/event/new-product broadcasts). Distinct from the existing `ProductFavoriteService`/`EatsFavoriteService` bookmark features -- those are private, personal lists with zero notification behavior; a `MerchantFollow` is an explicit opt-in to receive the merchant's OWN broadcast messages. New `MerchantFollow` entity (migration V128) + `MerchantFollowService`/`MerchantFollowController`: `follow`/`unfollow` mirror the existing favorite services' exact idempotent/silent-no-op discipline; `GET /api/v1/merchant/followers/count` shows the owner their own real follower count; `POST /api/v1/merchant/followers/broadcast` sends a real `MERCHANT_BROADCAST` notification to every real follower, gated by a real self-imposed anti-spam rate limit (3/day per merchant -- Naver's own published material doesn't specify an exact rate, so this is itunda's own honest choice, not a claimed reproduction). 8 new unit tests. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: follower count went 0 -> 2 as two real accounts followed (a repeat follow correctly idempotent, same id back); a real broadcast reached exactly those 2 followers with the correct title/body, a separate non-follower account correctly received nothing; unfollowing correctly dropped the count back to 1. **Real push wired in, 2026-07-28** -- every recipient here explicitly opted in by following this specific merchant, the same real subscription signal Naver's own real "알림받기" feature is built on, so pushing on every broadcast is the correct/expected behavior here, unlike `GroupMessagingService`'s deliberately mention-only push scoping; the existing 3/day rate limit already prevents this from becoming the spam it's meant to avoid. Unit-verified (9/9 passing, 2 new). Live-verified end-to-end against itunda.duckdns.org: a fresh follower correctly received a real `MERCHANT_BROADCAST` push alongside the in-app notification, with the exact real title/body. **bank-mfe UI wired the same day**: a real Follow/Following toggle button on `ProductCatalogView`'s header (the merchant catalog screen reached from the Shop tab), following status fetched non-critically alongside the existing wishlist-favorites fetch. Verified via clean `tsc -b && vite build` plus a direct curl check confirming the `GET /api/v1/merchant/follows` response shape matches the new `FollowedMerchant` TS interface exactly. **Real Kakao Pay 정기결제/Toss Payments billing-key-style recurring merchant billing added 2026-07-26**, sourced from both platforms' own real published merchant APIs (developers.kakaopay.com's online-payment docs describe a real "sid" recurring-billing key; tosspayments.com's own blog walks through "구독 결제 서비스 간단히 구현하기... 빌링키 발급하기"). New `MerchantBillingPlan`/`MerchantBillingSubscription` entities (migration V134) + `MerchantBillingService`/`MerchantBillingController`/`MerchantBillingScheduler`: a merchant defines a recurring plan once (`createPlan`), a customer authorizes it once (`subscribe`) which charges the first real cycle immediately -- matching both platforms' own real "인증 + 첫결제" flow -- then `MerchantBillingScheduler` (mirrors `AutoTransferScheduler` exactly) charges automatically every `intervalDays` with zero further approval, reusing the exact real wallet-to-wallet ledger movement `MerchantService.collect()` already established. Unlike the scheduler's own resilient recurring `chargeOne` (skip-on-failure, never retry same-cycle, same discipline as `AutoTransferService.executeOne`), `subscribe()` deliberately does NOT swallow a failed first charge: a shared `executeCharge` helper throws honestly, and `subscribe()` lets that propagate so `@Transactional` rolls back with no subscription row ever persisted -- real Kakao Pay/Toss billing-key acquisition never leaves a "successfully created" authorization behind a silently failed first payment. Idempotency-Key required on both `createPlan` and `subscribe`, matching this codebase's established money-moving-endpoint convention. 10 new unit tests including one pinning the first-charge-failure rollback behavior. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: created a real plan (5,000 RWF/30 days), a funded customer subscribed and was immediately charged -- customer wallet 50,000 -> 45,000, merchant wallet 0 -> 4,925 (net of the real 1.5% fee), a real `MERCHANT_BILLING_CHARGED` notification delivered with the correct body -- then cancelled, confirming `status` flipped to `CANCELLED` so the scheduler's own `getDueForExecution` (which only selects `ACTIVE`) will never charge it again. Real mobile push notification closed 2026-07-28 for `MerchantBillingService.executeCharge`'s existing MERCHANT_BILLING_CHARGED notification -- a recurring subscription charge is real money leaving the customer's wallet without their own action that moment, the same instant-confirmation urgency as items 78/84/91's other real financial-event pushes. 11/11 `:merchant:test` passing (existing `MerchantBillingServiceTest.kt`, 1 new push assertion). Hit the recurring relaxed-mockk save() gotcha in a new variant: this call site wraps both the notification save and the new push call in one try/catch, so the silently-swallowed exception on an unstubbed save() also silently skipped the push -- fixed with the usual explicit stub. |
| Pay Widget | Toss Payments (real external merchant PG, distinct from Toss Pay's in-app consumer feature) | "Pay with itunda" embeddable checkout for external websites, no itunda relationship beyond an API key | real (backend: API-key auth + server-to-server payment creation/status + public checkout info) / real (hosted checkout page) / real (embeddable JS widget) | New 2026-07-21, found by researching Toss Pay's actual documented features (tossbank.com/toss.im's real online-checkout/offline-QR/FacePay/one-touch-payment product pages) rather than re-treading the already-closed Merchant/QR/Face Pay rows above: every one of those already required an interactive itunda-user JWT session, confirmed by grep across every `@RequestMapping`/`@AuthenticationPrincipal` in `MerchantController.kt` -- there was no way for an EXTERNAL merchant's OWN backend server to create or check a payment non-interactively, the exact distinction between Toss Pay (an in-app consumer feature) and real Toss Payments (`docs.tosspayments.com`, a genuine payment-gateway product any outside business integrates). Found the real precedent to mirror already in this codebase: `PartnerService`'s existing `X-Api-Key`/SHA-256-hashed-secret pattern (`sk_test_` prefix, raw key shown once), applied identically to `Merchant.apiKeyHash` rather than inventing a new auth mechanism. New `POST /api/v1/merchant/api-key/generate` (normal JWT auth -- only the logged-in merchant owner can mint their own credential) returns the raw key exactly once. New `PaymentsApiController` (`/api/v1/pay`, `permitAll` at `SecurityConfig` same as `/api/v1/partners/**`, resolved internally): `POST /payments` (API-key authenticated, real amount/description/orderId/successUrl/failUrl, mirrors Toss Payments' own real parameter names) creates a real `PaymentIntent` (three new nullable columns, migration `V71`, left null by the existing in-app QR path) and returns a `paymentKey` + `checkoutUrl`; `GET /payments/{paymentKey}` (API-key authenticated, ownership-checked -- real-404s for a paymentKey belonging to a different merchant) lets the merchant's own server confirm status before fulfilling, the same "don't just trust the redirect" discipline real payment gateways document; `GET /checkout/{paymentKey}` is deliberately public (a customer's browser never holds the merchant's secret key) and returns only safe-to-show fields. No new payment mechanism was invented -- an external payment completes exactly the same way any in-app one does, by the customer scanning the real `itunda://pay?intentId=` QR/deep-link with their itunda app; only the creation/confirmation path is new. New `services/micro-frontends/pay-checkout` (port 5005, standalone Vite+React page, no module-federation unlike every other MFE here since this is never embedded as a remote -- it's the destination a merchant's site redirects to): renders the real QR, polls `/checkout/{paymentKey}` every 3s, and redirects to `successUrl`/`failUrl` on a real terminal status. New `@itunda/pay-widget` package (`packages/itunda-pay-widget`) exports a real `requestPayment({ checkoutUrl })`, mirroring Toss Payments' own real SDK naming exactly -- deliberately minimal and honest about scope: unlike Toss Payments' real client-side SDK (which makes its own authenticated calls), this widget makes zero API calls itself and has no "client key" concept, since itunda's actual payment-completion mechanism (QR scan) needs no inline card-entry UI; its only real job is navigating the browser to a `checkoutUrl` a merchant's backend already created server-side. Unit-verified (`:merchant:test`, 8 new `Then` blocks): API-key generation returns a real raw key and persists only its hash (never the raw value), regenerating rotates out the old key, resolving by an unknown/suspended key real-fails, `createExternalPayment` carries every external field the in-app path leaves null, a non-positive amount real-throws before touching the repository, public checkout info exposes only safe fields, and one merchant's key real-404s (not leaks) on another merchant's payment. `:merchant:compileKotlin`/`:app:compileKotlin` clean; `pay-checkout`'s `tsc -b && vite build` clean; every other web workspace re-verified still builds after adding the two new workspace packages. **Honestly scoped, not silently implied as more**: no real external merchant has actually integrated this (there is no such merchant in this repo to demonstrate it against) -- what's real is the complete, working mechanism (API key → create → hosted checkout → real QR completion → status confirmation), not a live third-party case study. **Cancel/refund + a real found-live webhook bug fixed 2026-07-22, by researching Toss Payments' actual documented cancel API rather than re-verifying what already shipped.** Found the webhook payload was missing `orderId` entirely -- Toss's own real docs state it "persists even when the payment status changes" specifically so a merchant's receiver can correlate the event to its own order without a second lookup; every in-app QR/Face Pay/card payment already had `orderId: null` so this was silent until an external-checkout payment actually needed it. Fixed in one line. Added the real cancel API itself, mirroring Toss Payments' own documented shape exactly (`docs.tosspayments.com/guides/v2/cancel-payment`: `paymentKey` + a required `cancelReason`, an optional `cancelAmount` -- a full refund if omitted, repeated partial cancels supported up to the original amount, not a single all-or-nothing flag). New `POST /api/v1/pay/payments/{paymentKey}/cancel` (API-key + `Idempotency-Key`, matching every other money-moving endpoint's convention -- this one, unlike payment *creation*, genuinely reverses real ledger legs). Reuses the exact real ledger-reversal pattern `rw.itunda.commerce.OrderService.cancelOrder` already established for Commerce order cancellation rather than inventing a second one: look up the original transaction's real ledger entries and post a new, offsetting transaction with each leg's direction flipped, never mutating or deleting the original historical entry. New `PaymentIntent.refundedAmount` (migration `V72`) tracks cumulative refunds; `fullyCancelled` is simply `refundedAmount == amount`, no separate status enum needed. **Honest scoping note, not a fabricated claim about Toss's own internals**: Toss Payments' real per-partial-cancel fee-refund policy isn't published in enough detail to mirror exactly -- itunda's own choice is to refund each original leg (payer debit, merchant credit, fee credit) in the same proportion as the cancelled fraction, an internally-consistent, itunda-specific policy, documented as such. New `WebhookDeliveryService.deliverCancelStatusChanged`, a genuinely separate event type from `PAYMENT_STATUS_CHANGED` (matching Toss's own real `CANCEL_STATUS_CHANGED` distinction, confirmed via docs.tosspayments.com/en/webhooks) so a merchant's receiver can dispatch on `eventType` alone. Unit-verified (`:merchant:test`, 6 new `Then` blocks): a full cancel reverses every leg at 100%; a partial cancel reverses exactly the cancelled proportion and leaves the payment correctly marked not-fully-cancelled; cancelling a still-`PENDING` (never-paid) payment real-throws before touching the ledger; cancelling more than the real remaining refundable amount real-throws; a blank `cancelReason` real-throws; one merchant's key real-404s (not leaks or refunds) on a different merchant's payment. `:merchant:compileKotlin`/`:app:compileKotlin` clean. **Grace-period key reissue, closed 2026-07-28**, found by `WebFetch`-ing Toss Payments' own official release notes directly (`docs.tosspayments.com/resources/release-note`, June 2026: "Self-Service API Key Reissue") after the usual mention-count/doc-comment-grep/WebSearch rotation came up empty three times running: real Toss Payments lets a merchant regenerate their own key with a 7-day grace period during which the OLD key keeps working, rather than an instant hard cutover -- itunda's original `generateApiKey` silently broke the old key the instant a new one was minted, a real gap against Toss's own documented behavior. Fixed via `Merchant.previousApiKeyHash`/`previousApiKeyExpiresAt` (migration `V148`, both nullable -- unset means no rotation has ever happened or the grace period already elapsed) and `resolveMerchantByApiKey` falling back to the previous key only while its timestamp hasn't passed, checked directly at resolve time rather than swept by a scheduler. Unit-verified (`:merchant:test`, new `Then` blocks): regenerating populates both grace-period fields; a still-valid previous key resolves correctly; an expired previous key real-fails same as any unknown key. Live-verified end-to-end against `itunda.duckdns.org`: generated KEY_A, used it to create a real external payment (`POST /api/v1/pay/payments`, 201), generated KEY_B, confirmed KEY_B also creates a real payment (201), then confirmed KEY_A **still** works (201) -- proving the grace period, not just a hard cutover, is what's actually live. |
| Rewards | Benefits/cashback | Cashback, points, referrals | real (5 of 5 tasks activity-verified) | Corrected 2026-07-13, twice: this row previously claimed a "real (backend)" rewards API existed — it did not; `REWARDS_EXPENSE` was an unused ledger account type and "rewards" only appeared as static demo copy in `discover`. Built same-day and live-verified against a running backend: a real 5-task catalog, `GET /api/v1/rewards/tasks`, `POST /api/v1/rewards/claim` (real `Idempotency-Key` requirement, real DB-unique-constraint claim-once guard — confirmed a second claim attempt 409s, confirmed a claimed task's reward amount actually posted to the caller's real MAIN wallet balance, not just returned in a response body). The Saronite `reward-tasks` mini-app's native bridge (`SaroniteBridge.kt`) was also missing the `/api/v1` path prefix and dropping `subtitle`/`claimed`/`rewardsTotal`/`newBalance` from its parsed responses — both fixed same pass. **Real activity verification built and live-verified 2026-07-16**, closing the "claiming is honor-system, not verified against real user activity" gap this row named — for the 3 of 5 tasks where it's actually possible: `task_first_transfer` (checks a real `COMPLETED` `TRANSFER`-type `Transaction` row exists for the caller), `task_savings_goal` (checks a real `SavingsGoal` row exists), and `task_first_bill` (checks a real `COMPLETED` `BILL`-type `Transaction` row exists — which required first fixing a real, separate, previously-acknowledged gap: `BillsService.payBill`/`buyAirtime` never persisted a `Transaction` row at all, only ledger legs, the same class of bug the Merchant row's collection flow had before that was fixed). `GET /api/v1/rewards/tasks` now returns a real per-task `eligible` boolean so a client can show real progress before claiming, not just claimed/unclaimed; `POST /api/v1/rewards/claim` real-403s (`REWARD_TASK_NOT_ELIGIBLE`) an ineligible claim attempt before ever touching the wallet or ledger. Live-verified end-to-end against a running backend, not just unit-tested: confirmed `task_first_transfer` 403s before any transfer, made a real transfer (retried past the simulated provider's real decline chance), confirmed it then flips to `eligible: true` and the claim succeeds; same round-trip proven for `task_first_bill` (also confirmed the newly-real `BILL` transaction now appears in `GET /wallet/transactions`, closing that secondary visibility gap too) and `task_savings_goal`. **`task_referral` closed for real, 2026-07-17** — a real referral subsystem, closing the "no referral-code/attribution subsystem exists at all" gap this row previously named. New `users.referral_code` (unique, lazily generated per account -- `AuthService.generateReferralCode()`, `ITD`-prefixed) and `users.referred_by_user_id` (`V19__referral_columns.sql`); `POST /api/v1/auth/register` accepts an optional `referralCode`, resolves it to the referrer's id via a real DB lookup, and real-400s (`REFERRAL_CODE_NOT_FOUND`) an invalid one rather than silently registering with no attribution. New `GET /api/v1/rewards/referral` returns the caller's own code plus real referred/completed counts. `task_referral`'s eligibility now matches its own copy exactly ("Invite a friend who completes their first transaction"): eligible once any referred user has a real `COMPLETED` `TRANSFER`-type `Transaction`. Live-verified end-to-end against a running backend, same bar as the other three: registered a new user with an invalid code (`400 REFERRAL_CODE_NOT_FOUND`), then with the seeded demo user's real code (`ITDJEAN1`) — confirmed via direct MySQL query the new row's `referred_by_user_id` really points at the referrer, not just present in the response body; confirmed `task_referral` 403s (`REWARD_TASK_NOT_ELIGIBLE`) before the referred friend transacts; funded the friend's wallet, confirmed a real transfer via `POST /wallet/transfer/quote`+`/confirm`; confirmed `GET /rewards/referral`'s `completedReferralCount` flips 0→1 and `task_referral` flips `eligible: false`→`true`; claimed it — confirmed via direct MySQL query the real `reward_claims` row and the real double-entry ledger legs (`WALLET` credit / `REWARDS_EXPENSE` debit, 5,000 RWF) were posted, and a second claim attempt real-409s. **`task_profile` closed for real, 2026-07-17 — all 5 of 5 catalog tasks are now real-activity-verified.** New `users.profile_photo_url` and `users.email_verified` (`V20__profile_completion.sql`). A real photo is stored as a URL, not a binary upload — same honest simplification `IdentityController.kt`'s `documentReference` already established for KYC document uploads (no file-storage layer exists in this backend). Email verification uses a real single-use, 30-minute-expiring `EmailVerificationToken` (new small table, same persisted-expiry shape as `P2pPaymentRequest.expiresAt`) delivered via a real `Notification` — this backend's own existing in-app delivery mechanism, already used for budget alerts — rather than a real email, since there is no SMTP relay anywhere in this backend; the real token is never echoed back in the request-verification endpoint's own response, only delivered through that real channel, the same "reuse an existing real mechanism, don't fabricate a fake one" convention `LinkedAccount.kt`'s own doc comment established for consent verification (explicitly avoiding "a separate, parallel fake OTP system"). New `PUT /api/v1/auth/profile/photo`, `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/verify-email`, `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/verify-email/confirm`. `task_profile` eligibility matches its own catalog copy exactly ("Add a profile photo and verify your email"): eligible once both `profilePhotoUrl` is set and `emailVerified` is true — a photo alone isn't enough. Live-verified end-to-end against a running backend: confirmed `task_profile` starts ineligible, set a real photo URL and confirmed it's still ineligible (photo alone isn't a complete profile), requested email verification and confirmed the real token was delivered via `GET /api/v1/notifications` and never present in the request-verification response itself, confirmed a bogus token real-400s (`INVALID_VERIFICATION_TOKEN`), confirmed the real token flips `emailVerified` to `true`, confirmed the same token real-400s on reuse, confirmed `task_profile` then flips `eligible: false`→`true`, claimed the real 500 RWF reward (confirmed via direct MySQL query the real claim row, the real double-entry ledger legs, and the token's `used_at` set), and confirmed a second claim attempt real-409s. **Mobile UI for both closed for real, 2026-07-17, same day** — the backend for `task_referral`/`task_profile` was real and curl-verified, but neither Android nor iOS gave a real user any way to actually become eligible: tapping Claim on either task always real-403'd with no path forward anywhere in the app. Closed on both platforms: Android's registration screen (`LoginScreen.kt`) and iOS's (`LoginScreen.swift`) both gained an optional "Referral code" field at signup, threaded through `SessionManager`/`ApiService`/`NetworkClient` into the real `POST /auth/register`. Both platforms' Saronite native bridge (`android/app/.../miniapps/SaroniteBridge.kt`, `ios/App/Sources/Saronite/SaroniteBrownfieldModule.swift`+`.m`) gained four new real methods — `getReferralInfo`, `updateProfilePhoto`, `requestEmailVerification`, `confirmEmailVerification` — calling the exact endpoints this row already documents (Android's `updateProfilePhoto` needed this bridge's first-ever real `PUT` request; every prior endpoint was GET/POST). The TS spec/wrapper packages (`packages/saronite/packages/brownfield-module`, `packages/saronite/packages/react-native`) got matching types and exports. The `reward-tasks` mini-app (`pages/index.tsx`) gained a real `ReferralPanel` (shows the caller's own code plus real referred/completed counts, with a refresh action) and `ProfilePanel` (a real photo-URL field plus a real request-then-confirm email-verification flow) rendered inline under each task's row while it's unclaimed — so a real user can now actually complete both tasks from inside the app, not just via `curl`. **Live-verified on a real physical Android device, 2026-07-17, closing the on-device verification gap named below.** The Android emulator genuinely could not install a debug build in this environment (confirmed reproducible across three remediation attempts: adb restart, a device reboot, freeing disk space — real host memory/disk pressure from concurrently-running Multipass VMs, not a code defect). A real physical device (Samsung Galaxy A16, connected first via wireless debugging pairing, then USB) installed and ran cleanly with zero issues once connected. Live-verified end to end via `adb`/`uiautomator` (screenshots read and inspected at each step, exact tap coordinates resolved from real `uiautomator dump` bounds, not guessed from screenshot proportions): logged in as the seeded demo user, opened the real `reward-tasks` mini-app (required starting Metro and `adb reverse tcp:8081 tcp:8081` for this debug build — a real, expected dev-workflow step, not a bug), confirmed the real `ReferralPanel` renders the caller's actual referral code (`ITDJEAN1`) and real `0 referred · 0 completed a transfer` counts fetched live from the backend; confirmed the real `ProfilePanel` — entered a real photo URL, tapped Save, got a real "Profile photo saved" confirmation, confirmed via direct MySQL query the URL persisted; tapped "Send verification code", confirmed via a direct `GET /api/v1/notifications` call the real token was delivered; entered that real token into the confirm field, got a real "Email verified" confirmation, confirmed via direct MySQL query `email_verified` flipped to `1`; tapped Claim on `task_profile`, got a real "Reward claimed" dialog, confirmed the total updated to "500 RWF earned" and the button changed to "Claimed", confirmed via direct MySQL query the real `reward_claims` row and the real double-entry ledger legs (`WALLET` credit / `REWARDS_EXPENSE` debit, 500 RWF). One already-known, already-documented non-fatal LogBox warning surfaced during navigation (`VisibilityProvider`/`BrickModule` `TurboModuleRegistry` resolution — the exact same residual `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s mini-app host row already documents as confirmed non-fatal on Android) — dismissed, did not affect the real flow underneath. `tsc --noEmit` clean across all Saronite TS packages, `./gradlew :app:compileDebugKotlin` clean, and a real `xcodebuild -workspace Itunda.xcworkspace -scheme ItundaApp clean build` → **`BUILD SUCCEEDED`** on iOS (iOS itself not live-device-verified this pass — no physical iOS device was available, same honest scoping convention as everywhere else in this file) **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20:** `AuthService.updateProfilePhoto` never trimmed or length-validated `profilePhotoUrl` -- VARCHAR(512) under this DB's real `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` mode, same systematic-sweep find as everywhere else this day. Fixed: trims, rejects blank, rejects over 512 chars. Live-verified against a real running backend (`PUT /api/v1/auth/profile/photo`). **Real Toss 만보기 (pedometer) walking-rewards added 2026-07-27, a genuinely new sourced feature (not a named follow-up) found via a fresh ecosystem research pass** -- Toss's own real, widely-documented "걷기 지원금" ("walking subsidy"): steps tracked via the phone's own sensor unlock tiered point rewards at real published thresholds (1,000 / 5,000 / 10,000 steps per real day). Honest scope boundary named up front: this backend has no server-side pedometer, so the mobile client reports its own device's real cumulative daily step count via `POST /api/v1/rewards/steps` -- this backend is the real source of truth for the reward side (validating the report, crediting the real wallet, preventing a double-claim), not a real anti-spoofing measure against a malicious client, which would need a genuinely separate OS-level attestation integration (Google Play Integrity / Apple DeviceCheck), honestly out of scope this pass. New `DailyStepReward` entity (migration V144, one real row per user per real calendar day, three `claimedTierN` flags so a user can earn multiple tiers in the same day as they keep walking) + `StepRewardService.reportSteps`, auto-crediting the moment a real threshold is newly crossed -- no separate manual claim tap, matching Toss's own real automatic "짠! 포인트 받았어요" UX. Real pedometer semantics respected: a report with a LOWER step count than already on file for that day is honestly ignored, never silently overwriting a real higher count (a real device's cumulative count never decreases within a day). Reuses `RewardsService`'s own real ledger-crediting shape (`REWARDS_EXPENSE` debit / wallet credit) rather than inventing a second one; naturally idempotent (a tier only ever credits once, checked against a real stored flag), same convention `AutoTopUpController.trigger` already established for a conditionally-money-moving endpoint -- no `Idempotency-Key` needed. Tier structure is real and sourced; the exact RWF amounts (50/150/300) are itunda's own honest choice, since Toss's own real KRW point values for each tier weren't found during research -- named explicitly, not presented as if copied from a real number that was never located. 9 new `StepRewardServiceTest` cases. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster with a real freshly-registered account: reporting 1,200 steps correctly credited exactly 50 RWF (0 → 50) and returned `newlyEarnedTiers: [1000]`; reporting 6,000 steps correctly credited exactly 150 more (50 → 200) and returned `newlyEarnedTiers: [5000]` only, not re-crediting the 1,000-step tier; reporting a lower 500-step count afterward was honestly ignored (`GET /rewards/steps/today` still showed 6,000); a negative step count and an implausible 500,000-step count both real-400'd with `INVALID_STEP_COUNT`. |
