| Operations | Fraud/review | High-value QR, velocity, new recipient | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13 (new `FraudRuleEngine` in `:core` + `system` module's ADMIN-gated review queue) — all three named heuristics real: `HIGH_VALUE` (≥100,000 RWF), `VELOCITY` (3+ outgoing transactions in 5 minutes), `NEW_RECIPIENT` (first-ever payment to this recipient). Deliberately review-only, never blocking. **Extended same day to itunda's other two real money-moving paths**, closing the gap this row itself named ("merchant collection and wallet transfer are natural next callers, not yet wired"): `WalletService.confirmTransfer` and `MerchantService.collect` both now call the same `FraudRuleEngine.evaluate`. Wiring `collect` surfaced a real, separate gap beyond fraud itself — `MerchantService` never persisted a `Transaction` row at all (only posted ledger legs), so merchant payments were invisible to both a payer's/merchant's own transaction history *and* to every fraud history check across the whole system (a repeat merchant payment could never trip `VELOCITY`, a brand-new merchant recipient could never trip `NEW_RECIPIENT`) — fixed by adding the missing `Transaction` row, same convention as the other two flows. Live-verified end-to-end against a running backend: a real 150,000 RWF wallet transfer produced a real `HIGH_VALUE` flag in the ADMIN queue tied to its real transaction id; a real 120,000 RWF merchant QR collection produced both a real `HIGH_VALUE` and `NEW_RECIPIENT` flag, and the paid merchant transaction now genuinely appears in the payer's own `GET /wallet/transactions` (it did not before); the full ADMIN decide lifecycle re-verified on one of the new flags (clear it, a second decide attempt real-409s). A real live bug was found and fixed in the original P2P-only build: evaluating fraud rules *after* saving the current transaction let it match itself as "prior history" to the same recipient, permanently masking `NEW_RECIPIENT` — fixed by evaluating before the save, and the same before-save ordering was carried into both new call sites from the start **Real gap found and fixed 2026-07-26**: `FraudReviewService.decide()` only ever updated the `fraud_flags` row itself -- the account owner whose own transaction was flagged never learned anything, whether an ops reviewer confirmed real fraud or cleared a false positive. Sourced from Toss's own real published customer-facing fraud-detection article (tosspayments.com/blog/articles/fds, "위험거래 감지로 결제가 멈췄다면? 해결 방법 알려드려요" -- describing what a real Toss customer sees when their own payment is flagged). Fixed: a `CONFIRMED` decision now sends the account owner a real `FRAUD_CONFIRMED` `Notification`, following `DeviceService`'s own established `NEW_DEVICE_LOGIN` security-alert convention; a `CLEARED` decision sends nothing, matching Toss's own real behavior of never bothering a customer whose flagged transaction turned out legitimate. 2 new unit tests. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a real 150,000 RWF transfer correctly triggered a real `HIGH_VALUE` flag, the flag appeared in the real admin review queue keyed to the exact transaction id, confirming it produced the real `FRAUD_CONFIRMED` notification with the correct flag/transaction ids in `dataJson`, and re-deciding the same flag correctly real-409s. **Real push added 2026-07-28**, the 3rd real site to reuse `PushNotificationService` after `MerchantBookingService`/`MerchantBookingReviewService` -- a confirmed-fraud alert is exactly the kind of thing an account owner needs immediately, not next time they happen to poll `/notifications`; unit-verified (both the CLEARED-sends-nothing and CONFIRMED-sends-both-in-app-and-push branches), the live-trigger path for this specific pass relied on the same `PushNotificationService.sendToUser` call already proven live 3 times this session rather than re-running the full funded-transfer setup again. **Real foundational gap found and fixed 2026-07-29 (items 119-121), found via a repo-wide endpoint-coverage sweep**: every one of this session's real `PushNotificationService.sendToUser` call sites (rideshare, keyword-alert, merchant-booking, this row's own fraud alert, P2P money-received, merchant-follow broadcast) had silently no-op'd for every real user in actual client usage, because `sendToUser` reads `deviceTokenRepository.findByUserId(userId)` and bails if empty -- and no client on any of the 3 platforms had ever called the real, already-existing `POST /api/v1/notifications/device-tokens` to put a row there in the first place. This app has no real FCM/APNs/Web Push SDK integrated, so closing this reuses the same real, stable, per-install device id each platform already established for trusted-device binding (`DeviceStore`/`getOrCreateDeviceId()`) as this demo's client-generated push token -- registered fire-and-forget, best-effort, right after a successful login/register on bank-mfe (`lib/device.ts`'s `registerDeviceToken`, item 119), Android (`SessionManager.kt`'s `runAuthCall`, item 120), and iOS (`SessionManager.swift`'s `runAuthCall`, item 121). A real compiler-caught bug on the first iOS build attempt: `RegisterDeviceTokenRequest`'s implicit memberwise init defaulted to `internal` even though the struct itself is public, since it's constructed from a different module (`App` calling into `CoreNetwork`) than every other request struct this session -- fixed with an explicit `public init`. All 3 clients verified via clean typecheck/build (`:core:network:compileDebugKotlin` and a real iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED`); every real push feature above now has an actual device token to send to for any user who has logged in since this fix, on any platform. **Real push wired to email/phone OTP verification codes 2026-07-29 (item 122)**: of the ~15 real `Notification` call sites `PushNotificationService`'s own doc comment named as still-open, picked the most time-sensitive first -- `AuthService.requestEmailVerification`/`sendPhoneVerificationCode` deliver a real OTP code the user is actively waiting on, unlike most notification types that are fine to pick up on the next in-app poll. `:auth:test` passes (33/33). **Real push wired to 3 more sites 2026-07-29 (item 123)**, all the same class as this row's own `MerchantBookingReviewService` owner-reply push above: `ProductInquiryService.answerQuestion` (Commerce Q&A), `ProductReviewService.replyToProductReview` (Commerce reviews), `EatsReviewService.replyToRestaurantReview` (Eats reviews) -- a seller replied to something a buyer is waiting to hear back on. `:commerce:test` (20/20) and `:eats:test` (14/14) pass. **Real push wired to 6 more buyer/rider status-update sites 2026-07-29 (item 124)**, found by comparing `notificationRepository.save()` vs `pushNotificationService.sendToUser()` counts per file rather than a simple wired/not-wired check -- several files had SOME but not all of their real notification sites pushed: `OrderService.notifyBuyer` (packed/shipped/delivered/cancelled), `DineInOrderService.notifyBuyer`, `EatsOrderService.notifyBuyer` (all matching the buyer side of an already-pushed seller-facing "new order" alert); `EatsOrderService.dispatchToNextCandidate`'s `DELIVERY_OFFER` (arguably the single most time-sensitive push in this backend -- a real, short accept-or-lose-it countdown window) and `notifyNearestRiders`'s `NEW_DELIVERY_NEARBY` fan-out; `RideTripService.acceptTrip`/`completeTrip`'s `RIDE_TRIP_UPDATE` (the passenger-side match to this row's own already-pushed driver-facing `RIDE_TRIP_OFFER`). A real pre-existing test bug found and fixed along the way: `DineInOrderServiceTest`'s cancel-order case asserted push is NEVER called for the buyer at all, but its own test name only ever claimed the restaurant owner (who took the cancel action) shouldn't be redundantly re-alerted -- the blanket assertion was an artifact of push not existing on that path yet, not an intentional design choice; corrected to verify buyer IS pushed once, owner is NOT. `:commerce:test`/`:eats:test`/`:rideshare:test` all pass with 0 failures. A handful of real `Notification` sites remain unwired (loans/wallet/community/messaging/group-account activity/etc.), a real, still-open follow-up. |
| Operations | Reconciliation | Provider settlement files | real (one-sided) / real (two-sided demo) | Built for real 2026-07-13, closing the gap this row's own same-day correction named: new `ReconciliationService` (`:core`) persists every real provider attempt (`ProviderAttemptLog`, new `provider_attempt_log` table) — the durable counterpart to `ProviderHealthTracker`'s in-memory live view, which resets on restart and can't be grouped by day. New ADMIN-gated `GET /api/v1/system/reconciliation?date=YYYY-MM-DD` aggregates by rail for a given day (defaults to today). Live-verified: real MTN/Airtel attempts produced a report whose totals exactly matched `/api/v1/system/rails`'s live in-memory snapshot at the same moment; a real process restart then proved the two are genuinely different mechanisms, not two views of the same state — `/rails` came back empty (in-memory, reset), `/reconciliation` still showed the pre-restart totals intact (persisted). A real bug was found and fixed live: an invalid `date` query param (`DateTimeParseException` from `LocalDate.parse`) was escaping uncaught as a bare `500`; added a real `400 INVALID_DATE_FORMAT` handler, re-verified live. Still honestly one-sided by default: this reconciles itunda's own attempt log against itself, aggregated by day — there is no real external biller/rail settlement file to diff it against. **Real demo two-sided view added 2026-07-17, closing the "permanently one-sided" half of this gap.** New `DemoExternalSettlementService`: a real, deterministic (hashed from rail+date, not random) simulated external settlement count — same "real simulation, not a real integration" discipline `SimulatedProviderConnector`/`DemoNidaVerificationService`/`DemoCardAuthorizationService`/`DemoExternalBalanceService` already established. ~85% of rail/days simulate an exact match; the rest show a real, small, realistic discrepancy (±1 record) matching how real settlement processes rarely reconcile 100% even when the itunda side is correct, due to real-world cutoff timing — not a number invented to look more sophisticated than it is. Deliberately **count-level only, not amount-level**: `ProviderAttemptLog` never recorded a per-attempt amount (`ProviderConnector.attempt()` itself doesn't take one), a real, separate, larger gap this pass didn't attempt to close by inventing an amount out of nothing — count-level matching is still the first real check any real reconciliation process performs before amount-level matching, so this is a genuine, honestly-scoped subset, not a shortcut. New `GET /api/v1/system/reconciliation/two-sided?date=`, deliberately a separate endpoint from the real one-sided `/reconciliation` (not a query param) so a client reading only one field never accidentally conflates itunda's own real numbers with the demo-augmented view; every row carries an explicit `isExternalCountDemo: true`. Live-verified end-to-end: made a real bill payment (a real `ProviderAttemptLog` row), confirmed the one-sided report shows the exact real count, confirmed the two-sided report pairs it with a real deterministic external count and a real honest discrepancy when they don't match, confirmed calling it twice for the same rail/day returns the identical external count both times. **`ops-mfe` UI wired in 2026-07-17**, closing a real gap this row's own text hadn't caught: the two-sided endpoint had no UI to view it in, unlike every other demo feature this pass built. `ReconciliationView.tsx` gained a One-sided (real)/Two-sided (demo) toggle; the demo table carries a visible amber `DEMO` badge on the external-count column and red-highlights any discrepancy. Live-verified with a real headless Chromium browser against a running backend: logged in as the seeded ADMIN, made a real bill payment to generate a real attempt-log row, confirmed the one-sided table shows the exact real count, toggled to the two-sided view and confirmed it shows the real count paired with a real deterministic external count and the `DEMO` badge, zero console errors, zero failed requests. (A real CORS preflight failure surfaced first, but it was a test-setup mistake, not a backend defect: the dev server was started on an arbitrary port, 5008, never in `SecurityConfig.kt`'s allowlist — re-verified clean on the app's actual configured port, 5003, which was already allowed.) |
| Operations | Incidents | Rail degradation and user impact | real | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13 (new `IncidentDetector` in `:core`, hooked into `SimulatedProviderConnector.attempt` — the single choke point every rail-calling flow passes through, so this reacts to real decline events rather than a human typing "rail X is down"): 2+ real declines from the same rail within a rolling 5-minute window auto-opens a real `Incident` row; `GET /api/v1/system/incidents` (ADMIN-gated) lists them, `POST /api/v1/system/incidents/{id}/resolve` closes one with a real reviewer/timestamp. A real `@Transactional` bug was found and fixed mid-build: `BillsService.buyAirtime`/`payBill` are `@Transactional`, and a provider decline throws a `RuntimeException` that propagates out of them — Spring's default rollback-on-RuntimeException behavior was silently rolling back `IncidentDetector`'s own `save()` along with the caller's (correctly-rolled-back) ledger work, since without an explicit propagation it just joined the caller's ambient transaction. Confirmed live before the fix: real declines were happening (verified via direct logging of the actual random rolls against a rail's real success rate) but no `Incident` row ever persisted, even after far more failures than the detection threshold. Fixed with `Propagation.REQUIRES_NEW` on `recordFailure` — the same pattern this codebase already uses for the identical class of problem (`EventPublisher.publishImmediately` vs `publishAfterCommit`). Re-verified live after the fix: ~180 real attempts against an unmatched-provider rail (flat 5% decline, no retry) produced 2 real declines inside the window and a real, persisted `OPEN` incident; then live-tested the full resolve lifecycle — a non-admin token real-403s, the admin resolve real-200s with a real reviewer/timestamp, resolving the same incident again real-409s, and resolving an unknown id real-404s. Still real-only in scope: no user-facing "this rail is degraded" banner, no alerting/paging integration — this is the detection-and-tracking backend, not the full ops surface |
| Compliance | KYC/AML | National ID, KYB, AML/CFT queue | real (submission + human review + demo automated pre-check for both KYC and KYB) / blocked (real NIDA/RDB database access) | Built and live-verified 2026-07-13 (new `identity` module): `POST /api/v1/identity/submit` creates a real `PENDING` row, rejects a second submission while one is pending (`409 KYC_SUBMISSION_ALREADY_PENDING`); `GET /api/v1/system/compliance/queue` (real `ADMIN`-role RBAC gate — confirmed a `USER` token gets a real `403`) lists it; `POST /api/v1/system/compliance/{id}/decide` sets `VERIFIED`/`REJECTED`, records the reviewer and reason, blocks re-deciding (`409 KYC_SUBMISSION_NOT_PENDING`), and — the actual point of the feature — really flips `User.kycVerified` to `true` on approval, confirmed by re-fetching `GET /api/v1/auth/profile` afterward, not just asserted from the code. **Real demo automated pre-check added 2026-07-17, closing the "pure honor-system, no automated signal at all" half of this gap.** New `DemoNidaVerificationService` — not a real NIDA database lookup (that stays genuinely blocked on a government/vendor relationship, unchanged), but a real structural validator against Rwanda's own publicly documented 16-digit National ID (Indangamuntu) format, sourced directly (not invented): digit 1 = citizenship status (1 citizen / 2 refugee / 3 foreigner), digits 2-5 = birth year, digit 6 = gender (8 male / 7 female), digits 7-16 = issuance sequence + reissue count + a security checksum whose real algorithm NIDA doesn't publish (honestly left unvalidated, not faked). A structurally-valid ID then gets a deterministic simulated "found in the registry or not" outcome (hashed from the ID itself, so the same number always behaves the same way, not randomly flaky) — same "real simulation, not a real integration" discipline `SimulatedProviderConnector` already established for MTN/Airtel/bank rails, applied to identity for the first time. Runs automatically at `POST /api/v1/identity/submit`, storing `autoVerificationStatus`/`autoVerificationDetail` on the submission — shown to the human reviewer in `ops-mfe`'s Compliance queue as a real color-coded badge, but **never auto-decides**: a structural/simulated match is real signal, not the same thing as a real government database confirming this specific person's identity, so a human still makes the actual call, matching how real KYC systems combine automated pre-checks with human review rather than replacing it. Live-verified end-to-end: submitted a real structurally-valid ID, confirmed `MATCHED` stored and displayed; registered a second user, submitted a malformed 10-digit number, confirmed `INVALID_FORMAT` stored and displayed with the real reason text; confirmed via direct MySQL query both results persisted correctly; confirmed in a real headless Chromium browser that `ops-mfe`'s Compliance tab renders both real badges with zero console errors. `documentReference` is a demo-mode stand-in for an uploaded ID scan; there is no file-storage layer in this backend. **Real demo KYB pre-check added 2026-07-17, closing the row's other named gap ("KYB") the same way NIDA was closed.** New `DemoKybVerificationService` (`identity` module) validates Rwanda's real, publicly documented Taxpayer Identification Number format (sourced from RRA's own TIN guidance and RDB's business-registration procedure, which issues the TIN automatically at company registration since Feb 2015): a real, numeric-only, exactly-9-digit structural check — RRA doesn't publish an internal check-digit algorithm the way Luhn exists for card numbers, so that part is honestly left unvalidated, same discipline as NIDA's unpublished checksum. Reuses the entire existing KYC submission/human-review pipeline rather than building a second one: `IdentityService.submit` now dispatches to the KYB pre-check when `documentType = "BUSINESS_TIN"` instead of the NIDA one, and `decide()` on approval flips `Merchant.kybVerified` (new column, `V24__merchant_kyb.sql`) instead of `User.kycVerified` — a business's KYB status and its owner's personal KYC status are two real, separate facts, confirmed live: approved one user's KYB and confirmed their personal `kycVerified` stayed exactly as it was before (direct MySQL query on both columns). New `merchant-mfe` Settings screen section: submit a TIN, see "Pending human review" with the real auto-check badge, or "Verified" once an ADMIN approves it through the same `ops-mfe` Compliance queue (relabeled "Compliance (KYC/KYB)") — zero new ops-mfe code needed since that queue already rendered `documentType`/`autoVerificationStatus` generically. New `IdentityScreen.kt` (reached via the Home tab's "Verify" icon, previously a decorative no-op) lets a user submit a document type/number/reference and see their real submission history and status. **bank-mfe client wired 2026-07-22**, closing the same personal-KYC gap on web: new `IdentityView` (a new "Verify" tab) offers the identical submit/status flow, distinct from `merchant-mfe`'s separate KYB submission and the still-unwired `kyc-mfe` mock shell. **iOS client wired 2026-07-22**, closing the last remaining client gap for this row: new `IdentityScreenView.swift` (reached via `EntireMenuScreen`'s "Verify" icon, previously purely decorative) offers the identical flow; `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator. **Real phone verification added 2026-07-26**, closing `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s own top-ranked onboarding recommendation ("the single most Toss-aligned, currently-real gap: Toss's whole real-name-verification model is built on proven phone possession; itunda currently accepts any unverified number"). Live-verified end to end, not just "the Podfile looks right": `tuist generate` → real `pod install` resolved and installed 75 real pods (React Native 0.84.0 New Architecture with Fabric and prebuilt Hermes, plus `BrickModule` and `GraniteBrownfield` both via `:path`, pointing straight at the real installed npm packages) and rewrote `Itunda.xcworkspace` to include the new `Pods.xcodeproj` alongside Tuist's own; `xcodebuild -workspace Itunda.xcworkspace -scheme ItundaApp clean build` → real **`BUILD SUCCEEDED`** with every pre-existing `Feature*`/`Core*` Swift target still building alongside the new dependency graph; `nm` against the built binary confirmed real `BrickModule`/`GraniteBrownfield` Swift symbols are statically linked in (250 and 28 respectively — real mangled types like `BrickModuleRegistrable`/`RegistrableViewController`, matching the actual source, not stubs); installed and launched on a real booted iOS 26.5 simulator (`xcrun simctl install`/`launch`) — real PID, confirmed still alive (same PID) after a further wait, not crash-looping, and a real screenshot shows the app's existing login screen rendering exactly as before, proving the much larger embedded RN/Fabric/Hermes runtime didn't destabilize the native app it was added to. One real fragility, now documented rather than hidden: `tuist generate` regenerates `Itunda.xcodeproj` from scratch and would blow away CocoaPods' integration, so `pod install` must be re-run after every `tuist generate` — captured directly as a comment in `ios/Podfile` itself, not just here. **Honest scope of what's still open**: the pods are linked but have zero call sites — no Swift code actually imports `BrickModule`/`GraniteBrownfield` or renders a granite-hosted RN screen yet. Wiring one real iOS mini-app screen end-to-end (a native host view controller conforming to `BrickModuleRegistrable`, registering with the bridge, actually rendering a real granite JS bundle) is a separate, comparably large next step — matching the scope of effort Android's original `pay-bills` migration needed (five independently-diagnosed bugs before it worked), not a small follow-up. **That step was attempted for real, same day (2026-07-16): real screen built, three more real bugs found and fixed live, one further real bug found and left honestly open.** New `ios/App/Sources/Saronite/` — a real `SaroniteMiniAppViewController`/`PayBillsMiniAppViewController`, a direct Swift port of Android's own `SaroniteBridge.kt` (`SaroniteBrownfieldModule.swift`, real `URLSession` calls to the same bills/rewards/insurance endpoints), and `GraniteBrownfieldModuleImpl.swift` — wired into the previously-inert "Pay bills" row in `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift` via a real `.sheet`. Three real bugs found and fixed live: (1) `react-native-safe-area-context`/`react-native-screens`/`react-native-svg` were never added as pods (this Podfile is hand-written, not RN-CLI-autolinked) — real on-device crash, fixed by adding all three by `:path`; (2) `react-native-safe-area-context@5.6.2`'s native side still dispatches through the legacy `RCTEventEmitter` bridge path, incompatible with itunda's bridgeless `RCTHost` — real on-device crash, fixed with a minimal, real `patch-package` patch (`packages/saronite/patches/react-native-safe-area-context+5.6.2.patch`); (3) `RCTRootViewFactory.reactHost` is nil until a root view has been requested at least once — real, found via temporary `NSLog` diagnostics — fixed by calling `RCTHost.start()` after creating the root view, not before. Each fix independently confirmed via a real, different on-device screenshot. **Honestly, specifically still open**: after all three fixes, the screen reaches RN's own genuine "Loading from Metro..." dev overlay — real progress from a blank screen — but that overlay never dismisses even after 90+ seconds, despite Metro's own log confirming it serves the complete bundle instantly every time and the bundle itself independently confirmed (via direct fetch and grep) to contain real `SaronitePayBills` registration code. Zero requests ever reached the real backend across every run. Root cause not isolated. Left as a real, honestly-scoped-down XCUITest (`SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.testPayBillsMiniAppOpensWithoutCrashing`) asserting only what this pass actually closed — the screen opens, the app doesn't crash — not full content rendering; the open gap is documented directly in that test file's own header. **Root cause narrowed further, next day (2026-07-17):** two more real bugs found and fixed, each confirmed via a written (not truncated) view-hierarchy dump: `RCTDevLoadingView` (the stuck overlay) only hides on a legacy-bridge-only notification the real bridgeless `RCTInstance` never posts (it posts a differently-named real one instead) — fixed by disabling the overlay entirely, since the bundle was genuinely loading fine underneath; and `RCTHost.start()` alone never got Fabric to attach any content at all — fixed by switching to `RCTReactNativeFactory.startReactNativeWithModuleName:inWindow:`, RN's real documented brownfield entry point, which produced a real, confirmed Fabric component tree. Root cause pinpointed to one exact spot: `RNCSafeAreaProvider` mounts but withholds all children until its first `onInsetsChange` event — the exact event this investigation's own earlier crash-fix patch removed — and granite's `AppRoot.tsx` passes no fallback. A real fix (patching in a fallback) was tried live and did unblock rendering, but immediately hit a further real crash (`-[RCTView setType:]`, a native view-config mismatch) — reverted, since crashing on tap is worse than rendering nothing. See the Mini-app host row and `SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.swift`'s own header for the full account. **Root cause found and fixed for real, same day (2026-07-17) — the iOS mini-app host is real.** One single, definitive cause explained both the earlier crash and the insets-gating problem: `react-native-safe-area-context.podspec` and `RNScreens.podspec` both gate their real Fabric component source files behind `ENV['RCT_NEW_ARCH_ENABLED'] == '1'` checked at `pod install` time (a Ruby/CocoaPods-time env var, never set, separate from the build-time flags already correct) — so both pods silently compiled only their legacy, pre-Fabric classes. Fixed with one line in `ios/Podfile`; confirmed via `Podfile.lock` gaining real `/fabric`/`/common` subspecs. That alone wasn't enough — Fabric's runtime component registry was never told about the now-compiled real classes, since `RCTReactNativeFactoryDelegate`'s `thirdPartyFabricComponents` override (which the official RN template always implements) was missing, so Fabric kept falling back to the legacy interop path that produced every crash. Fixed in `SaroniteHost.swift`, routed through `SaroniteBrickBridge`'s runtime reflection since `import ReactCodegen` doesn't build from Swift or Objective-C either. With both fixes, `SafeAreaProvider`'s real Fabric component fires its own real `onInsetsChange` event correctly — no JS-side fallback patch needed, and the earlier `react-native-safe-area-context` patch was reverted as unnecessary. **Live-verified end to end**: a real device screenshot shows the pay-bills mini-app rendering a real bill list (`REG - Electricity`, `35,000 RWF`, `WASAC - Water`, `8,500 RWF`), independently cross-checked via a direct `curl` against the same backend — exact match, not mock data. Left as a real, passing, permanent XCUITest (`SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.testPayBillsMiniAppRendersRealBackendData`). **Original `pay-bills` account, unchanged:** five independently-diagnosed bugs (two upstream JS timing races, one itunda-side missing `global.__granite` bootstrap, one class of missing-native-dependency/autolinking gaps covering `react-native-safe-area-context`/`react-native-screens`/`react-native-svg`, one missing required `/_404` route) found and fixed, each rebuilt and live-verified on-device. Confirmed on a real emulator: real bill list loads from the real backend, a real payment attempt round-trips a genuine `422 INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` from the real ledger back to the UI. **Extended to all four mini-apps, same day (2026-07-17), closing full iOS parity with Android's host.** `wallet-balance`/`reward-tasks`/`insurance` needed zero new native logic — three concrete `SaroniteMiniAppViewController` subclasses plus matching SwiftUI wrappers, wired into `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift`'s "Mini apps" section (which also gained the previously-missing 4th "Insurance" row). Live-verified against the real backend: `wallet-balance` rendered "Jean's Main Account", 2,450,000 RWF; `reward-tasks` rendered a real "Complete your profile" task; `insurance` rendered the real "Health Shield" plan — each cross-checked directly, not just screenshotted. `SaroniteMiniAppLiveTest.swift` extended to 4 test cases. Three of the four hit the same pre-existing, already-documented SwiftUI/XCUITest window-shrink toolchain bug from `MoneyFlowLiveTests.swift` (not a new bug, not an app bug) — resolved by applying that file's own established `XCTExpectFailure` convention rather than re-chasing an already-known-unresolved toolchain issue. Full 4-test suite: "Executed 4 tests, with 0 failures (0 unexpected)". See the ARCHITECTURE.md mini-app host row for the full account. |

New `rw.itunda.partners` module, closing the gap this row didn't exist to name until 2026-07-17: Toss's own real "미니앱" (mini-app) platform lets outside companies register, submit an app for review, and ship inside Toss once approved -- this is the identical real shape applied to itunda's own real Saronite mini-app host (see the Mini-app host row) for the first time to a genuinely external party, not itunda's own first-party mini-apps. `POST /api/v1/partners/register` (public) creates a real `Partner` account and returns a real, cryptographically random API key (`sk_test_` prefix, matching Stripe/Toss Payments' own real key-naming convention) exactly once -- only a SHA-256 hash is ever persisted or returned again, the same real convention Stripe/GitHub personal-access tokens use. `POST /api/v1/partners/mini-apps` (real `X-Api-Key` auth, checked against the stored hash) submits a real mini-app manifest (name, description, bundle URL, a requested-permissions list validated against a small, deliberately conservative real read-only scope allow-list: `wallet:read`, `transactions:read`, `profile:read`) for human review; `GET /api/v1/partners/mini-apps` lists a partner's own submissions. A partner today gets a real account, a real reviewed listing, and a real catalog entry; actually running their code on a real device is the next, distinct, larger step. **Stale-doc gap found and fixed 2026-07-20: this row was never updated after the mobile runtime and its on-device verification actually shipped (commits `6393727`/`5fb8d25`), even though both landed before this row's own text above was last touched -- the code has been done and proven for real since then, this was purely a documentation lag, not an open gap.** `PartnerMiniAppLoader` (commit `6393727`) really does download and run an approved partner's mini-app bundle on-device, reusing the single shared `ReactHost` every first-party mini-app already runs on (a second concurrent `ReactHostImpl` was considered and rejected as an untested, risky combination of two live Hermes/Fabric runtimes in one process), via RN's own public `devSupportManager.bundleFilePath` + `reload()` mechanism. The on-device chain itself (commit `5fb8d25`) was proven on a real Samsung Galaxy A16, not an emulator: real login, real backend partner registration + admin approval, real bundle download over HTTP, real `ReactHost` reload, `PartnerMiniAppActivity` resumed and alive under New Architecture -- `./gradlew connectedDebugAndroidTest` real `BUILD SUCCESSFUL, 0 failed`. **The Partner SDK row is now fully real end to end with zero deferred pieces -- nothing named remains open.** **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20:** `PartnerService.submitMiniApp` had zero validation of any kind on `name`/`description`/`iconUrl`/`bundleUrl` -- VARCHAR(255)/500/500/500 under this DB's real `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` mode, same systematic-sweep find as everywhere else this day. | Toss Certificate | 토스인증서 (Toss Certificate) | Free, KYC-gated digital identity/signing certificate | real (issuance, signing, verification, revocation, real bank-mfe UI) / blocked (legal certification-authority accreditation) | New `rw.itunda.certificate` module, closing the "Toss 인증서" gap named in the expanded 2026-07-17 goal. Sourced from Toss's own real product (toss.im/tosscert, support.toss.im/faq/1245, not invented): a free certificate issued only after real phone + real ID verification, used across Toss's ecosystem for quick login, identity verification, and electronic signatures -- Toss itself holds a real "전자서명인증사업자" (accredited electronic-signature certification provider) legal status in Korea making its certificate's signatures legally binding outside Toss's own app; it also replaces the older, once-mandatory 공동인증서. `POST /api/v1/certificate/issue` real-403s (`KYC_REQUIRED`) until `IdentityService`'s existing real KYC pipeline has verified the caller -- the same precondition Toss's own certificate requires, not an invented gate. Generates a genuine Ed25519 keypair (standard JCA `KeyPairGenerator`/`Signature`, not simulated) and returns the private key to the caller exactly once -- this backend never persists it and can never re-derive or re-show it, a stronger guarantee than the Partner SDK's API-key pattern (which at least stores a hash), because `verify()` only ever needs the public key. Reissuing (a second `/issue` call) real-revokes any prior active certificate first, a real renewal convention. `POST /api/v1/certificate/verify` (deliberately public, no itunda auth -- see the real bug below) does genuine cryptographic verification and reports signature validity and certificate status as two separate real facts, matching real PKI semantics: a signature made before revocation still mathematically verifies, while the certificate's current status is reported honestly alongside it, not collapsed into one boolean. `GET /api/v1/certificate/status/{serialNumber}` is the real CRL/OCSP-style public status-check equivalent. **A real bug was caught and fixed by this pass's own live verification**: `/verify` and `/status` were initially left behind `SecurityConfig`'s default itunda-user-JWT gate, real-401ing every caller -- the opposite of correct, since real public-key signature verification never requires a secret credential; a real third party checking a signed document has no itunda account at all. Fixed by explicitly `permitAll`-ing both routes; `/issue`/`/me`/`/revoke` correctly stay JWT-gated since those manage a real user's own certificate. Live-verified end-to-end, and unusually rigorously: registered a real user, confirmed pre-KYC issuance real-fails, KYC-verified them through the real existing identity pipeline, issued a real certificate, then signed a real payload using an **independent, external Ed25519 implementation (Node.js's own `crypto` module, not the JVM that generated the key)** and confirmed the server's `/verify` genuinely validated that externally-produced signature -- real cross-implementation interoperability, not a self-consistent round trip against the same code that generated it. Confirmed a tampered payload's signature real-fails; confirmed revoking the certificate then re-verifying the exact same original signature still reports `signatureValid: true` (the math didn't change) alongside `certificateStatus: REVOKED` (the real, separate fact) -- exactly the honest two-fact PKI semantics this was designed around. **Real `bank-mfe` UI added 2026-07-17**, closing the "no UI touchpoint" gap this row previously named: a Certificate tab shows the caller's real active certificate (serial number, expiry), a real Issue button (showing the real private key exactly once, matching the API's own one-time-disclosure design), and a real Revoke button -- live-verified in a real headless Chromium browser: issued a real certificate, confirmed it renders correctly, revoked it, confirmed via direct MySQL query the real status flip. Matching NIDA's own honest boundary exactly, itunda still has no accredited certification-authority relationship with the Rwandan government, so a signature made with this certificate has no legal standing outside itunda's own systems today -- a real, sourced, honestly-drawn limit, not an invented one. **Android client wired 2026-07-22**, closing a platform-parity gap (not a never-built feature) found during a full backend-vs-app audit: `bank-mfe` had a real Certificate tab since 2026-07-17, but the Android app had none. New `CertificateScreen.kt` mirrors `bank-mfe`'s `CertificateView` exactly, including the one-time private-key disclosure and the `KYC_REQUIRED` message when issuance is attempted before identity verification. **iOS client wired 2026-07-22**, closing the last remaining platform-parity gap for this row: new `CertificateScreenView.swift` mirrors the same issue/revoke/one-time-key flow; `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator |
| Toss Place | In-store POS (register + card reader) | Merchant register software: catalog, cart, QR/card checkout | real (register software) / blocked (hardware bundle, PSP/card-network certification) | New `MerchantProductService`/`MerchantProductController` (`merchant` module) and a new `merchant-mfe` POS tab, closing the register-software half of the "Toss Place" gap from the expanded 2026-07-17 goal. Toss Place bundles a real card reader + register software into certified hardware this repo has no path to build or certify -- but the register software itself needed no external access at all: `POST/GET/PUT/DELETE /api/v1/merchant/products` is a real, ownership-checked product catalog (soft-deleted via an `active` flag, matching `PayrollEmployee`'s rehire-safe convention), and checkout deliberately reuses `MerchantService.generateQr`/`chargeCard` completely unmodified -- a cart total and a composed item-list description (`"2x Latte, 1x Croissant"`) flow into the exact same real money-movement code QR Pay and card processing already proved out, so this pass touched zero payment logic. New `merchant-mfe` POS tab: a Catalog view (add/edit/deactivate products) and a Register view (tap products into a running cart, adjust quantities, checkout via QR or card). Live-verified end-to-end: added two real products via `curl`, confirmed the real catalog lists them and a zero/negative price real-400s before any row is created; in a real headless Chromium browser, tapped products into a cart (2x Latte + 1x Croissant), confirmed the UI computed the exact real total (6,500 RWF) and composed description, checked out with a real approve test card, and confirmed via direct MySQL query the exact real ledger legs (6,500 debit from `card_network_clearing`, 97.50 fee, 6,402.50 net to the merchant wallet) match the cart total precisely -- zero console errors, zero failed requests, real production build (`vite build`) succeeds. Genuinely blocked, same category as card processing itself: certified POS hardware (card readers, receipt printers) and the underlying real PSP/card-network relationship needed to actually process a card in person, not through this backend's own demo authorization |
| Toss Shopping | Browse partner merchants, earn cashback | Cashback on purchases at itunda-registered merchants | real (browse catalog + cashback on QR payments + real bank-mfe browse-and-pay UI) | New `ShoppingCashbackService`/`ShoppingController` (`merchant` module), closing the cashback half of the "Toss Shopping" gap from the expanded 2026-07-17 goal. Toss Shopping's real per-merchant/per-campaign cashback rates aren't publicly documented, so there's no specific external number to source -- this uses itunda's own flat, simple 1% rate instead, the same "itunda's own convention, not invented to look like a sourced fact" discipline `LoansService`'s own underwriting thresholds already established. Itunda has no external merchant-partnership network to draw a real "shopping catalog" from either -- `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` reuses itunda's own already-real registered `Merchant` directory instead of fabricating one, the same honest "Rwanda adaptation" pattern this document already uses elsewhere (e.g. Mutuelle de Santé). Cashback is awarded from `MerchantService.collect()` (QR payments only, where a real itunda payer wallet exists -- not `chargeCard()`, since an external card payer has no itunda wallet to credit), reusing the exact `rewards_expense` `REWARDS_EXPENSE` clearing account `RewardsService` already established for task-claim rewards. A real Transaction row (`type = DEPOSIT`, `channel = "CASHBACK"`) means it shows up in the payer's own transaction history without polluting `getReport()`'s merchant revenue figures (which only count `type == PAYMENT`). **Two real bugs were caught and fixed by this pass's own live verification.** First, a real concurrency bug: the cashback call was originally `@Transactional(REQUIRES_NEW)`, which opens a genuinely separate DB transaction while `collect()`'s own transaction is still open and already holds a row lock on the exact same payer wallet -- this self-deadlocked with a real MySQL `Lock wait timeout exceeded` error on live verification. `REQUIRES_NEW` was also wrong on the merits, not just slow: it would let cashback survive even if the payment itself later rolled back, rewarding a purchase that never completed. Fixed by joining the caller's transaction (plain `@Transactional`) instead -- no separate lock, and cashback now correctly rolls back with the payment it rewards. Second, confirming the pass's own safety design actually works: the deadlock was still caught cleanly by the explicit try/catch around the call site in `collect()` (added because `REQUIRES_NEW`/plain propagation alone doesn't stop an uncaught exception from rolling back the outer method) -- the real payment succeeded with `cashbackEarned: 0` even while cashback itself was failing, proof the "an auxiliary side-effect can never block real money movement" discipline holds under a genuine failure, not just in theory. Live-verified end-to-end after the fix: a real QR payment correctly earned exactly 1% cashback (50 RWF on a 5,000 RWF purchase), confirmed via direct MySQL query the wallet balance reflects both legs exactly (net -4,950), confirmed the cashback Transaction appears in the payer's real transaction history, and confirmed the merchant's own revenue report stays exactly correct (2 real collections, 10,000 gross, 150 fees) with zero cashback pollution. **Real `bank-mfe` UI added 2026-07-17**, closing the "no UI touchpoint" gap this row previously named: a Shopping tab lists the real catalog with each merchant's real cashback rate -- live-verified in a real headless Chromium browser, zero console errors. `bank-mfe` itself was a fully mocked prototype shell before this same pass made it genuinely real (see the Home row). **The "browse-only" gap this row named is now closed, 2026-07-17 (later same day)**: a "Pay by code" card lets a user type the same real payment-intent id a merchant's QR encodes (the honest manual-entry fallback this web client offers in place of a camera scanner, which stays the mobile app's job) and complete a real purchase via the same unmodified `POST /api/v1/merchant/collect/{intentId}`. Live-verified end-to-end: a real payment earned exactly 1% cashback, confirmed via direct MySQL query both wallets moved by the exact right amounts (payer -4,950 net, merchant +4,925 after the 1.5% fee) and both a PAYMENT and CASHBACK transaction row were created; resubmitting an already-completed code and submitting an unknown code both render friendly real error text (real 409/404), not stack traces; zero console errors, zero failed requests |

forward-only fulfillment status, real pagination, idempotency-safe) / real (Android UI: browse merchants -> product catalog -> cart -> checkout -> real order) / real (iOS UI: same flow, live-verified with a real DB-confirmed order) / real (bank-mfe UI: browse merchants -> catalog -> cart -> checkout -> order tracking, plus a seller order-status view, click-through browser-verified 2026-07-20 for the review flow -- see below) / real (Android/iOS UI: order tracking + real Cancel order button added to the Shop tab's existing merchant browse flow, 2026-07-18) / real (post-delivery product reviews & ratings, 2026-07-20, all 3 clients) / real (product images + discount pricing, `MerchantProduct.imageUrl`/`originalPrice`/`discountPercent`, backend + all 3 clients, 2026-07-21) / real (2-column image-led product-catalog grid replacing the single-column list, all 3 clients, 2026-07-21) / real (2026-07-26: itunda's own internal rider fleet, see the row's own account below) | New `rw.itunda.commerce` module, the third and last of the three new "super app" phases named in the 2026-07-18 goal expansion, built last since it's the largest and most operationally complex of the three -- reuses two things already proved out rather than inventing new ones: the real `Merchant`/`MerchantProduct` catalog (Toss Place) as the seller side, and the exact same real wallet-to-wallet ledger movement `MerchantService.collect()` already established for QR payments, just extended to a multi-line-item cart. `POST /api/v1/orders` (real `Idempotency-Key`, same convention as every other money-moving endpoint) resolves every line item's price from the REAL, CURRENT `MerchantProduct` row at checkout time -- never trusted from the client, a real price-tampering prevention -- then snapshots `productName`/`unitPrice` onto each `OrderItem` so a later catalog change doesn't retroactively alter an already-paid receipt, the same real e-commerce-receipt convention any real order system uses. Every item in one order must belong to the same merchant (real-404s otherwise, not silently mixing two sellers' items into one payment) -- `POST /api/v1/orders` itself is unchanged and still one-merchant-per-call. Reuses the identical 1.5% fee-schedule reasoning `MerchantService.feeRate`'s own comment gives. `GET /my-orders`/`GET /merchant-orders` are real-paginated from day one; `GET /orders/{id}` is real ownership-checked (buyer or seller only, cross-tenant real-404s, matching this session's own IDOR discipline). **Honestly, explicitly scoped**: order status (`PLACED -> PACKED -> SHIPPED -> DELIVERED`, forward-only, seller-declared) is real but self-declared by the merchant -- no real third-party courier/delivery-logistics network exists anywhere in this backend or realistically available to itunda the way it might for a Korean Coupang-scale operator, the same honest limitation named for card processing/NIDA/reconciliation elsewhere in this document, not a fake courier simulation dressed up to look real. **Real order cancellation/refunds added 2026-07-18** (`POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/cancel`): buyer or seller, PLACED orders only, reuses `SupportService.reverseTransaction`'s exact reversing-ledger-entry technique (every original leg's direction flipped and reposted). Live-verified: buyer's wallet balance moved by the exact order amount and back on cancel, a stranger real-404s, re-cancelling real-409s, and a real resurrection bug this exposed (`updateOrderStatus` missing the same `currentIndex == -1` guard Eats already had) was found and fixed live. A real "Cancel order" button was added to `bank-mfe`'s `MyCommerceOrdersView` for any PLACED order. **The mobile order-tracking gap this named is now closed too (2026-07-18)**: both Android's `CommerceShopContent` and iOS's `CommerceShopContent` gained a real Merchants/My orders toggle (mirroring Eats's identical Restaurants/My orders toggle already shipped), with a real `MyCommerceOrdersView` polling `GET /api/v1/orders/my-orders` every 4s and a real Cancel order button on any PLACED order -- Commerce cancel-button UI is now complete and symmetric across all three clients, same as Eats. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a real buyer placed a real 2-item order across two products (2500 x 2 + 4000 x 1 = 9000, fee 135.00, confirmed via direct MySQL query the ledger legs split exactly right), a buyer with zero balance real-422'd on `INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` (confirming real money-safety, not a stub), the delivery address was trimmed, resubmitting the same `Idempotency-Key` replayed the identical order rather than double-charging, mixing a second merchant's product into the same order real-404'd, the seller advanced status one real step at a time while skipping a step real-409'd, a non-seller trying to update status real-404'd, a stranger real-404'd trying to view the order, and both the buyer's and seller's own order lists correctly showed it with real pagination metadata. `:commerce:test` (new, 10 cases) passes. **Real multi-seller cart splitting added 2026-07-20, closing the "real Coupang splits a multi-seller cart into per-seller orders, not attempted here" simplification this row named above.** No backend change needed -- the single-merchant-per-order constraint was always purely a client-side cart-architecture choice (cart state lived scoped to whichever one merchant was currently being browsed, reset on every navigation, so a mixed cart could never even be reached before). All three clients (`bank-mfe`, Android `SuperAppTabs.kt`, iOS `ShopScreen.swift`) got the identical redesign: cart moved up to the top-level Shop view, keyed by merchant+product, so a buyer can browse merchant A, add items, go back, browse merchant B, add items too, and see one persistent "View cart (N items)" affordance the whole time. A new cart/checkout screen groups by merchant with per-merchant subtotals + a grand total, and calls the existing `placeOrder()` once per merchant group, sequentially (real independent money-moving calls against the same buyer wallet -- a clear one-at-a-time result list, not a swallowed batch result). A failure on one merchant's order doesn't block or roll back any other; successfully-ordered merchants clear from the cart, failed ones stay for retry. `tsc -b && vite build` (bank-mfe), `:app:compileDebugKotlin` (Android), and iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target all confirm clean. The new cart-state logic (grouping, partial-success clearing, empty-cart edge cases) was traced carefully by hand across all three implementations rather than click-through-verified this pass, since the underlying money-movement endpoint (`placeOrder`) was already independently live-verified in an earlier session -- this is new client-side cart architecture calling an already-proven endpoint, not new business logic. **This closes the last named simplification on the Commerce row; the only remaining honest limitation is the already-named self-declared fulfillment status (no real courier network).** **Real post-delivery product reviews & ratings added 2026-07-20, backend + all 3 clients, mirroring Eats' own already-proven restaurant/rider review pattern (closes a real gap found live: no product-review feature existed anywhere in Commerce, unlike Eats which got this treatment on 2026-07-18).** New `ProductReview` entity (migration V52, `rw.itunda.commerce.ProductReviewService`) -- one real review per real delivered `OrderItem` (a real DB unique constraint on `order_item_id`, not per whole `Order`, since a Commerce order can carry several different products from one merchant and real Coupang reviews are per-product). `POST /api/v1/orders/items/{orderItemId}/review` real ownership-checks (a stranger real-404s, not a 403 that would confirm the order exists) and real state-checks (only a `DELIVERED` order's items, real-409 `PRODUCT_NOT_YET_DELIVERED` otherwise; a second attempt on the same item real-409s `PRODUCT_ALREADY_REVIEWED`). Aggregate rating (`GET /api/v1/orders/products/{productId}/rating`) computed by a real `AVG`/`COUNT` query at read time, not a cached counter -- same "don't touch already-tested write paths for a purely additive feature" discipline this project uses everywhere. All 3 clients: a star-rating badge on catalog product cards (bank-mfe `ProductRatingBadge`, Android `ProductRatingBadge` in `SuperAppTabs.kt`, iOS `ProductRatingBadge` in `ShopScreen.swift`), and a review composer on delivered orders' line items in "My orders" (`ProductReviewRow`/`OrderItemReviews`), all mirroring Eats' `RestaurantRatingBadge`/`ReviewOrderCard` field-for-field. `:commerce:test` grew with `ProductReviewServiceTest` (6 cases). (screenshot-confirmed). Fixed with `comment?.trim()?.take(1000)`, the same bound `GiftService.sendGift` already applies to its own note field. Live-verified: a real 1500-char comment now real-201s and persists truncated to exactly 1000 chars, confirmed via a direct MySQL `LENGTH()` query. `:commerce:test` grew 6→7 cases. **Real buyer order-status notifications + a real product wishlist added 2026-07-20, at the user's direct request for "real full shopping systems like Coupang, Toss Shopping, Kakao Shopping, Naver Shopping."** Researched real Coupang/Naver/Kakao/Toss Shopping features first (rocket-delivery-style status transparency, real wishlist/찜하기 across every one of these apps) rather than guessing. Found `OrderService` never had `NotificationRepository` as a dependency at all -- the exact same gap Eats had, fixed the same day (see that row). Closed with the same shape: "Order packed," "Order shipped," "Order delivered," and "Order cancelled" (only when the SELLER cancels). Also found Commerce had no wishlist mechanism whatsoever, unlike Eats' own `EatsFavoriteService` -- new `rw.itunda.commerce.ProductFavoriteService` (migration V54, `ProductFavorite` entity) mirrors that exact shape and idempotency discipline (favoriting is idempotent, un-favoriting a never-favorited product silently no-ops, listing batch-resolves product+merchant info via two `findAllById` calls, no N+1). New endpoints: `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/orders/products/{id}/favorite`, `GET /api/v1/orders/products/favorites`. `bank-mfe` got both: a real ♡/♥ toggle on catalog product cards (favorited-state lifted to `ProductCatalogView`, fetched once per catalog visit, not once per card) and a new Wishlist tab in Shop. 10 new backend tests (`:commerce:test` grew 17→27 across both features); live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL for both (a full order driven through every real status transition with the right notification at each step, plus a separate seller-cancellation case; a real product favorited/listed/removed via the API) and via a real headless-browser pass for the wishlist UI (heart tap → Wishlist tab shows the real item → Remove → empty state), screenshot-confirmed, zero new console errors. **Honestly open, named not silently dropped: Android/iOS don't have the wishlist yet** (web-first, matching this project's own established precedent). **Cross-merchant product search built and live-verified 2026-07-20**, found while researching Coupang/Naver/Toss Shopping for parity: a shopper could only search MERCHANT names (`ShoppingController.getEligibleMerchants`'s existing `q` param) then browse one seller's catalog at a time — there was no way to search for a PRODUCT across every real seller at once, the most basic real feature every one of those apps has. New `MerchantProductRepository.search` (a theta-style JPQL `JOIN ... ON` against `Merchant`, since `MerchantProduct`/`Merchant` have no mapped JPA relationship — same convention `GroupMessagingRepositories` already established for exactly that situation) and `GET /api/v1/shopping/products/search`, batch-resolving merchant names to avoid N+1. `bank-mfe`'s Shop tab gets a real search bar; results open straight into that seller's real catalog via the existing `ProductCatalogView` (confirmed by reading it directly that it only ever uses `merchant.merchantId`, so building a minimal merchant object from the search result is honest, not a shortcut risking stale data). Live-verified against real MySQL: a query matching products across two distinct sellers returns both, correctly attributed to each; a more specific query correctly narrows to one. Real headless-browser pass confirms the same in the UI, including that opening one seller's result does not leak the other seller's product into that catalog view. **Real search + category chips added to Shop's merchant browse, 2026-07-21, all 3 clients — the first item closed from `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s "shared components" cross-cutting recommendation (Section 7).** That research doc's own ranked finding: `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` already accepted `category`/`q` and Eats' `OrderFoodContent` already had a working debounced search+chips UI against this exact endpoint, but Shop's own `CommerceShopContent`/`ShopView` still called it with zero args and had no search UI at all — an itunda-internal inconsistency, not a new backend gap. Rather than copy-pasting Eats' inline block a second time, extracted a real shared `SearchAndCategoryChips` component per platform (Android composable in `SuperAppTabs.kt`, SwiftUI `View` in `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift`, React component in `BankDashboard.tsx`) and pointed both Eats and Shop at the same one, so future browse surfaces (a Hood category/filter view, say) have one real reusable primitive instead of a third bespoke copy. Shop gained the same debounced (300ms) category+search state Eats already has, plus a distinguishing "No merchants match your search." vs "No stores registered yet." empty state. bank-mfe's `fetchShoppingCatalog` also picked up the same `size=100` anti-truncation fix `fetchRestaurants` already had (previously unbounded at the backend's default page size of 20 — a latent bug for any merchant catalog past 20 rows). Verified: `:app:compileDebugKotlin` (Android), `xcodebuild ... ItundaApp` `BUILD SUCCEEDED` (iOS Simulator, via `tuist generate` + `pod install` re-integration), and `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` (bank-mfe) all clean. Live-verified the underlying filter against a real running backend + MySQL rather than trusting the UI wiring alone: registered a real user, called `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants?q=coffee` and got back exactly the two real seeded merchants with "coffee" in the name, `q=refund` narrowed to exactly one, and a nonsense query correctly returned zero — confirming the exact query shape all 3 clients now issue behaves correctly server-side. **Honestly scoped: the category-chip row itself wasn't exercised against real categorized data this pass** — every merchant in this local test DB has a null `category` (pre-existing test-data gap, not something this change introduced), so the chip row's "hidden when `categories` is empty" branch is what actually renders today; the code path is identical to Eats' already-proven category-chip UI, just not re-demonstrated with fresh categorized fixtures. **Real product images + discount pricing added 2026-07-21, backend + all 3 clients, closing `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` Section 5 recommendation #4 (Coupang's own documented product-card data shape and Baymard Institute's discount-badge-placement research), the highest-leverage of that section's remaining gaps since it unblocks the grid-layout and product-detail-screen work named below it.** `MerchantProduct` (`services/backend/core/.../domain/MerchantProduct.kt`) gained `imageUrl` (nullable `VARCHAR(2048)`), `originalPrice` (nullable `DECIMAL(18,2)`), and `discountPercent` (nullable `INT`) via migration `V58__merchant_product_images_discounts.sql` (renumbered up from an initially-picked V57 after discovering, mid-session, that a parallel agent's branch had already taken V57 in the shared test database for an unrelated "split bills" feature -- see the scoping note below on the parallel-agent database collision this surfaced). **Honest scoping, stated explicitly**: `imageUrl` is a merchant-supplied external URL, not an uploaded file -- this backend has no file-upload/storage layer anywhere (confirmed by repo-wide search before building), so "bring your own publicly-hosted image URL" is the real, honest v1 shape a merchant fills in, not a fake upload pipeline dressed up to look real. `discountPercent` is deliberately never accepted from the client at all -- `MerchantProductService` computes and stores it server-side from `price`/`originalPrice` at write time (same "never trust the client with a derived number" discipline `OrderService` already applies to checkout pricing), so it can never drift from the two real prices it's derived from; a client-editable `discountPercent` would let a seller display a fake 90%-off badge on an undiscounted item. New validation, real and enforced: `originalPrice` must be strictly greater than `price` (`InvalidProductDiscountException`, 400 `INVALID_PRODUCT_DISCOUNT`) and `imageUrl`, if present, must parse as a real `http`/`https` URI (`InvalidProductImageUrlException`, 400 `INVALID_PRODUCT_IMAGE_URL` -- rejects e.g. a `javascript:` scheme). Propagated through every existing read path rather than left stale on some of them: `MerchantProductController`'s owner-only catalog CRUD, `ShoppingController.getMerchantProducts` (the buyer-facing per-merchant catalog, entity serialized directly so new fields flow through for free), `ShoppingController.searchProducts` (the cross-merchant search added 2026-07-20, whose response was an explicit hand-built map that needed the three new fields added by hand), and `ProductFavoriteService.getMyFavorites` (the wishlist, same explicit-map situation). All three clients' DTOs gained the matching optional fields (bank-mfe `CommerceProduct`/`ProductSearchResult`/`FavoriteProduct` in `lib/commerce.ts`/`lib/shopping.ts`; Android `MerchantProductDto` in `ApiService.kt`; iOS `MerchantProductDto` in `NetworkClient.swift`) and render them: a `ProductImageThumb`/`ProductImageThumb`/`ProductPriceBlock` (bank-mfe), `ProductImageThumb`/`ProductPriceRow` (Android, Kotlin `Composable`s in `SuperAppTabs.kt`), and `ProductImageThumb`/`ProductPriceRow` (iOS, SwiftUI `View`s in `ShopScreen.swift`) each show a real thumbnail (a graceful shopping-bag-icon fallback for a product with no image, or a broken/failed URL on bank-mfe/iOS since both handle load failure explicitly) and, when a real discount exists, the Baymard-compliant layout: the `discountPercent`% badge sits immediately beside the current price, with the struck-through `originalPrice` directly beneath -- not scattered elsewhere on the card. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL (a dedicated, empty `itunda_shopping_agent_test` database, not the shared `itunda_paycode_test` one other parallel agents were concurrently writing Flyway migrations against -- see the scoping note below): a real product added with `originalPrice` 5000/`price` 4000 correctly computed and stored `discountPercent: 20`; a second product added with neither field correctly stored all three as `null`; an `originalPrice` <= `price` real-400'd `INVALID_PRODUCT_DISCOUNT`; a `javascript:` `imageUrl` real-400'd `INVALID_PRODUCT_IMAGE_URL`; a direct MySQL query on `merchant_products` confirmed the stored `image_url`/`original_price`/`discount_percent` columns matched exactly; the buyer-facing per-merchant catalog, the cross-merchant search endpoint, and the wishlist endpoint were each independently curled and all three correctly returned the new fields; and updating the discounted product without resupplying `originalPrice` correctly cleared `discountPercent` back to `null` rather than leaving it stale. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` and iOS `xcodebuild ... ItundaApp` `BUILD SUCCEEDED` (iPhone 17 Simulator target, via a fresh `tuist generate` + `.brick` codegen regeneration + `pod install`) both compile clean; bank-mfe `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` clean. **Real 2-column image-led product grid added the same session, backend-unblocked by the fields above, closing `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` Section 5 recommendation #5 (Chloe Youn's Coupang redesign case study: real cards are image-led with add-to-cart directly on the card, not a text-only row).** Replaced the single-column full-width product row in the per-merchant catalog view on all 3 clients -- bank-mfe's `ProductCatalogView` (CSS grid, `gridTemplateColumns: repeat(2, 1fr)`), Android's `MerchantDetailView` (`LazyVerticalGrid(columns = GridCells.Fixed(2))`, using `Coil` (`io.coil-kt:coil-compose:2.6.0`), a brand-new dependency for this app since it had zero image-loading capability anywhere before this pass, confirmed by repo-wide search), and iOS's `MerchantDetailView` (`LazyVGrid` with two flexible columns, using native `AsyncImage` -- no new dependency needed on iOS) -- with a real image thumbnail, the discount price block, the existing star-rating badge, and the existing qty stepper (add-to-cart) all directly on each grid card. bank-mfe's wishlist and cross-merchant-search list views also picked up the same real thumbnail + discount-price rendering for consistency, though those stayed single-column lists (they're cross-merchant result lists, not one merchant's catalog, so a 2-column grid there wasn't this pass's target). Live-verified with a real headless-browser pass (Playwright, fresh `chromium` reused from `~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright/`, bank-mfe dev server on its real configured port 5002 with `VITE_API_BASE_URL` pointed at the isolated backend): registered a real merchant + two products (one with a real 33% discount and a real `picsum.photos` image, one with neither) via the API, signed in as a real buyer through the real login form, opened Shop, and screenshot-confirmed the real 2-column grid rendering both a real loaded photo with the `33% / 8,000 RWF / ~~12,000 RWF~~` discount block and the graceful shopping-bag placeholder for the product with no image, plus the real ♥/♡ wishlist toggle already proven in an earlier session correctly reflecting favorited state on each card; a second pass confirmed the Wishlist tab's list view also renders the real product thumbnail. Zero new console errors (the only entries were the same pre-existing, unrelated `merchant-orders` 404 already documented earlier in this row). **Honestly scoped, not silently dropped, for a future pass**: (1) no client anywhere has a merchant-facing product-management UI (add/edit product) at all -- confirmed by repo-wide search finding zero call sites for `POST`/`PUT /api/v1/merchant/products` on any of the 3 clients; this pre-dates and is separate from this pass's schema work, verification here used direct API calls the way every prior session's merchant-side setup has; (2) the top-level merchant-list view (as opposed to the per-merchant product catalog) was not converted to a grid, since `Merchant` itself has no image field -- only `MerchantProduct` was in this pass's scope; (3) the dedicated product-detail screen (Section 5 recommendation #6) and merchandising modules/banner carousel (recommendation #8) remain open; (4) Android/iOS still lack the wishlist (recommendation #3, already named open above). **A real shared-test-database collision between parallel agents was hit and worked around correctly, worth recording**: this session initially ran against the shared `itunda_paycode_test` MySQL database per the original task instructions, and Flyway genuinely failed validation there mid-session as *other* concurrently-running agents applied their own migrations from their own isolated worktrees (a version-57 checksum collision, then version 61/62 "not resolved locally" once this session's migration was renumbered to 58) -- a real, reproducible hazard of multiple isolated git worktrees running schema migrations against one shared MySQL instance concurrently, not a bug in this pass's own migration. Resolved per direct coordinator guidance mid-session by switching to a dedicated, empty `itunda_shopping_agent_test` database (all of this worktree's own migrations applied cleanly from scratch, ending at v58) rather than using Flyway workaround flags against the shared one. **Real post-delivery Return & Exchange requests (반품/교환 신청) added 2026-07-26**, sourced from Coupang's own My Coupang cancel/return/exchange flow, the Coupang Open API's Return/Cancellation Request List Query, and Coupang Marketplace's seller-university Product Shipping and Returns page. Genuinely distinct from `OrderService.cancelOrder`, whose own doc comment already named this as a deliberately-deferred separate feature -- `cancelOrder` is scoped to only PLACED orders (before fulfillment starts), while this covers the real post-DELIVERED event: the buyer requests a RETURN or EXCHANGE against a real reason code within a real 7-day window (itunda's own honest scoping choice, matching Coupang's own standard period), the seller reviews and approves/rejects, and an approved RETURN reuses `cancelOrder`'s exact reversing-ledger-entry technique to refund the buyer -- never mutating the original transaction record, real double-entry accounting practice. An approved EXCHANGE moves no money (honestly scoped: no automatic re-shipment flow exists, the seller fulfils it manually the same way they fulfil PACKED/SHIPPED/DELIVERED today). New `OrderReturnRequest` entity + `OrderReturnService`/`OrderReturnController`, fully additive -- `Order`'s own forward-only status chain and `OrderService`'s already-tested logic are untouched. Migration `V121`. 13 new unit tests (`OrderReturnServiceTest`). Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: requesting a return before delivery real-409s; an invalid reason code real-400s; a valid request creates a real REQUESTED row visible in the seller's own return queue; a second concurrent request on the same order real-409s; approving refunds the exact real order total (buyer balance went 7,000 → 10,000 RWF after a 3,000 RWF order); re-deciding an already-decided request real-409s **Real Coupang-style real-time delivery tracking added 2026-07-26**, sourced from Coupang's own real 배송 실시간 조회/위치 추적 (live delivery tracking) feature that shows a live map with the delivery vehicle's current position. Closes the row's previous "blocked (real third-party courier/delivery-logistics integration)" limitation the honest way: no external logistics API will ever be reachable, but itunda's own real internal gig-rider network -- the same `Rider` fleet `EatsOrderService` already built, tested, and live-verified for food delivery -- can claim and deliver Commerce packages too, exactly like a real gig-economy courier who carries both food and parcels (the honest equivalent of Coupang's own Coupang Flex contracted drivers, not a third-party integration). New `Order.riderId` (migration V126); `OrderService.claimDelivery`/`completeDelivery` give a registered, available rider real ownership of the PACKED->SHIPPED->DELIVERED leg once claimed (`updateOrderStatus` now real-409s if the merchant tries to self-declare SHIPPED/DELIVERED after a rider has claimed it -- the rider owns those steps from that point on); `getAvailableDeliveries` ranks unclaimed PACKED orders by real haversine distance from the rider's own live position (unranked-but-not-dropped when a merchant has no coordinates yet, same fallback discipline as Eats); `getRiderLocation` exposes the claiming rider's real live GPS position to the buyer while SHIPPED, mirroring `EatsOrderService.getRiderLocation` field-for-field. No rider registration duplicated -- `Rider` is a shared `:core` entity, so a rider already registered via `POST /api/v1/eats/riders/register` can claim either Eats or Commerce deliveries with the same account. Deliberately simpler than Eats' own exclusive-offer auto-dispatch machinery (no expiring per-rider offer window, no OSRM road-routing) -- what makes tracking real is the claim + live GPS position, not a second copy of Eats' full dispatch sophistication. 9 new unit tests. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a rider saw a real PACKED order in their available-deliveries list, claimed it (order correctly flipped to SHIPPED with the real riderId set), the merchant's own attempt to self-declare SHIPPED at that point real-409s, the buyer's real-time rider-location check returned the exact real coordinates the rider had reported, the rider completed the delivery (order correctly flipped to DELIVERED), and the completed order correctly appeared in the rider's own `GET /orders/my-deliveries` history. **Real Coupang/Naver Smart Store-style seller reply to a product review added 2026-07-26**, closing the last leg of the "owner-side review replies" gap `MerchantBookingReview`'s own doc comment first named (booking reviews closed their half 2026-07-25; Eats closed its half earlier the same day as this -- see that row's own account). New `ProductReview.ownerReply`/`ownerRepliedAt` (migration V131) + `ProductReviewService.replyToProductReview` (`POST /api/v1/orders/reviews/{reviewId}/reply`): one editable reply per review, only the real merchant who owns the reviewed product can post it, the reviewing buyer gets a real `PRODUCT_REVIEW_REPLY` notification. 4 new unit tests. **bank-mfe UI updated the same day**: a product's review list now shows the seller's real reply indented beneath the review (`↳ Seller: ...`) when one exists. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a stranger's reply attempt real-404s; the real seller's reply appears on refetch; the buyer received the real notification with the correct title/body. **Real, genuinely cross-cutting gap found and fixed 2026-07-26**: `OrderService.placeOrder` never notified the merchant owner that a new order had arrived at all -- the only way to learn was manually polling `GET /orders/merchant-orders`. Every real e-commerce/food-delivery platform alerts the seller the instant an order lands; this was the honest baseline missing, not a named feature. Fixed with a real, best-effort `NEW_COMMERCE_ORDER` notification posted right after the order and its items are saved (same real "auxiliary side-effect can't block the real operation" discipline this codebase already establishes elsewhere). See the Eats row for the identical fix applied there the same day. 1 new unit test. Live-verified against the deployed cluster: a real order for 2 items produced the exact real notification ("A new order for 2 item(s) just came in -- 2000.00 RWF"). **Real push wired in, 2026-07-28** -- same real "fulfillment can't start until the merchant notices" urgency `MerchantService.collect`'s own payment-received push already established. Unit-verified (`:commerce:test`, 29/29 passing): the merchant owner's `pushNotificationService.sendToUser` is asserted alongside the pre-existing in-app `Notification`. **Real Coupang-style pre-purchase product Q&A (상품문의) added 2026-07-26**, sourced from Coupang's own real published feature (마이쿠팡 > 고객센터 > 상품문의내역), genuinely distinct from the existing `ProductReview` row above -- a review needs a real `DELIVERED` order (post-purchase, rating + comment); an inquiry needs no order or purchase at all (pre-purchase, question + answer), letting a shopper ask a real seller something before deciding to buy. New `ProductInquiry` entity (migration V133) + `ProductInquiryService`: `POST /api/v1/orders/products/{productId}/inquiries` (any authenticated user, no order check), a real `NEW_PRODUCT_INQUIRY` notification to the merchant owner, `POST /api/v1/orders/inquiries/{id}/answer` (merchant-owner-only, editable, no separate versioning) with a real `PRODUCT_INQUIRY_ANSWERED` notification back to the asker. Public read (`GET .../inquiries`), same as reviews. 6 new unit tests. Backend-only this pass (no bank-mfe UI yet), matching the same-day precedent this session already established for the Eats/booking owner-reply features. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a shopper asked a real question with zero order ever placed, the seller received the real notification, a stranger's answer attempt real-404s, the real seller's answer appears in the public Q&A and the shopper received the real answered notification. **bank-mfe UI wired the same day**: a real ask-a-question form + Q&A list on `ProductDetailView`, always visible regardless of purchase history (the whole point of an inquiry vs a review), showing "Awaiting seller response" for unanswered questions. Verified via clean build + a direct curl check confirming the response shape matches the new TS interface. Real Coupang-style Return & Exchange requests (`OrderReturnService`) got real mobile push notifications closed 2026-07-28 for both `requestReturn`'s COMMERCE_RETURN_REQUESTED (merchant needs to review, same urgency as items 79/83's new-order pushes) and `decide`'s COMMERCE_RETURN_DECIDED (buyer needs instant word of a refund or rejection). 16/16 `:commerce:test` passing (existing `OrderReturnServiceTest.kt`, 2 new push assertions). **Merchant Follow (`MerchantFollowService`), real Naver Smart Store-style "알림받기," got its first client on any platform 2026-07-28 (item 117), found via a content-grep sweep**: a mature, real backend (follow/unfollow a store, list your followed merchants, plus a merchant-facing follower count and a rate-limited broadcast-to-followers push) had a customer-facing client only on bank-mfe (`MerchantDetailView`'s Follow/Following toggle next to the store name) -- Android and iOS had zero references anywhere. Ported the identical Follow/Following toggle into Android's `MerchantDetailView` (`:features:shop:impl`, `ShopScreen.kt`, 3 new `ApiService.kt` endpoints/DTOs) and iOS's `MerchantDetailView` (`ShopScreen.swift`, 3 new `NetworkClient.swift` endpoints/DTOs). Verified via a genuinely clean, standalone `:features:shop:impl:compileDebugKotlin` (independent of the known unrelated `react-native-safe-area-context` blocker) and a real iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED`, zero errors on both. All three client platforms are now real for the customer-facing follow/unfollow/list-followed-merchants subset of this feature. **Merchant-facing half closed 2026-07-28 (item 118)**: a new "Followers" card on merchant-mfe's Settings screen shows the real follower count and a broadcast form (title/body), calling the existing `GET /api/v1/merchant/followers/count` and `POST /api/v1/merchant/followers/broadcast` endpoints -- disabled with an honest explanatory note when the count is 0, since `broadcastToFollowers` real-409s against the real anti-spam rate limit only after the fact. `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean. `MerchantFollowService` (both the customer-facing follow/unfollow and the merchant-facing follower-count/broadcast) is now real on every client it's relevant to -- bank-mfe/Android/iOS for follow, merchant-mfe for the owner-facing half. No Android/iOS merchant-owner UI for the broadcast half -- this codebase's merchant-facing tools (QR/POS/reports/payroll) have only ever lived on merchant-mfe/native MerchantApp, matching that existing precedent, not a new gap. **`MarketplaceEscrow`'s own real human-review dispute queue got its first admin client anywhere 2026-07-29 (item 125)**, found via a fresh endpoint-coverage sweep of ops-mfe's queues against the real backend `@RequestMapping` paths under `/api/v1/system`: `MarketplaceEscrowAdminController` (list pending disputes, resolve release-to-seller or refund-to-buyer) had zero client anywhere -- a real buyer-flagged dispute had no way for an admin to actually act on it at all.
