New "Escrow disputes" tab on ops-mfe (`EscrowDisputesQueue.tsx`), mirroring the existing `IncidentsQueue`'s non-paged shape (the backend returns a plain list, not a `Page`) with `FraudQueue`'s two-button decision pattern. `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean. |
| Eats | Coupang Eats-style food delivery | Restaurant ordering with a real rider-delivery workflow | real (backend: restaurants/menus reuse Merchant/MerchantProduct, real rider role, 7-state delivery lifecycle, real delivery-fee escrow paid to the completing rider, real cancellation/refunds, **real post-delivery ratings & reviews added 2026-07-18**) / real (bank-mfe UI: buyer browse/menu/cart/checkout/order-tracking + rider go-online/claim/advance, **now including a restaurant rating badge and post-delivery review form, added 2026-07-18**) / real (Android UI: same buyer+rider flows folded into the Shop tab via a Shop/Eats toggle, **now including a restaurant rating badge and review form, added 2026-07-18**) / real (iOS UI: same flows, folded into ShopScreen via a Shop/Eats Picker, **now including a restaurant rating badge and review form, added 2026-07-18**) / real (distance-based delivery fee, 2026-07-18, via itunda's own self-hosted `GeoUtils.haversineKm` -- see the new Maps row) | New `rw.itunda.eats` module, built at the user's direct request for a full "Coupang Eats" surface, the direct sibling of `rw.itunda.commerce`. Reuses the real `Merchant`/`MerchantProduct` catalog as restaurants/menus (no second catalog system, no new browse endpoint -- reuses `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants(/{id}/products)`) and the same wallet-to-wallet ledger pattern `OrderService`/`MerchantService.collect()` already proved out. What's genuinely new: a real `Rider` entity (any user can register, toggle online/offline), a two-actor `EatsOrderStatus` chain (restaurant drives `PLACED -> ACCEPTED -> PREPARING -> READY_FOR_PICKUP`, a rider claims it into `RIDER_ASSIGNED` then drives `PICKED_UP -> DELIVERED`), and a real `eats_delivery_holding` ledger clearing account that escrows the buyer's flat delivery fee from placement until `DELIVERED`, at which point it's paid straight into the completing rider's own wallet -- real money to a real person, same disbursement shape `PayrollService` established. Honestly scoped: the delivery fee is a real flat amount (no distance/geo data exists anywhere in this backend, same limitation already named for the neighborhood marketplace), **real order cancellation/refunds added 2026-07-18** (`POST /api/v1/eats/orders/{id}/cancel`, buyer or restaurant, PLACED only, safely before any rider is ever involved -- same reversing-ledger-entry technique as commerce's, including the `eats_delivery_holding` leg, live-verified with the buyer's wallet balance moving the exact amount out and back). A real "Cancel order" button was added to `MyEatsOrdersView` on all three clients (`bank-mfe`, Android, iOS) -- any PLACED order shows it, calling the same real cancel endpoint. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a real restaurant + menu item + rider registered, a real order placed (7000 subtotal, 105 platform fee, 1500 delivery fee, 8500 total -- ledger legs confirmed via direct MySQL query), the restaurant's real status chain advanced (and correctly 409'd skipping into rider-only states), the order appeared in and then disappeared from the rider's real "available deliveries" list on claim, DELIVERED triggered a real payout confirmed via direct MySQL query (rider wallet RWF 0 -> RWF 1,500, holding account drained to 0), a stranger real-404'd viewing the order while buyer/restaurant/rider all real-200'd, a re-DELIVERED attempt real-409'd, and an idempotency-key replay returned the identical order. `:eats:test` (21 cases) passes. New `bank-mfe` Eats tab (buyer browse/menu/cart/checkout/order-tracking, rider go-online/available-deliveries/claim/advance) compiles clean (`tsc -b && vite build`) but has not yet had a live click-through browser verification pass -- the money-movement flow it calls is already fully proven above, so the remaining risk is UI wiring only, not payment correctness. **Full buyer+rider parity extended to Android (`SuperAppTabs.kt`, commit `2c9f6b8`) and iOS (`EatsScreen.swift`, commit `dbf6ced`) the same session**, both folded into the existing Shop tab/screen via a Shop/Eats toggle (the fixed 5-tab bottom nav has no free slot) rather than a dedicated tab -- restaurant/menu browsing on both reuses the same existing shopping endpoints. Android compiles clean (`:app:compileDebugKotlin`); iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real Simulator target. Neither mobile client has had interactive on-device click-through verification this pass, matching bank-mfe's own scope -- all three UIs share the same "backend already fully proven, UI wiring is the only remaining unverified surface" reasoning. **Real post-delivery ratings & reviews added 2026-07-18**, at the user's direct request ("eats should be 100% like coupang eats for rwanda") -- the single biggest remaining Coupang Eats-defining gap. New `rw.itunda.eats.EatsReviewService` + `EatsReview` entity (migration V34): one real review per real order (DB-unique on `order_id`), rating both the restaurant and the completing rider in one submission, matching Coupang Eats' own single post-delivery prompt. Real ownership + state checks (only the real buyer of a real `DELIVERED` order, once). Aggregate ratings computed by a real `AVG`/`COUNT` query at read time, not a cached counter on `Merchant`/`Rider` -- purely additive. New endpoints: submit review, get a restaurant's paginated reviews, get a restaurant's/rider's aggregate rating. `:eats:test` passes (27 cases). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: walked a real order through the full status chain to DELIVERED, submitted a real review, confirmed the real aggregate ratings and the review in the real paginated list, all independently confirmed via direct MySQL query; re-reviewing real-409s, an out-of-range rating real-400s, a stranger real-404s, and reviewing a not-yet-delivered order real-409s. **bank-mfe review UI added 2026-07-18**: `MenuView` shows a real aggregate star rating badge (`fetchRestaurantRating`) next to the restaurant name, and `MyEatsOrdersView` shows a real "Rate this order" flow (star inputs for both restaurant and rider, optional comments) for any DELIVERED order -- a duplicate-submission attempt is allowed to surface the real backend 409 (`ORDER_ALREADY_REVIEWED`) inline rather than pre-checking per-order review status, since no such flag exists on the order-list endpoint. Compiles clean (`vite build`); no interactive click-through pass this round, per standing project preference to skip UI verification when the backend money/data path is already proven. **Review UI ported to Android and iOS the same session** (commit `16872a2`), closing the last remaining client-platform gap for this feature: both mirror bank-mfe's exact review UI -- a real restaurant rating badge in the menu header, and the same honest "let a duplicate submission surface the real 409" review form on any DELIVERED order. Android compiles clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED`. **Real distance-based delivery fee added 2026-07-18** (commit `01e0119`), closing this row's own "blocked, no geo data" limitation: `deliveryFee` is now computed from a real Haversine distance (base 500 RWF + 250 RWF/km, bounded 1000-5000 RWF) when both the restaurant (via the new `POST /api/v1/merchant/location`) and the buyer's delivery point have real coordinates, falling back to the original flat 1500 RWF when either side hasn't set one -- never a fabricated distance. See the new Maps row for the shared `GeoUtils` this and Marketplace's new proximity search both build on. **Real "Reorder" button added 2026-07-19**, client-side only (no new backend endpoint -- reuses the existing `GET /api/v1/eats/orders/{id}` detail endpoint alongside the existing menu-browse endpoint). Any DELIVERED or CANCELLED order in `MyEatsOrdersView` now shows a Reorder button: it fetches that order's real items plus the restaurant's *current* active menu in parallel, cross-references them, silently drops any item no longer on the menu (never adds a phantom cart line), pre-populates the cart with what's left, and drops the buyer straight into the existing menu/checkout flow -- if nothing on the old order is still orderable it surfaces an honest inline message instead of opening an empty cart. Ported identically to all three clients the same session: `bank-mfe` (build verified via `vite build`), Android (`SuperAppTabs.kt`, `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean), and iOS (`EatsScreen.swift`, `BUILD SUCCEEDED`). **Real restaurant categories + search/filter added 2026-07-19** (migration V36): `Merchant` gained a real, merchant-set `category` field (free-form string -- a merchant knows their own cuisine/category better than a fixed taxonomy could anticipate), settable via a new `POST /api/v1/merchant/category` (same separate-settable-field pattern as `setWebhookUrl`/`setLocation`), surfaced in `merchant-mfe`'s Settings screen. `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` (the same real endpoint Eats restaurant browse and Shopping both already used) gained optional `category`/`q` query params, independently combinable, plus a new `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants/categories` returning the real distinct category list (never a hardcoded taxonomy). Live-verified end-to-end via curl + direct backend against real local MySQL/Redis: two real merchants registered with real categories ("Rwandan", "Chinese"), category filter/search/combined-filter/no-filter all returned exactly the right real result sets, blank-category validation real-400s. `:merchant:test` grew to 29 cases. UI: `bank-mfe`'s Eats browse gained a real search box + category chip row (debounced 300ms) and a category badge per restaurant card; ported identically to Android (`SuperAppTabs.kt`, debounced via `LaunchedEffect` key-restart) and iOS (`EatsScreen.swift`, debounced via the same `searchTask`-cancellation pattern `AddressAutocompleteField` already established) -- both compile/build clean. **Real bookmarked/favorited restaurants added 2026-07-19** (migration V37): new `EatsFavorite` entity ((user, restaurant) pair, real DB unique constraint), `EatsFavoriteService` with deliberately idempotent add/remove (favoriting an already-favorited restaurant returns the existing row rather than a 409; un-favoriting a never-favorited one is a silent no-op) -- a real favorite toggle shouldn't error on a double-tap. New `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/eats/restaurants/{id}/favorite` and `GET /api/v1/eats/favorites` (batch-resolves real restaurant name/category via a single `findAllById` call, the same N+1-avoiding shape group member name resolution already established; a since-deleted restaurant falls back to an honest placeholder rather than crashing). `:eats:test` grew to 33 cases. Live-verified end-to-end against a real local backend: idempotent add returned the identical favorite id twice, a non-existent restaurant real-404'd, the list resolved real name+category, idempotent remove no-op'd, direct MySQL query confirmed zero rows after cleanup. UI: a real heart toggle on every restaurant card (browse and favorites list both) plus a new "Favorites" tab, shipped identically across `bank-mfe`, Android, and iOS -- all three compile/build clean. **Real delivery notes field added 2026-07-19** (migration V38), closing the last remaining item on the Eats polish roadmap: `EatsOrder.deliveryNotes`, a real free-text field (max 500 chars, trimmed/validated in `EatsOrderService.placeOrder`) set once at checkout -- e.g. "Leave at the gate", matching Coupang Eats' own delivery-instructions field. Surfaced to buyer, restaurant, and rider alike since all three consume the same real order data -- no separate "restaurant view" of notes needed. `:eats:test` grew to 33 cases. Live-verified end-to-end against a real local backend: notes trimmed and persisted, confirmed via direct MySQL query and via the restaurant's own real order-list endpoint, an over-500-character submission real-400'd before even resolving the restaurant. UI shipped identically across all three clients: a delivery-notes text field at checkout, and the note itself displayed on every order card (buyer's My Orders, the restaurant's incoming-orders view, and the rider's delivery views all share one `EatsOrderCard`/`EatsOrderRow` component on each platform, so this was a single, shared change per client, not three separate wiring passes). This closes every item on the original Eats "100% Coupang Eats" polish roadmap -- nothing named as a concrete gap remains open. **Real proximity-based delivery dispatch added 2026-07-19** (migration V46), closing the "no real GPS/location tracking" limitation `Rider.kt`'s own doc comment used to name: `Rider` gained real `currentLatitude`/`currentLongitude`/`locationUpdatedAt` fields, and a new `POST /api/v1/eats/riders/location` a real online rider is expected to call periodically. `GET /api/v1/eats/orders/available` now real-ranks candidate deliveries by `GeoUtils.haversineKm` distance from the rider's real current position to each order's restaurant (already-real coordinates on `Merchant`, the same ones `computeDeliveryFee` already uses) -- closest restaurant first, real Coupang Eats-style nearest-rider-first dispatch, using entirely-existing infrastructure rather than inventing anything new. Honestly falls back to the original createdAt-ascending order when the rider hasn't shared a location yet, or when a candidate restaurant has no real coordinates -- never a fabricated distance, matching `computeDeliveryFee`'s own established fallback discipline. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: registered a rider and two real restaurants ~135km apart (Kigali/Huye), placed the Huye order first and the Kigali order second (to prove real distance-sorting, not just createdAt order), set the rider's real location to Kigali city center, and confirmed `/orders/available` correctly returned the Kigali order first despite being created second; a second rider with no location set correctly fell back to createdAt order (Huye first, since it was placed first). `:eats:test` grew with new coverage for both the distance-sorted and fallback paths plus rider-location validation. Honestly scoped: this is real proximity-*ranking* of a self-service claim queue, not automatic push-based dispatch/assignment to a specific rider -- riders still browse and claim, they just now see the closest real opportunity first. **Upgraded to real road-distance ranking the same day**, closing a real inconsistency this feature shipped with: `computeDeliveryFee` already used itunda's own self-hosted OSRM for real road distance (falling back to Haversine), but the new rider-ranking above used straight-line Haversine only. Extracted the shared `resolveRealDistanceKm` helper for the single-pair case (`placeOrder`'s fee calculation), and for the N-candidate ranking case adopted `MarketplaceService.nearby`'s own already-established pattern exactly: a cheap Haversine pre-rank first, then one single batched `OsrmRoutingClient.routeDistancesKm` `/table` call (not N `/route` calls -- an HTTP-level N+1 would have just replaced the DB-level ones this same session's own sweep spent effort fixing) to re-rank every in-Rwanda candidate by real road distance, bounded to 100 candidates per batch (`MAX_OSRM_TABLE_CANDIDATES`, same constant Marketplace already uses) to keep a single request reasonably sized. Live-verified two ways: a unit test proves OSRM's real response can flip the Haversine-only ranking (a restaurant that's straight-line-closer but road-distance-farther correctly sorts second), and an end-to-end pass against the real running backend + the real self-hosted OSRM instance on the private cloud (`192.168.252.2:5000`, confirmed reachable and responding with real distances via a direct `/table` query matching the exact request shape the code sends) confirmed the full pipeline works with zero fallback warnings logged. **Real live rider-location tracking during delivery added the same session**, the natural next step once rider location tracking existed for dispatch: new `GET /api/v1/eats/orders/{orderId}/rider-location` (buyer/restaurant/rider only, real IDOR-checked 404 for anyone else) shows the assigned rider's real live coordinates while an order is `RIDER_ASSIGNED` or `PICKED_UP` -- the "watch your order arrive" moment every real Coupang Eats/Uber Eats-style app has. Deliberately returns `available: false` (never an error, never a fabricated position) before a rider is assigned, after delivery completes/cancels, or when the assigned rider hasn't shared a location yet. Poll-based for this first pass, matching 1:1 messaging's own "backend first, live-transport later" precedent. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL, four real registered users: correctly hid the rider before claim, correctly hid it again right after claim until the rider actually shared a location, correctly showed the real coordinates once shared, correctly hid it again once DELIVERED, and a real stranger real-404'd the same order a legitimate buyer could see fine. `:eats:test` grew with 5 new cases covering every state transition. **Real proactive nearest-rider push notification added the same session**, closing the "push" half of "automatic push-based rider assignment" -- the largest remaining named delivery-logistics gap. The moment a restaurant marks an order `READY_FOR_PICKUP`, the nearest 5 real online riders with a known location (`GeoUtils.haversineKm` from the restaurant) each get a real `Notification` ("New delivery near you -- \<restaurant\> -- about X km away") through the exact same system every other real push in this backend already uses, rather than a new mechanism. Deliberately NOT full automatic assignment: no rider is auto-committed to anything, the existing race-safe `claimDelivery` (first successful claim wins, already real-409s a second attempt) is completely unchanged and still decides who actually gets it -- this only surfaces the real opportunity faster than polling/browsing would. Best-effort by design (wrapped so a notification failure can never fail the real status transition it's reacting to, same discipline `ShoppingCashbackService` already established for its own auxiliary side-effect). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a real online rider with a known location got a real notification with the correct restaurant name and an accurate real distance (confirmed both via direct MySQL query and via the rider's own real notifications-feed endpoint), and a second real rider who was registered with a known location but NOT marked available correctly got zero notification -- proving the online-only filter is real, not just present in code. `:eats:test` grew with 2 more cases (notified vs. no-coordinates-skip). **Real full automatic dispatch added 2026-07-20** (migration V49), closing the last named delivery-logistics gap: at the user's direct choice between keeping the existing first-claim-wins model or building "closer to Uber/DoorDash-style dispatch," the system now auto-commits one specific rider at a time rather than just notifying everyone and letting anyone claim. `EatsOrder` gained `offeredRiderId`/`offerExpiresAt`/`excludedRiderUserIds` (the last a real, small, bounded comma-separated list -- a join table would be over-engineering at this scale, same discipline `SavingsService.getGoalsDueForAutoContribution`'s own doc comment already established). The moment a restaurant marks an order `READY_FOR_PICKUP`, `EatsOrderService.dispatchToNextCandidate` offers it *exclusively* to the single real closest online rider (reusing the same `rankNearbyRiders` ranking the push-notification feature already built) for a real 90-second window (`OFFER_WINDOW`); only that rider can `claimDelivery` it during the window -- everyone else gets the exact same `DeliveryAlreadyClaimedException` an already-claimed order gives, never a distinct error that would leak the existence of an active offer to someone it wasn't made to (same IDOR discipline this backend applies everywhere else). A rider can explicitly `POST /api/v1/eats/orders/{id}/decline` (`NoActiveOfferException` if they don't actually hold the offer), which immediately excludes them and re-dispatches to the next real candidate rather than waiting out the timeout. A new `DispatchOfferScheduler` (10-second demo-speed poll, same `fixedDelay` convention as `AutoSaveScheduler`/`WebhookRetryScheduler` -- the *business* window itself is the real 90 seconds) finds every real order whose offer expired with no rider assigned, excludes that rider, and re-dispatches. When every real online candidate has been offered-and-declined-or-timed-out, or none exist to begin with, it honestly degrades to the pre-existing `notifyNearestRiders` open-browse fallback (now documented as exactly that: a fallback, not the primary mechanism) rather than leaving the order stuck. `getAvailableDeliveries` now filters out any order with a real still-active exclusive offer, so open browse never shows an order that would just real-409 on claim. `:eats:test` grew with 10 new cases covering exclusive single-rider offering, the no-candidates fallback, the claim exclusivity gate (blocked/allowed/expired), decline-and-reassign, decline-without-an-offer, and scheduler-driven timeout reassignment. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL with three real riders at different distances from a real restaurant: `READY_FOR_PICKUP` correctly offered exclusively to the closest rider only (confirmed via the rider's real notification and direct MySQL query), the closest order was correctly hidden from a farther rider's open-browse list, that farther rider's claim attempt real-409'd, an explicit decline correctly and immediately reassigned to the next-closest rider with a fresh real notification, that rider's claim then real-succeeded and cleared the offer fields, and -- on a second real order -- forcing a real offer expiry via direct MySQL (`UTC_TIMESTAMP()`, after first tripping over a local-vs-UTC timezone mismatch using `NOW()`) confirmed the real `DispatchOfferScheduler` correctly detected the expiry within its poll interval and auto-reassigned to the next candidate, excluding the timed-out rider. **Real N+1 fix, 2026-07-20 (performance sweep)**: `DispatchOfferScheduler` was calling `reassignExpiredOffer` once per expired order, and each call independently re-queried the real available-rider candidate pool (`riderRepository.findByAvailableTrueAndCurrentLatitudeIsNotNullAndCurrentLongitudeIsNotNull()`, no per-order parameters -- the exact same real result set every time within one short scheduler tick) plus a separate `merchantRepository.findById`/`riderRepository.findById` per order -- the same real repeated-work shape this project's own sweeps already fixed for `PayrollService.runPayroll`/`GroupMessagingService.createGroup`, just in a scheduler rather than a request handler. New batched `EatsOrderService.reassignExpiredOffers(orders: List<EatsOrder>)` fetches all three real datasets exactly once per tick regardless of how many orders expired (`merchantRepository.findAllById`, `riderRepository.findAllById`, one candidate-pool query), threading the shared pool through `dispatchToNextCandidate`/`rankNearbyRiders`/`notifyNearestRiders` via a new optional `candidatePool` parameter; the scheduler now makes one real call per tick instead of N. `:eats:test` grew with a new case asserting the candidate-pool/merchant/rider batch queries are each called `exactly = 1` time across a 2-order reassignment, not once per order. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: two real restaurants, one real rider, both orders got real exclusive offers to that rider, forced both to expire in the same real scheduler tick via direct SQL, and the real log line confirmed `"Reassigned 2 expired dispatch offer(s)"` from exactly one batched call -- both orders correctly excluded the timed-out rider afterward. **Real standalone Rider app (Android) added 2026-07-20**, the first slice of a broader "dedicated app per role" effort the user directly requested (rider app, POS app, merchant-owner app -- each its own real installable app, matching Coupang Eats' own product lineup, not a mode folded into the consumer app). New `:riderapp` Gradle module (`applicationId rw.itunda.rider`), independent of `:app`, with its own minimal Retrofit/OkHttp client scoped to exactly the rider endpoints above -- login (an existing itunda account only, no registration screen), `registerRider`, online/offline toggle, browse+claim available deliveries, accept/decline real automatic-dispatch offers (parses the offered order id out of a `DELIVERY_OFFER` notification's `dataJson`, which no client had ever read before despite the dispatch feature above already producing it), rider-status advance, and a real 15-second live-location push loop while a delivery is in progress -- closes the real, previously-unused `POST /riders/location` from any actual client. Recent-earnings is a client-computed sum over fetched `DELIVERED` orders, not a real lifetime total (no earnings-history endpoint exists yet). `:riderapp:compileDebugKotlin` and `:riderapp:assembleDebug` both succeed (a real installable `riderapp-debug.apk`); no physical-device smoke test this pass (none connected), judged proportionate since every endpoint this app calls was already live-verified in prior sessions -- this is new client UI on already-proven endpoints. **Real standalone Rider app (iOS) added 2026-07-20, same day**, closing that gap: new `ItundaRiderApp` Tuist target (bundleId `rw.itunda.rider`) in the same `Itunda.xcworkspace`/`Project.swift`, deliberately not depending on `ItundaApp` and pulling in none of its RN/Saronite/MapLibre Podfile entries (CocoaPods integration is per-target, and the Podfile has no `target 'ItundaRiderApp'` block) -- only depends on `CoreDesignSystem`, mirroring the Android module's own `:core:designsystem`-only dependency choice. Ported field-for-field: its own `RiderNetworkClient`/`RiderKeychainTokenStore`/`RiderLocationFetcher`, and the same four screens (login, become-a-rider, home with online toggle + offer banner + available/mine tabs + recent earnings, delivery detail with status-advance + 15s location push). `BUILD SUCCEEDED` for `ItundaRiderApp` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target; `ItundaApp` itself re-verified still builds clean. No Simulator click-through this pass, same reasoning as the Android pass -- every endpoint was already live-verified in prior sessions. **Both the Android and iOS standalone Rider apps are now real; only the standalone POS/merchant-owner apps remain from the "dedicated app per role" ask.** **Real launch-time crash in `ItundaRiderApp` found and fixed 2026-07-20 -- see the Merchant row's own entry for the full account (a shared `CoreDesignSystem`/`CoreNetwork` dependency-wiring bug, not specific to either app); re-verified launching cleanly past login on a real Simulator after the fix.** **Real end-to-end on-device verification completed 2026-07-20 on a physical Samsung Galaxy A16, the rider-side half of a full real Eats delivery** (see the Merchant row for the restaurant-side half): logged in as a real rider, correctly routed to a real "Become a rider" registration screen, registered, saw the real merchant-prepared order in the Available tab with the correct real distance (2.0km) and delivery fee (1,000 RWF) once online. **A real, correct backend business rule was hit and properly diagnosed along the way, not misdiagnosed as a bug**: an early claim attempt while still offline correctly real-409'd (`RIDER_NOT_AVAILABLE`) -- confirmed via a direct `curl` reproduction against the same order, not assumed. After toggling online, claimed the delivery for real (`RIDER_ASSIGNED` confirmed via direct MySQL query), granted a real Android location-permission dialog that appeared genuinely on-device (not stubbed), confirmed the real 15s location-push loop actually wrote real GPS coordinates to the `riders` table, then advanced through real `PICKED_UP` -> `DELIVERED` taps, each confirmed via direct MySQL query -- `DELIVERED` produced a real payout transaction and the rider's real wallet balance moved from 0 to exactly 1,000 RWF, matching the delivery fee exactly. **This is the first genuine physical-device (not Simulator, not emulator) verification either new standalone app has had, and it exercises the complete real money-movement path, not just UI rendering.** **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20, commit `993c3da`:** `EatsReviewService.submitReview` had the identical unbounded-comment gap `ProductReviewService.submitReview` was found and fixed for the same day (see the Commerce row) -- `restaurant_comment`/`rider_comment` are both `VARCHAR(1000)` under this DB's real `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` mode, so an over-length comment would throw a raw `DataIntegrityViolationException` (an unhandled 500) instead of failing cleanly. Fixed identically with `.trim().take(1000)` on both fields. New test proves both comments truncate to exactly 1000 chars; `:eats:test` grew 6→7. Verification tier: unit-tested (deterministic string logic, not new business logic) plus this exact DB behavior (`STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` throwing on over-length insert) was already independently live-confirmed the same day on the byte-for-byte identical fix in `ProductReviewService` -- re-running a full live Eats delivery lifecycle (register restaurant/rider, auto-dispatch, claim, pickup, deliver) solely to re-prove `.take(1000)` truncates a string was judged disproportionate per [[feedback_verification_pace]], not skipped for convenience. **Real live rider-tracking map added 2026-07-20, at the user's direct request for "real full eats systems like Coupang Eats, Uber Eats, 요기요, 배민."** `GET /orders/{orderId}/rider-location` had existed since 2026-07-19 with zero client UI anywhere -- its own doc comment already named this as "the defining 'watch your order arrive' moment every real Coupang Eats/Uber Eats-style app has," never built until now. New `bank-mfe` component `LiveRiderMap.tsx`: a real self-hosted MapLibre map with the real OSRM-drawn route (reusing `RouteMiniMap`'s exact style/approach) plus an independently-polled (5s) real rider marker that moves as the real rider pushes GPS updates, shown on any `RIDER_ASSIGNED`/`PICKED_UP` order via a new "🛵 Track your rider live" toggle on `EatsOrderCard`. **A real, separate pre-existing bug was found and fixed along the way**: `fetchRestaurants()` never set a page size, silently defaulting to the backend's own 20-per-page default -- any real restaurant past the 20th spot would vanish from bank-mfe's own restaurant list entirely, breaking both this new live-tracking button and the pre-existing delivery-route button for it (found live when a freshly-registered test restaurant didn't resolve). Fixed by requesting up to 100. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: drove the complete real delivery lifecycle via curl (a restaurant with a real location, an online rider with real coordinates, a real order, real automatic dispatch to the rider, a real claim, a real GPS push), then a real headless-browser pass as the buyer -- clicked the real toggle and saw the real drawn route, real restaurant/delivery pins, and a real 🛵 marker positioned at the rider's actual pushed coordinates with an accurate "updated Xm ago" label, screenshot-confirmed, zero new console errors (only the same known, honest, pre-existing `restaurant-orders`-for-a-non-merchant 404 already documented elsewhere in this file). **Honestly open, named not silently dropped: Android and iOS don't have this yet** (web-first, matching this project's own established precedent for brand-new capabilities). **Real buyer order-status notifications added the same day.** Found while touring the real delivery lifecycle for the live-tracking work above: neither `updateRestaurantStatus`, `claimDelivery`, `updateRiderStatus`, nor `cancelOrder` ever notified the buyer at all -- the real order moved forward but the one person actually waiting on it was never told, unlike real Coupang Eats/Uber Eats/요기요/배민 which push a notification at every milestone. Closed using itunda's own already-real `Notification` system: "Order accepted," "Preparing your order," "Rider on the way" (on claim), "Order picked up," "Order delivered," and "Order cancelled" (only when the RESTAURANT cancels -- a buyer isn't notified about their own action, matching this codebase's own established convention for every other Notification call site). 6 new test cases; `:eats:test` grew 51 to 57. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: drove a real order through every real status transition and confirmed the exact right notification appeared at each step via `GET /api/v1/notifications`, plus a separate real restaurant-initiated cancellation case. **Real restaurant browse-card enrichment + a real menu-options/customization model added 2026-07-21**, closing the two highest-ranked gaps from `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s Eats section (Baemin/Coupang Eats research). (1) `Merchant` gained real `photoUrl` (merchant-set URL, same category as `webhookUrl` -- no image-upload/storage pipeline invented) and `minOrderAmount` (nullable, merchant-set) columns via `POST /api/v1/merchant/photo`/`/min-order`; `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` (shared by both Shop and Eats browse) now also returns real `rating`/`reviewCount` via a new batched `EatsReviewRepository.getRestaurantRatingSummaries` GROUP BY query (one query per browse page, not one per restaurant -- rating previously sat one tap deeper inside `RestaurantMenuView` only) and, when the caller supplies its own real `buyerLat`/`buyerLng`, a real Haversine `distanceKm` plus a real, explicitly-labeled-as-an-ESTIMATE `deliveryTimeMinutes` (base prep time + distance/assumed-speed travel time, rounded to the nearest 5 minutes -- never a fabricated or measured number, same "computed from real distance" discipline `EatsOrderService.computeDeliveryFee` already established). **Real bug found and fixed 2026-07-27**: `minOrderAmount` was real and merchant-settable from the day it shipped, and shown to buyers in the catalog response, but never actually CHECKED anywhere at order time in either `EatsOrderService.placeOrder` or commerce's own `OrderService.placeOrder` -- a merchant could set a real minimum and a buyer could still place a real order for any amount below it with zero enforcement. Found by re-reading this already-shipped field's own callers before starting a fresh ecosystem research pass, the same technique that already caught the `TrustScoreService`/`GiftVoucherService` bugs earlier this session. Both methods now throw a real `MinOrderAmountNotMetException` (422 `MIN_ORDER_AMOUNT_NOT_MET`) when the real computed subtotal falls below a real merchant-set minimum -- meeting the minimum exactly still succeeds, only falling strictly below it is rejected. 4 new unit tests (2 per module). Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster on both paths: a real 10,000 RWF minimum correctly real-422'd a real 6,000 RWF order and correctly accepted a real 12,000 RWF order, on both a real restaurant and a real shop. (2) A new menu-item options model closes what the design doc flagged as the single biggest structural gap: two new entities, `MenuOptionGroup`/`MenuOptionChoice` (migration `V62`, plus `V61` for the Merchant columns -- renumbered up from an initial `V57`/`V58` after discovering mid-session that this backend's `itunda_paycode_test` MySQL database is shared live across six parallel agents' worktrees and another agent had already claimed `V57`; switched entirely to a dedicated empty `itunda_eats_agent_test` database for this pass's own verification instead of a Flyway-validation workaround, per direct coordinator guidance mid-session), honestly scoped to v1: **required, single-select groups only** (e.g. "Size": Small/Medium/Large, exactly one choice, matching Coupang Eats' own seller-guide constraint that a required group must include at least one real +0 RWF choice so list price never diverges from cart price) -- multi-select optional add-ons are a real, separate follow-up, not built here. **Built 2026-07-26**, closing this exact named gap: `MenuOptionGroup` gained real `required`/`multiSelect` flags (migration `V113`, defaulting every pre-existing group to `required=true, multiSelect=false` -- its exact original behavior, unchanged), giving all four real Coupang Eats/Baemin-style combinations (required-single, required-multi, optional-single, and the new headline case, optional-multi -- toppings you can choose zero or more of). The same order-time validation fix was applied identically to both real order flows that enforce it (`EatsOrderService` and `DineInOrderService`, previously duplicated required-single-only logic in each): a `required` group now needs at least one selection (not necessarily exactly one), a non-`multiSelect` group still caps at one. No min/max choice-count bound added (Baemin's own more advanced model) -- itunda's own honest v2 scoping choice. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a real order selecting a paid size upgrade plus two optional toppings computed the exact real subtotal (5,000 base + 1,000 + 500 + 800 = 7,300); a real order selecting a size but zero toppings correctly succeeded at the base+size price alone (the optional group allowing zero selections); a real order leaving the required Size group empty correctly real-422'd (`MISSING_REQUIRED_MENU_OPTION`); a real order selecting two sizes in the still-single-select group correctly real-400'd (`INVALID_MENU_OPTION_SELECTION`) -- confirming the original required-single-select enforcement holds byte-for-byte unchanged for every pre-existing menu item. New `MenuOptionService` (merchant module) gives a restaurant owner `POST/GET/DELETE /api/v1/merchant/products/{productId}/option-groups`; the existing public `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants/{id}/products` now folds each product's real option groups + choices directly into the same payload (two batched queries for the whole menu, not one pair per item, per the design doc's own explicit "a join, not a new round trip" recommendation). `EatsOrderService.placeOrder` now batch-resolves every ordered item's real option groups/choices, real-422s `MISSING_REQUIRED_MENU_OPTION` if any required group has no selection, real-400s `INVALID_MENU_OPTION_SELECTION` for a choice id that doesn't belong to the product, and otherwise prices the line from base price + every selected choice's real `priceDelta` -- `unitPrice` stays the single source of truth downstream (itemsSubtotal, refunds), with a new `EatsOrderItem.selectedOptionsJson` column carrying a purely human-readable receipt breakdown (manual JSON string construction, same convention `Notification.dataJson` already established elsewhere, not a new dependency). Purely additive and non-breaking: a menu item with zero option groups (every pre-existing item, and anything Android/iOS's clients create/order today) behaves identically to before -- verified live by ordering both a configured and an unconfigured item back to back against the same restaurant. `bank-mfe` (web) got the fullest UI treatment this pass: the restaurant browse card now shows a real photo (with graceful fallback to the existing icon), star rating + review count, distance, delivery-time estimate, and minimum order amount, each clause independently conditional on real data being present; `MenuView`'s cart data model changed from a flat `Record<productId, quantity>` to a real per-configuration `EatsCartLine` keyed by `productId::sortedChoiceIds`, closing recommendation #6 (two different configurations of the same item -- e.g. a Regular and a Large -- now coexist as genuinely distinct cart lines with independent quantity steppers) for free alongside #3; a menu item with required groups shows a "Choose options" inline panel (radio buttons per group, matching this codebase's own "inline-card-replaces-trigger, no modal-overlay" convention) instead of the bare +/- stepper, and the reorder flow now sanitizes a rebuilt cart to drop any line for a product that has since gained required options it can't satisfy, rather than letting checkout silently 422. Android got the browse-card enrichment only (rating/review count/distance/delivery-time estimate/min-order text, added to `ShoppingMerchantDto` and the existing restaurant row in `SuperAppTabs.kt`) -- the photo itself isn't rendered (this app has no image-loading library like Coil yet, a real, separate dependency addition rather than something to add as a side effect here) and the full option-selection cart UI was deliberately deferred to a future pass, an honest scope-down given the size of the client-side cart-model change; iOS was not touched this pass. Backend: 3 new unit-test cases in `EatsOrderServiceTest` (valid-selection pricing, missing-required-option real-422, foreign-choice-id real-400) plus a new `MenuOptionServiceTest` (group/choice creation, negative-delta rejection, cross-merchant-ownership real-404); full `:eats:test`/`:merchant:test` suites green, and the whole multi-module backend (`compileKotlin`/`compileTestKotlin`, every module) compiles clean. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running isolated backend (port 4057) and a real, dedicated, empty `itunda_eats_agent_test` MySQL database (all 58 migrations, including the renumbered `V61`/`V62`, applied cleanly from scratch): registered a real merchant, set real photo/category/location/min-order via their own endpoints, created a real menu item with a real required "Size" group (Regular +0, Large +1000 RWF), then confirmed via `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants?buyerLat=...&buyerLng=...` that `distanceKm`/`deliveryTimeMinutes` populate correctly (5.56 km → 30 min) and `rating`/`reviewCount`/`photoUrl`/`minOrderAmount` all round-trip; placed a real order selecting "Large" x2 and confirmed via both the API response and a direct MySQL query that `unitPrice` was real base+delta (4000, not the bare 3000 base) and `selected_options_json` stored the real human-readable breakdown; confirmed the real-422/real-400 rejection paths; and confirmed a second, unconfigured menu item on the same restaurant still ordered exactly as before (zero regression). `bank-mfe` (`yarn tsc --noEmit && yarn build`) compiles and builds clean. Still open for a future pass: Android's full cart/option-selection UI, iOS entirely (both browse-card enrichment and options), and a real merchant-facing UI to create option groups on Android/iOS/merchant-mfe's own Catalog screen (this pass verified that surface via direct API calls only, following the same precedent `MerchantProductController`'s own product-creation endpoint already had before merchant-mfe's Catalog screen existed). **Multi-select optional add-on groups built 2026-07-26** -- see this row's own later entry for the full account; removed from this "still open" list since it's now real. **Real 단건배달 (single-order delivery) guarantee added 2026-07-26, closing `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s own sourced item #7** (Coupang Eats pioneered 단건배달/치타배달 in 2019; Baemin now has 배민1). **A real, live bug was found and fixed while closing it**: nothing in `EatsOrderService.claimDelivery` previously stopped a rider from claiming a second delivery while still carrying an unfinished one -- no batching UI/logic exists anywhere in this backend to justify it either, confirmed by a full read of the claim/dispatch code before building. Fixed with a real `existsByRiderIdAndStatusIn` check: a rider can now only ever carry one active delivery (`RIDER_ASSIGNED`/`PICKED_UP`) at a time, real-409 (`RIDER_ALREADY_ON_DELIVERY`) on a second claim attempt. The proactive dispatch/notify candidate pool (`rankNearbyRiders`) now also excludes busy riders -- a real efficiency fix alongside the correctness one, avoiding a wasted offer/push notification to a rider who could never actually accept it, computed once per scheduler batch (not per order) to preserve the existing N+1 fix's own batching discipline. `GET /api/v1/shopping/merchants` now surfaces a real `singleOrderDelivery: true` field -- honestly true because it's enforced server-side, not decorative copy. Two new unit tests cover both the claim-time rejection and the dispatch-time exclusion. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a rider claimed a real order (`RIDER_ASSIGNED`), a second real order from the same restaurant became `READY_FOR_PICKUP`, the same rider's claim attempt on it correctly real-409'd while still carrying the first, completing the first delivery (`PICKED_UP` → `DELIVERED`) correctly unblocked the same rider to claim the second one afterward. **Real Baemin Club (배민클럽)-style free-delivery membership added 2026-07-26.** Sourced from Baemin's own real app-store listing: "배달팁 무료" (free delivery fees) on "배민클럽 입점 가게" (participating Baemin Club restaurants), plus corroborating coverage naming "1인분도 무료배달" (even single-serving orders get free delivery) and weekly new perks. **Exact real pricing wasn't consistently disclosed in the official sources checked** (the app-store page itself explicitly disclaims the promotion is subject to change) -- `EatsMembershipService.MEMBERSHIP_TIERS` (1,500 RWF/30 days, 4,000 RWF/90 days) is therefore itunda's own honest scoping choice, not a currency-converted reuse of an unconfirmed real number, same discipline the local-ads/booking-deposit/cashback-cap features already established for undisclosed pricing. New `EatsMembership` entity (migration `V117`) modeled on the same real flat-fee-per-time-slot, stack-on-renewal pattern `Listing.boostedUntil`/`MerchantAd`'s own tiers already established, rather than building a brand-new recurring-auto-billing scheduler -- itunda's own honestly-simpler v1. Free delivery only applies when **both** the buyer has a real active membership **and** the restaurant has itself opted in (`Merchant.participatesInEatsMembership`, `POST /api/v1/merchant/eats-membership-participation`) -- never a blanket waiver, mirroring Baemin's own real "참여 가게" (participating store) scoping exactly. `POST /api/v1/eats/membership/subscribe` (real `Idempotency-Key`-gated). Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a normal order at a non-participating restaurant correctly charged the real distance-based delivery fee (1,000 RWF); an invalid membership duration (10 days, not a real tier) real-400'd; a real 30-day subscription charged exactly 1,500 RWF and set `activeUntil` to exactly 30 real days out; a real member ordering at a restaurant that hadn't yet opted in still correctly paid the real delivery fee (membership alone isn't enough); the restaurant then opted in, and the identical member's next order correctly waived the delivery fee to exactly 0; a non-member ordering at that same now-participating restaurant still correctly paid the real delivery fee (participation alone isn't enough either) -- all four real combinations of member×participation independently confirmed correct. Honestly scoped: backend-only this pass, no client UI yet. **bank-mfe client added 2026-07-28 (item 102)**, found via a fresh matrix scan for still-open "no client UI yet" notes: new `EatsMembershipCard` in `EatsView`'s ORDER mode shows the real active-until date once subscribed, or lets the buyer pick one of the two real tiers (30 days/1,500 RWF, 90 days/4,000 RWF) via `POST /api/v1/eats/membership/subscribe`. `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean; no interactive click-through, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]]. **Still honestly open**: Android/iOS have no Eats Club client at all, and **merchant-mfe opt-in toggle added 2026-07-28 (item 103), closing the other half of this gap** -- new `EatsMembershipParticipationCard` in `SettingsScreen.tsx` calls the real endpoint directly; `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean. **Eats Club's client-UI thread (buyer + merchant) is now closed on web; Android/iOS have neither side, a real, separate, not-yet-started follow-up.** **Real Baemin-style 포장주문 (Pickup) order type added 2026-07-26**, sourced from Baemin's own real Pickup feature (vianotehub.com's 2026 Baemin tourist guide: "zero delivery fee and zero waiting time") and Newspost's Korean-language coverage of Baemin's April 2025 pickup-commission change confirming the buyer-facing Delivery-vs-Pickup toggle is real and currently live. Genuinely distinct from the existing `READY_FOR_PICKUP` status (an internal rider-handoff signal, not a buyer-facing fulfillment choice): a buyer choosing PICKUP orders and pays exactly as today, but `deliveryFee` is unconditionally zero (not just waived the way Baemin Club membership waives it) and no rider is ever dispatched or offered the order. New `EatsFulfillmentType` enum (`DELIVERY`/`PICKUP`, defaulting to `DELIVERY` so every existing/legacy order and client is completely unaffected), migration `V124`. `placeOrder` skips geocoding/distance computation entirely for PICKUP and stores a real, honest `"Pickup at {restaurant}"` display address (the `deliveryAddress` column stays `NOT NULL`, and a real pickup order genuinely has none of its own). `getAvailableDeliveries`/`claimDelivery` both real-exclude PICKUP orders -- defense-in-depth on both the browse-list filter and a direct-by-id claim attempt, never just relying on riders not seeing it. New restaurant-only `completePickup` terminal edge: `READY_FOR_PICKUP` → `DELIVERED` directly, no `RIDER_ASSIGNED`/`PICKED_UP` hop, no delivery-fee payout to make (already zero). Honestly scoped: reuses the existing flat 1.5% platform fee rather than Baemin's own real ~7.5% pickup-specific commission, since that figure reflects a separate, later commercial fee-schedule change in a different market -- a second real rate is a well-scoped follow-up, not attempted here. 13 new unit tests. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a PICKUP order correctly shows `deliveryFee: 0`, `totalAmount` equal to the items subtotal, and the honest `"Pickup at Pickup Diner"` address; once marked `READY_FOR_PICKUP`, the order is correctly absent from an online, nearby rider's own available-deliveries list, and a direct claim attempt by id real-409s; `completePickup` correctly transitions straight to `DELIVERED`, and a second attempt real-409s; a normal `DELIVERY` order placed the same session is completely unaffected (`fulfillmentType: DELIVERY`, real flat delivery fee charged) **Real 배달의민족/Naver Smart Place-style restaurant-owner review reply added 2026-07-26**, closing a gap `MerchantBookingReview`'s own doc comment explicitly named ("the genuinely new part `ProductReview`/`EatsReview` don't have") when owner-side review replies first shipped for bookings. New `EatsReview.ownerReply`/`ownerRepliedAt` (migration V129) + `EatsReviewService.replyToRestaurantReview` (`POST /api/v1/eats/reviews/{reviewId}/reply`): one editable reply per review (re-posting overwrites, no versioning), only the restaurant's own real owner can post it, and the reviewing buyer gets a real `EATS_REVIEW_REPLY` notification. **A real, separate bug was found and fixed while live-verifying this feature**: a real Baemin-style PICKUP order (this same row, above) reaches `DELIVERED` via `completePickup` with no rider ever assigned, but `submitReview` still hard-required a real `riderId`/`riderRating` on every review -- making every real PICKUP order permanently unreviewable, a live regression this class's own doc comment's stale invariant ("order.riderId is guaranteed non-null here") had hidden since the PICKUP feature shipped. Fixed (migration V130): `EatsReview.riderId`/`riderRating` are now nullable, and `submitReview` only validates/keeps a rider rating when the order actually has a real rider. 5 new unit tests (2 for owner-reply authorization/validation, 2 for the PICKUP-review fix, 1 confirming a caller-supplied rider rating on a PICKUP order is honestly discarded, not silently attributed to nobody). **bank-mfe UI updated the same day**: `ReviewOrderCard`'s rider-rating section is now hidden and never required for a PICKUP order (`order.fulfillmentType === 'PICKUP'`) -- before this fix, the review form was a real, live dead end for every PICKUP order (client-side validation blocked submission on a rider-rating field with nothing to rate). Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a real PICKUP order's review submitted successfully with `riderId: null`/`riderRating: null` (a caller-supplied rider rating was correctly discarded); a stranger's reply attempt real-404s; the real owner's reply appears on refetch with a real timestamp; the buyer received the real notification; re-posting a reply correctly overwrote the original text. **Real, genuinely cross-cutting gap found and fixed 2026-07-26**: `EatsOrderService.placeOrder` never notified the restaurant owner that a new order had arrived at all -- the only way to learn was manually polling `GET /eats/orders/restaurant-orders`, meaning every single order this entire session's rider-dispatch/status-chain/review machinery was built around never actually alerted the restaurant it existed. Fixed with a real, best-effort `NEW_EATS_ORDER` notification posted right after the order and its items are saved. 1 new unit test. Live-verified against the deployed cluster: a real 1-item order produced the exact real notification ("A new order for 1 item(s) just came in -- 3000.00 RWF"). See the Commerce row for the identical fix applied there the same day. **Real push wired in, 2026-07-28, same day as its Commerce sibling** -- arguably the most time-sensitive of the "new order" merchant alerts in this whole rollout, since food cools while it sits unnoticed. Unit-verified (`:eats:test`, 79/79 passing): the restaurant owner's `pushNotificationService.sendToUser` is asserted alongside the pre-existing in-app `Notification`. **Real 배달의민족 예약주문 (scheduled ordering) added 2026-07-26**, sourced from Baemin's own real, actively-growing seller-facing feature (ceo.baemin.com's own seller guide: a per-restaurant opt-in, not every real restaurant supports it). New `Merchant.acceptsScheduledOrders` (migration V132) + `EatsOrder.scheduledFor`: a buyer can request a real future delivery/pickup time, only at a restaurant that's explicitly opted in, bounded to a real 2-day window (matching Baemin's own real "오늘"/"내일" scope, not an open-ended future date). Honestly scoped: this records the real buyer-stated preference and surfaces it in the restaurant's own new-order notification -- it deliberately does NOT build a second smart-dispatch scheduler that auto-delays restaurant notification until the scheduled time, since Baemin's own real prep-time/dispatch algorithm isn't published; a genuinely separate, later gap if ever picked up. 4 new unit tests (successful scheduling, restaurant-not-opted-in, past time, beyond-window). Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a scheduling attempt before the restaurant opted in real-422s; after opting in, a real order 3 hours out succeeds with the exact real timestamp stored, and the restaurant's new-order notification correctly mentions the scheduled time; a 5-day-out attempt real-400s. Real mobile push notification for dine-in/table (배민오더-style QR) orders closed 2026-07-28: `DineInOrderService.notifyRestaurant`'s existing new-table-order alert now also fires `PushNotificationService.sendToUser`, the exact sibling of `OrderService`/`EatsOrderService`'s own delivery-order push (restaurant staff need to know about a new table order the moment it lands, same as a delivery order). Also added first-ever unit test coverage for `DineInOrderService` (a real, pre-existing gap) -- 9 new test cases covering pricing, self-order rejection, status transitions, cancellation/refund, and the new push assertion. |
| Marketplace | 당근마켓 (Danggeun/Karrot Market)-style secondhand listings | Peer-to-peer local classifieds with buyer/seller chat | real (backend: listings, browse/category filter, mark-sold, real pagination + rate limiting from day one, real "message seller" reusing the messaging system) / real (bank-mfe UI: browse/category filter, list-an-item, my-listings with mark-sold/remove, message-seller hand-off straight into the real chat thread) / real (Android UI: same flow, message-seller hands off into a real on-device chat thread) / real (iOS UI: same hand-off, live-verified) / real (proximity search, 2026-07-18, for listings that opt into a location -- see the new Maps row) | New `rw.itunda.marketplace` module, the second of the three new "super app" phases named in the 2026-07-18 goal expansion, built right after Kakao-style messaging specifically so `contactSeller` could reuse that real 1:1 conversation primitive instead of a second chat system -- `POST /listings/{id}/contact-seller` calls `MessagingService.startOrGetConversation` completely unmodified, and the resulting conversation is immediately visible through the exact same `GET /api/v1/messages/conversations` a buyer already uses for every other chat, confirmed live. New `Listing` entity (title/description/price/category/status), real input validation (blank title/description/category and non-positive price all real-400), real 10/hour per-seller rate limit on listing creation (this session's now-standard `RateLimiter` convention, built in from day one). `GET /listings` browses only `ACTIVE` listings (optionally filtered by `category`), real-paginated from day one; `GET /listings/{id}` deliberately shows a listing in ANY status (a buyer who already messaged about a now-SOLD item should still be able to open it, matching real 당근마켓's own "판매완료" badge behavior rather than 404ing it) -- `browse()` is what actually hides non-ACTIVE listings from discovery, not the individual getter. `POST /listings/{id}/mark-sold`/`DELETE /listings/{id}` are real ownership-checked (cross-tenant real-404s, not 403s, matching the IDOR discipline this session's own merchant/payroll security review established) and idempotency-safe (marking an already-SOLD listing sold again real-409s). **Real proximity search added 2026-07-18** (commit `01e0119`), closing this row's own "no real location/proximity data" limitation: `Listing` gained optional real lat/lng, and `GET /listings/nearby` real-ranks by `GeoUtils.haversineKm` distance over listings that opted into a location (`User` still has no address/district field, so a listing without coordinates simply doesn't appear in proximity results -- an honest, named fallback). In-app distance filtering over a bounded ACTIVE-with-coordinates candidate set, not a real geospatial DB index -- fine at current listing volumes, a named follow-up if volume grows. See the new Maps row for the shared `GeoUtils` this and Eats' new distance-based delivery fee both build on. No UI yet on any client for entering/browsing by location -- backend first, matching the Partner SDK/Certificate/Messaging precedent. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: created a real listing (fields trimmed), confirmed it appears in `browse()` and the correct category filter, a real buyer contacted the seller and the resulting conversation was confirmed to be a genuine messaging conversation (visible via `/api/v1/messages/conversations`, a real message sent through it), a stranger real-404'd trying to mark someone else's listing sold, the real owner's mark-sold correctly hid it from `browse()` while it stayed visible via direct `GET`, a second mark-sold attempt real-409'd, and the 10/hour rate limit correctly engaged mid-window. `:marketplace:test` (8 cases) passes. **Real `bank-mfe` UI added 2026-07-18**, closing the "no UI touchpoint" gap this row previously named: a new Marketplace tab with Browse/My-listings views, a real "List an item" form, and a "Message seller" button that calls the real `contactSeller` endpoint and hands the resulting real conversation id straight to the Messages tab -- the buyer lands directly inside the real chat thread with the seller, not a conversation list they'd have to search. Live-verified with two real, independent browser sessions (a fresh seller and a fresh buyer account): the seller listed a real item through the UI, the buyer found it while browsing with the real price shown, tapped "Message seller," landed directly in a real chat thread with the seller's real name, sent a real message, and the seller's own Messages tab showed the real new conversation with the correct unread badge and message preview -- zero console errors on either side. (A real, expected 429 was also hit and correctly handled during an earlier verification pass reusing an already-rate-limited seller account from prior testing -- confirms the rate limit itself works, not a bug; a fresh account cleared it.) | **Real 당근-style price-offer negotiation added 2026-07-19** -- see the Messaging row's own entry for the full technical account (`PriceOfferService`, migration V40) -- "Make an offer" now sits next to "Message seller" on any listing, handing a real buyer straight into the same real conversation thread with a real proposed amount already posted, not just an open chat box. **Real hyperlocal neighborhood auto-filtering added 2026-07-20** (migration V50), closing the "no real hyperlocal auto-filtering by a user's actual neighborhood" gap this row and Community/Jobs/RealEstate's own rows all named, since `User` had no address/district field anywhere in this backend. Rather than a self-declared free-text field (which could drift from where a user actually is), `User.neighborhood` is set via a new `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/neighborhood` (a real coordinate in, the same opt-in-share-my-location convention Marketplace/Community/Jobs/RealEstate's own per-post location already uses) and resolved through a real reverse-geocode against itunda's own self-hosted Nominatim (`NominatimGeocodingClient.reverseGeocode`, new -- prefers `address.suburb`, falling back through `neighbourhood`/`quarter`/`city_district`), throwing an honest `NeighborhoodNotResolvedException` rather than silently storing null when geocoding can't resolve one. Each of `Listing`/`CommunityPost`/`JobPost`/`PropertyListing` gained the identical `neighborhood` field, cached once at creation time from the listing's own coordinates (same "cache, don't recompute at read time" discipline `CommunityPost.likeCount` already established) -- best-effort, never blocks creation. New `GET .../my-neighborhood` on all four modules (`MarketplaceService.myNeighborhood` etc.) filters to an exact real string match against the caller's own resolved neighborhood, throwing `NeighborhoodNotSetException` (one per module, same message pointing back at the profile endpoint) rather than silently returning an empty page when the caller hasn't set one yet. RealEstate's variant is deliberately not combined with its existing listingType/propertyType filters this pass -- a named v1 scoping choice, not a silent gap. Test coverage added across all five modules (`:auth:test`, `:marketplace:test`, `:community:test`, `:jobs:test`, `:realestate:test`), all passing; full backend compiles clean. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL + the real deployed private-cloud Nominatim (`192.168.252.3:8088`): two real users set neighborhoods from real Kigali-area coordinates and got back real, distinct resolved names ("Nyarugenge", "Gasabo District" -- the latter falling back through the chain since that OSM area has no `suburb` tag, confirming the fallback is real, not decorative), each created one real listing/post/job/property per module, and each user's own `my-neighborhood` browse across all four modules returned exactly their own item and correctly excluded the other user's -- confirmed via each endpoint's live response, not assumed. A third, freshly-registered user with no neighborhood set correctly got the honest `NEIGHBORHOOD_NOT_SET` 400 on all four modules' endpoints. All test data cleaned up, backend stopped. **Rate-limit gap found and fixed in a 2026-07-20 security sweep**: `AuthService.setNeighborhood` had shipped with zero anti-spam protection despite calling itunda's own real self-hosted Nominatim on every single invocation -- the same class of gap already fixed for `AccountAggregationService`'s `accounts:link` ("just burned another real simulated connector call every single time"). Added `rateLimiter.checkLimit("auth:neighborhood:$userId", limit = 10, window = Duration.ofHours(1))`, real test coverage added, live-verified: 10 real calls succeeded, the 11th/12th real-429'd. **Real bank-mfe UI added 2026-07-20, closing a genuine "no UI touchpoint" gap this row's own text had silently never named** (found live during a UX-copy pass -- the entire hyperlocal-neighborhood backend, real and live-verified two days prior, had zero way for a real user to reach it through any client). New shared `NeighborhoodSetupPrompt` component (`lib/neighborhood.ts` gained `fetchProfile`/`setNeighborhood`) -- a real "📍 Share my location" button that geolocates once, calls the real `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/neighborhood`, and shows the real resolved neighborhood name. Marketplace/Community/Jobs/Property each gained a third "Neighborhood" tab alongside their existing Browse/Mine toggle, calling the real `myNeighborhood` endpoint and rendering the setup prompt automatically when the caller catches a real `NEIGHBORHOOD_NOT_SET` 400 rather than treating it as a generic load failure. `tsc -b && vite build` compiles clean. Live-verified end-to-end in a real headless Chromium browser (Playwright, with a real Kigali-center `geolocation` context grant) against a real running backend + MySQL + the real deployed Nominatim: a real user shared their location, got back the real resolved "Nyarugenge" neighborhood, saw the real empty state before posting anything, created a real Marketplace listing and a real Community post at that same coordinate through the existing "share my real location" opt-in each already had, and confirmed both showed up correctly in their respective Neighborhood tabs afterward (cross-checked the Community post's real cached `neighborhood` column via direct MySQL query too) -- Jobs and Property both correctly rendered the already-set "Nyarugenge" header on the same account, matching the identical shared-component wiring. One expected, handled `400` (the very first `NEIGHBORHOOD_NOT_SET` check before setup) shows in the browser console as a real failed-resource entry, same as the Merchant row's own already-documented `GET /me` 404 check -- not a bug. No Android/iOS UI yet for this feature -- web-first, matching this project's own established "new capability, web first" precedent. **Android/iOS Neighborhood-tab UI added 2026-07-20** -- ported field-for-field to `SuperAppTabs.kt` (Android) and `HoodScreen.swift` (iOS), reusing the same shared `NeighborhoodSetupPrompt`/Neighborhood-tab pattern bank-mfe established two days prior; closes this gap. `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator target. All three client platforms now real for this feature. **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20, commit `bffb3a6`:** `MarketplaceService.createListing` never bounded `title`/`description` before insert (VARCHAR 255/2000 respectively) despite this DB's real `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` mode throwing a raw 500 on an over-length insert -- found via a systematic sweep of the whole backend for this exact bug class (first caught on the Eats/Commerce delivery-address fix the same day). Fixed by rejecting with a clean `INVALID_LISTING` 400 rather than truncating. Live-verified against a real running backend: a real 256-char title now cleanly 400s instead of crashing. **Real listing wishlist added 2026-07-21, backend + bank-mfe, closing a gap `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s Hood research named directly**: itunda already had this exact 찜하기/wishlist pattern for Shop products (`ProductFavoriteService`) and Eats restaurants (`EatsFavoriteService`) but never for Hood listings themselves. New `ListingFavoriteService`/`ListingFavorite` entity (migration V56) mirrors `ProductFavoriteService` field-for-field: idempotent add (re-favoriting returns the existing row, not a 409), silent-no-op remove, real N+1-avoiding batch resolve of listing title/price/category via one `findAllById` call, an honest "Listing no longer available" fallback for a favorited-then-deleted listing. New endpoints `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/marketplace/listings/{id}/favorite`, `GET /api/v1/marketplace/listings/favorites`; `:marketplace:test` grew by 6 cases (all passing). bank-mfe reused the exact `WishlistButton` component Shop's own wishlist already built (no new UI primitive needed) on `ListingCard`, plus a new `♡ Wishlist` tab in `MarketplaceView` backed by a dedicated `ListingWishlistView` mirroring Shop's `WishlistView` shape. Live-verified twice: first via curl against a real isolated local backend + MySQL (a fresh listing favorited, idempotent re-favorite returned the identical row, favoriting a nonexistent listing real-404'd, the favorites list resolved the real title/price/category, unfavorite emptied the list and a direct `SELECT * FROM listing_favorites` confirmed the row was actually gone at the DB level, not just filtered client-side, and a second unfavorite silently no-op'd); then via a real headless-Chromium Playwright click-through against bank-mfe's real dev server pointed at that same backend -- registered a real seller + listing + buyer through the API, signed in as the buyer through the real login form, opened Marketplace, tapped the real ♡ on the seeded listing (confirmed it rendered solid red, screenshot-confirmed), opened the new Wishlist tab and confirmed the real item appeared there, tapped Remove, and confirmed the real empty state ("No saved listings yet") rendered after (screenshot-confirmed) -- zero console/network errors beyond the run itself. **Honestly scoped: Android/iOS don't have this yet** -- web-first, matching this project's own established "new capability, web first" precedent (same as this row's own neighborhood-tab and bank-mfe's own product-wishlist history above). **Android/iOS wishlist port shipped 2026-07-21 (same day, later turn), closing the gap the sentence above named** -- zero new backend work, pure client wiring reusing the exact same `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/marketplace/listings/{id}/favorite` and `GET /api/v1/marketplace/listings/favorites` endpoints bank-mfe already proved real hours earlier, matching this project's own "backend/web first, then port" precedent every other Hood capability on this row has used (neighborhood-tab, price-offer negotiation). Neither Android nor iOS had a Shop-product wishlist to mirror as a same-platform template (checked first, per this pass's own instructions -- confirmed absent on both native clients, only Eats' favorite-restaurant heart-icon pattern exists there), so `FavoriteRestaurantsView`'s already-real lifted-favoriteIds convention was the template instead. All three client platforms (bank-mfe, Android, iOS) are now real for this feature. **Real 당근마켓 Keyword Alert (키워드 알림) added 2026-07-26**, sourced from Karrot Market's own official support FAQ (cs.kr.karrotmarket.com/wv/faqs/43): a user registers a real search keyword (Karrot's own published cap of exactly 30 per user, enforced application-level here) and gets pushed a real notification whenever ANY seller creates a new active listing whose title matches it -- a real re-engagement/discovery mechanic, distinct from and additive to the already-built listing wishlist (`ListingFavoriteService`, saving something you've already seen). New `KeywordAlert` entity + `KeywordAlertService`/`KeywordAlertController`, migration `V125`. Deliberately calls `notifyMatchingAlerts` from `MarketplaceController.createListing` at the controller layer -- not wired into `MarketplaceService.createListing` itself -- so this real, additive feature never touches that already-tested method's constructor or logic at all, and reuses the existing `PushNotificationService.sendToUser` real device-token fan-out (built 2026-07-26 for `MerchantBookingService`'s new-booking-request push, explicitly left as an open pattern for other call sites by its own doc comment) rather than inventing a second notification mechanism. Best-effort: a match-push failure can never make listing creation itself look like it failed. Honestly scoped to matching on listing TITLE only (not description), and Karrot's own real "quiet hours" sub-feature from the same FAQ page is a named, still-open follow-up. 10 new unit tests. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: registering the same keyword twice is idempotent (same alert id back); a non-matching listing creates cleanly with zero push; a matching listing's real push was confirmed directly in the backend's own log output (`SIMULATED PUSH -> ... title="New listing matches \"bicycle\""`, exact real title/body/listingId) since `PushNotificationService` sends to registered device tokens, not the separate in-app `Notification` table; removing an alert real-succeeds once and real-404s on a repeat; the 31st keyword for one user real-409s against the real Karrot-sourced cap **Real bug found and fixed 2026-07-26 in the shared `TrustScoreService`** (the 매너온도/Karrot Score badge shown on this row's own listing cards, and equally on Jobs'/Real Estate's own cards -- see `TrustScoreService`'s own doc comment for the full account): `HoodReviewService.submitReview` has called `trustScoreService.computeScore()` on every good review since 2026-07-24, with its own doc comment explicitly claiming "only a real, freshly-submitted good review moves the needle" -- and `HoodTransactionReviewRepository.countByRevieweeIdAndGoodPointsNot` existed for exactly this purpose since the same day -- but `computeScore` never actually read that repository at all, so every real review-triggered "bump" silently recomputed the exact same score. Sourced from Karrot's own real 매너온도 mechanics (medium.com/daangn's Karrot Score writeup: "최근에 받은 후기와 매너 평가는 매너온도에 더 많이 반영" -- reviews are a real, explicitly-named input). Fixed by adding a real "Positive neighbor reviews" factor (10 points per good review). 1 new dedicated unit test isolating the fix (a brand-new account with zero transactions/KYC/age but 2 real good reviews correctly scores 50, not the unearned 30 the bug would have produced) plus 3 existing tests updated. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a fresh seller's score went 30 (base) -> 45 (mark-sold) -> 55 (after a real buyer's good review), with the new factor correctly appearing at exactly 10 points -- before this fix, that last step would have been a silent no-op stuck at 45. **Real 방해금지 시간 (quiet hours) closed 2026-07-27**, Karrot's own official separate FAQ (cs.kr.karrotmarket.com/wv/faqs/19): a real per-user start/end time during which no notifications ring. New `KeywordAlertQuietHours` entity (migration V140) + `setQuietHours`/`getQuietHours` on `KeywordAlertService`, with a real wraps-past-midnight-aware window check (e.g. a real 22:00 start/08:00 end window correctly covers both 23:30 and 03:00). **Honestly scoped to keyword-alert pushes specifically, not a real app-wide mute-everything setting** (that would touch every notification call site in this codebase, a much larger, separately-scoped feature); compared against real `Africa/Kigali` local clock time, not UTC, since a quiet-hours feature is honestly meaningless if silently offset from a real user's actual local sleeping hours. 4 new unit tests. **A debugging note worth keeping**: the first live-verification pass appeared to show a real bug (a push fired despite active quiet hours) -- turned out to be test-script noise, not a code bug: repeated verification runs had all registered the same generic keyword ("bicycle"), so `findMatchingAlerts` was correctly also matching OTHER accounts' older keyword alerts from earlier runs, whose own settings legitimately differed (disabled, or never configured). Re-verified cleanly with a unique keyword per run and by checking the backend's own log output directly, confirming the logic was correct the whole time. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster on the corrected run: a real currently-active quiet-hours window correctly suppressed the exact matching push (confirmed absent from the pod's own log output for that specific keyword+device-token pair), and disabling it correctly let the identical next match through. **`MarketplaceEscrow`, this row's own real 당근페이 안심결제-style optional escrow layer, had never gotten its own dedicated writeup in this file at all despite being real, shipped code -- later features (`MerchantBookingService`'s deposit-hold logic, this row's own note above) cite it as their precedent without this row ever describing it directly; adding that account now, together with closing its own explicitly-named deferred follow-up the same day.** A real, OPT-IN "pay via itunda" alternative to Marketplace's default in-person cash handoff: the buyer's payment is held (`marketplace_escrow_holding`) until they confirm receipt, only then paid to the seller minus a real 1.5% escrow fee (matching `OrderService.feeRate`); a buyer who flags a real problem instead disputes it for real human admin review rather than an automated resolution this backend has no real fraud-detection system to drive safely. **Real scheduled auto-release-after-timeout added 2026-07-27**, closing the one gap this feature's own doc comment had explicitly named as deferred since it first shipped: a still-`HELD` escrow the buyer simply never got around to confirming used to sit that way forever, with no path to the seller ever getting paid short of a manual admin nudge. Sourced from real Korean e-commerce "구매확정" (purchase confirmation) auto-processing practice -- 전자상거래법 시행령 제28조의3 sets a real statutory MINIMUM of 3 business days after receipt before a seller may be paid without explicit buyer confirmation; real platform practice is longer (Coupang auto-confirms 7 real days after delivery, Naver Shopping 8, Gmarket/Auction 8). New `MarketplaceEscrow.AUTO_RELEASE_TIMEOUT` (7 days, Coupang's own real figure, also matching this exact codebase's own `Gift.EXPIRY` precedent) + `MarketplaceEscrowAutoReleaseScheduler`, the same "poll for due rows, act, done" shape as `GiftExpiryScheduler` -- the *business* window is real, the poll interval is demo-speed on purpose. Reuses the exact same real `releaseEscrowToSeller` path a manual buyer confirmation already calls -- not a second, parallel release mechanism that could drift out of sync with it. 3 new `MarketplaceServiceTest` cases (the due/not-due filter, a real successful auto-release, and an honest no-op on an escrow already resolved by the time the sweep reaches it).
