New `GET /api/v1/messages/presence?userIds=...` (works for any set of ids, not just 1:1 partners -- a group thread can pass every member's id) backs an on-demand poll; a new real-time `"presence"` WebSocket push type notifies a user's real 1:1 conversation partners the moment they come online/go offline (via a new `ConversationRepository.findPartnerUserIds` query), honestly scoped to 1:1 only -- group members are never proactively pushed a presence change (unbounded fan-out, low value for this pass), only ever resolved via the poll endpoint. `:messaging:test` grew to 13 cases. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend with two real independent WebSocket connections (Node's native `WebSocket`): connecting fired a real online push to the other real connected user within ~1s, the poll endpoint agreed while both were connected, disconnecting fired a real offline push, and the poll endpoint correctly reflected the new state afterward -- both the push and the poll independently correct, not just one checked. UI shipped across all three clients: a green online dot on each conversation-list avatar (polled every 10s) plus a live "Online"/"Offline" label in the open thread's header (initial fetch, then updated instantly via the same WebSocket connection the thread already holds for messages). bank-mfe build clean, Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean, iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED`. **Real typing indicators added 2026-07-19**, closing the second Talk polish item: `MessagingWebSocketHandler` gained its first-ever inbound (client-to-server) frame handler -- every prior push had been purely server-to-client. A client sends `{"type":"typing","conversationId":...}` (or `groupConversationId`) over the already-open socket; the server verifies the sender is a real participant/member (same IDOR discipline as every REST endpoint -- a non-participant's frame is silently dropped, not relayed), rate-limits to one relay per (user, conversation) per 2 seconds via the existing `RateLimiter`, and relays `{"type":"typing","userId":...}` to the real other participant(s) -- for 1:1 via a new `ConversationRepository.findPartnerUserIds`-adjacent lookup, for groups via `GroupConversationMemberRepository`, fanned out to every other real member. Ephemeral by design: never persisted, best-effort (a malformed/rate-limited/unauthorized frame is silently dropped, never errors the socket). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend with three real independent WebSocket connections (Node's native `WebSocket`): a 1:1 typing frame reached the real other participant and was correctly withheld from a real stranger; sending twice inside the same 2s window correctly relayed exactly once; a group typing frame reached both other real members; a non-participant's frame targeting the 1:1 conversation was correctly dropped with nobody receiving it. UI shipped across all three clients: an "X is typing…" line above the message input, client-throttled to match the server's own 2s window, auto-clearing after 3s of silence (the same client-side "infer stopped typing" convention every real chat app uses, since there's no explicit "stopped" event) -- both 1:1 and group threads, showing multiple names in a group ("A and B are typing…"). bank-mfe build clean, Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean, iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED`. **Real emoji reactions added 2026-07-19, closing the third Talk polish item, backend + full 3-client UI in one pass.** `MessageReaction`/`GroupMessageReaction` (migration V39): one row per (message, user, emoji) with a real DB unique constraint, so a user can react with several different emoji but never twice with the same one -- tapping an already-active reaction is a real toggle-off (deletes the row), not a duplicate/409, matching the same idempotent-toggle UX Eats favorites already established. `MessagingService.toggleReaction`/`GroupMessagingService.toggleReaction` push the updated real reaction summary live over the existing WebSocket (`publishReactionChange`/`publishGroupReactionChange`), and `getMessages` on both REST endpoints now attaches every message's real reaction summary via one batched query (`getReactionSummaries`), not one query per message. UI ported to all three clients in one pass: a reaction badge row under every bubble (emoji + real count, highlighted and tappable-to-remove when the current user is one of the reactors) plus a small quick-react palette (👍 ❤️ 😂 😮 😢) behind a smile-icon button -- bank-mfe (`MessageReactions` component + `toggleReaction`/`toggleGroupReaction` in `lib/messaging.ts`), Android (`MessageReactionsRow` in `SuperAppTabs.kt`, `ReactionGroupDto`), iOS (`MessageReactionsRow` in `TalkScreen.swift`, a `Menu` for the quick palette, `ReactionGroupDto` with a custom `Decodable` init since the real-time push for a brand-new message omits `reactions` entirely). All three clients also route the new `"reaction"` WebSocket push type to live-update a message's badges without waiting for the next poll. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` (needed the standard post-`tuist generate` `pod install` refresh); `:messaging:test` passes. No interactive click-through this pass -- matches [[feedback_verification_pace]], since the reaction toggle/summary logic itself was already backend-tested and this increment is UI wiring on top of proven endpoints. **당근-style price-offer negotiation shipped 2026-07-19, closing the Talk backlog completely -- every item from the original "100% KakaoTalk + 당근 채팅" ask is now real.** New `rw.itunda.marketplace.PriceOfferService` (migration V40, `PriceOffer` entity): a real offer/counter-offer negotiation posted as real chat messages in the same real 1:1 conversation `contactSeller` already establishes -- no new chat surface, no automatic money movement or listing-status change (an accepted offer is a real conversational agreement only; the seller still uses the existing `markSold` separately, matching real 당근마켓's own separation between price agreement and the actual in-person/off-platform exchange). Each offer is one real DB row; a counter-offer is a brand-new row (the original flips to `COUNTERED`), not an edit, so the full negotiation history survives per conversation. Real IDOR discipline: only the participant who did NOT propose the current pending amount may respond (`OwnOfferException` if you try to accept your own offer), a non-participant gets a 404 on `/offers/{id}/respond`, and the new `GET /marketplace/conversations/{id}/offers` endpoint filters to rows where the caller is genuinely the buyer or seller rather than trusting the conversation id alone (a stranger passing a real conversation id belonging to two other people gets an honest empty list, not their real negotiation history). Full bank-mfe UI in the same pass: a "Make an offer" button next to "Message seller" on any listing, and real offer bubbles rendered inline in the Talk thread (amount + status + Accept/Decline/Counter, shown only to whichever participant didn't propose the pending amount) -- reuses the conversation's existing message poll/WebSocket push, refreshing the offer history whenever a new message arrives so an offer/response renders correctly without waiting for the next poll tick. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend (`verify_offers.mjs`): seller blocked from offering on their own listing, buyer blocked from accepting their own offer, a real stranger real-404s responding and sees an empty conversation-offer history, a real chat message posted with the correct formatted body, a real counter-offer created as a new PENDING row with the proposer flipped and the original COUNTERED, responding to an already-resolved offer real-409s, and a final accept succeeds. Also live-verified via two real headless-browser passes (buyer's view: real offer bubble showing "💰 35,000 RWF" / "Pending" with no action buttons since they're the proposer; seller's view: real Accept/Decline/Counter buttons present, clicking Accept updates the bubble to "Accepted" and shows the real "✅ Offer accepted" confirmation message) -- both with zero console errors. `:marketplace:test` grew with a new `PriceOfferServiceTest` (12 cases). Honestly scoped: Android/iOS UI not built this pass, web-first matching this project's own established precedent for new (not just ported) features. **Ported to Android and iOS the same day, commit `0a6308b`.** Both mirror bank-mfe's exact design: a "Make an offer" button next to "Message seller" on any listing (Android: `ListingCard` in `SuperAppTabs.kt`; iOS: `ListingCard` in `HoodScreen.swift`), and real offer bubbles in the 1:1 chat thread (Android: `OfferBubble` in `SuperAppTabs.kt`'s `ChatThreadView`; iOS: `OfferBubble` in `TalkScreen.swift`'s `ChatThreadScreen`) showing amount + status + Accept/Decline/Counter, refreshed off the same real message poll/WebSocket push both platforms already had for reactions. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` clean; iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` on the first attempt. No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass -- the underlying offer/response logic and the equivalent bank-mfe UI were both already live-verified in the prior slice, so per [[feedback_verification_pace]] a clean compile/build was judged proportionate for a straight port of already-proven logic onto two new platforms. **This closes price-offer negotiation to full parity across all three clients.** **Real N+1 fix, 2026-07-19 (performance sweep)**: `GroupMessagingService.createGroup` was issuing one `findById` call per invited member instead of one batched `findAllById`, and `createGroupByPhoneNumbers` did the same with one `findByPhoneNumber` call per invited phone number (new `UserRepository.findAllByPhoneNumberIn` added to fix it) — both the exact same N+1 shape `PayrollService.runPayroll` was already fixed for earlier this project. `sendMessage`'s per-recipient notification loop also switched from N individual `.save()` calls to one batched `.saveAll()`. Live-verified against a real running backend: group creation by user id, by phone number, and message send with correct real notification fan-out to both other members (confirmed via direct MySQL query) all still work identically; an invalid member id still correctly real-404s. `:messaging:test` (34 cases) passes. **Real KakaoTalk-style "선물하기" money gift shipped 2026-07-20, backend + full 3-client UI in one pass.** New `rw.itunda.gift` module (migration V51, `Gift` entity): sending a gift moves the sender's real money out of their wallet immediately into a new `GIFT_HOLDING` escrow clearing account (same "hold, don't move directly" shape `EatsOrderService`'s own delivery-fee escrow already established, added to `LedgerAccount.SEED_IDS` -- a real, live-caught bug: `postLedgerTransaction` throws `IllegalStateException: Unknown ledger account` for any non-seeded account id, wrongly assumed reusable ad hoc from reading `EatsOrderService`'s bare string literal), and only reaches the recipient's wallet once they explicitly claim it (`POST /api/v1/gifts/{id}/claim`) -- an unclaimed gift auto-refunds to the sender after 7 days via a new `GiftExpiryScheduler` polling `getExpiredPendingGifts`, reversing the exact hold leg pair back, the same reversing-ledger-entry technique `SupportService.reverseTransaction` already uses. Two send entry points: `POST /api/v1/gifts` by phone number (starts/reuses a 1:1 conversation, same `startOrGetConversation` primitive) and `POST /api/v1/gifts/conversations/{id}` sent directly inside an already-open thread (recipient resolved as "whichever participant isn't me" via a new `MessagingService.getConversationForParticipant` wrapper, never re-typed) -- both delegate to a shared private `createGift` helper and post a real chat message into the conversation, the same "special message body" convention `PriceOfferService` already established. Real IDOR discipline throughout: `getGift`/`getGiftsForConversation` real-404 for a stranger rather than leaking gift existence, `claimGift` real-403s (`NOT_GIFT_RECIPIENT`) if the sender tries to open their own gift. Real anti-spam limits via the existing `RateLimiter` (20 sends/hour, 30 claims/hour per user). A real ordering bug caught during a mid-implementation refactor: the self-gift check briefly moved after `startOrGetConversation`, which throws its own `SelfConversationException` that `GiftController` has no handler for -- would have surfaced as an unhandled 500 instead of the honest `SELF_GIFT_NOT_ALLOWED` 400; fixed by re-ordering the check first, verified by a new test asserting `startOrGetConversation` is never called on a self-gift attempt. `:gift:test` (15 cases) passes. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL: a phone-based gift correctly escrowed the sender's money (wallet debited, `gift_holding` credited), a conversation-based gift correctly auto-resolved the recipient from the conversation (not a re-typed phone number), claiming moved the escrowed amount into the recipient's real wallet and posted a real "🎁 Gift opened" confirmation message, a self-gift attempt correctly 400'd before any conversation was created, and the rate limiter correctly 429'd after repeated sends. Full 3-client UI in the same pass, all reusing the conversation-based endpoint plus a new `GET /api/v1/gifts/conversations/{id}` (mirrors `PriceOfferService`'s own per-conversation offer fetch) to render every gift in a thread as an inline bubble: bank-mfe (`GiftBubble` + gift composer in `BankDashboard.tsx`, `lib/gift.ts`), Android (`GiftBubble` + composer in `SuperAppTabs.kt`'s `ChatThreadView`, `GiftDto`/`SendGiftInConversationRequest` in `ApiService.kt`), iOS (`GiftBubble` + composer in `TalkScreen.swift`'s `ChatThreadScreen`, matching DTOs in `NetworkClient.swift`) -- a 🎁 button next to the message composer opens an amount+note form, and a gift bubble shows a real Open/Claim button only to the recipient of a still-pending, unexpired gift. bank-mfe build clean (typecheck + `vite build`); **Android live-verified end-to-end on a physical Galaxy A16**: sent a real 1,500 RWF gift through the on-device UI (sender wallet correctly debited 10,800 → 9,300), logged in as the recipient and claimed it through the UI (wallet correctly credited 7,500 → 9,000); iOS `BUILD SUCCEEDED` and launched without crashing on a real Simulator, but not interactively click-through verified -- this environment has no iOS Simulator touch-injection tooling (no idb/XCTest harness) unlike Android's real physical-device path, so per [[feedback_verification_pace]] a clean build plus exact parity with the already-verified Android/web implementation was judged proportionate. **This adds a brand-new KakaoTalk capability with no remaining named gaps across backend or any client.** **Real crash-risk fix, 2026-07-20, commit `bffb3a6`:** both `MessagingService.sendMessage` (1:1) and `GroupMessagingService.sendMessage`/`createGroup`/`createGroupByPhoneNumbers` never bounded their real DB column limits (`Message.body`/`GroupMessage.body` VARCHAR 2000, `GroupConversation.name` VARCHAR 100) -- the same systematic-sweep find as the Eats/Commerce delivery-address fix the same day, since this DB genuinely runs `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` and throws a raw 500 on an over-length insert. New `MessageTooLongException`/`GroupMessageTooLongException`/`GroupNameTooLongException` reject cleanly with a 400 instead. Live-verified against a real running backend: a real 2001-char message now cleanly `MESSAGE_TOO_LONG` 400s instead of crashing. **Real KakaoPay-style "정산하기" (settlement/split-bill) shipped 2026-07-21, backend-only this pass, per `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` Section 6's own top-ranked, most-sourced recommendation for Bank/Pay.** New `rw.itunda.splitbill` module (migration V57, `SplitBill`/`SplitBillParticipant` entities), chat-embedded in an existing group conversation rather than KakaoPay's spending-history-anchored contrast case (Toss's own 더치페이) -- itunda's Talk-first architecture and Gift's own precedent ("a real chat message rather than a separate notification surface," per `GiftService.kt`'s own doc comment) made the chat-embedded pattern the natural fit, matching Section 7's own cross-cutting call for Gift and Split-bill to share one "chat-embedded money action" shell. Structurally the *reverse* of Gift, not a copy: Gift escrows money out of a sender's wallet for a recipient to later claim; a split bill's organizer has already fronted the whole bill outside this system and is requesting it back, so each named participant pays their own share directly into the organizer's wallet -- a direct WALLET-to-WALLET push reusing `P2pService.sendDirect`'s exact real ledger shape (no escrow account, no fee), not Gift's hold-then-claim. `POST /api/v1/split-bills/conversations/{groupConversationId}` (real `Idempotency-Key`-gated, same convention as every other money-moving creation endpoint) resolves participants against `GroupMessagingService`'s own real membership (a new public `getGroupForMember` wrapper mirroring 1:1 `MessagingService.getConversationForParticipant`), silently excludes the organizer from their own request rather than erroring, and real-400s (`PARTICIPANT_NOT_GROUP_MEMBER`) if a named participant isn't an actual group member -- an honest input-validation error, not an IDOR check, since these are the organizer's own listed names. Scoped down from KakaoPay's full feature set per the design doc's own explicit warning that "a plain even-split alone would just be re-doing Toss": this ports the even-split floor plus exactly one real KakaoPay differentiator, silent rounding-remainder absorption -- computed in the currency's own minor unit (cents) so one deterministically-chosen participant's share silently absorbs whatever integer division leaves over, reconciling the sum of every participant's share to the total exactly, never off by a rounding error. `POST /api/v1/split-bills/{id}/pay` real-404s for anyone not a named participant (including the organizer, who was never added as one) rather than a fabricated permission error, real-409s (`SPLIT_BILL_SHARE_ALREADY_PAID`) on a repeat payment attempt, and flips the split bill from OPEN to SETTLED the moment every participant has paid, posting a real settlement confirmation into the same group chat. `:splitbill:test` (13 cases, `SplitBillServiceTest`) passes, covering the rounding-absorption math in isolation plus the full creation/payment/IDOR matrix. Live-verified end-to-end against a real running isolated backend (port 4060) + direct MySQL cross-checks, not just 200 responses: a real 3-member group's 10,000 RWF dinner bill split evenly 5,000/5,000 between two named participants, organizer's wallet correctly credited exactly 10,000 RWF total across both payments (confirmed via direct `ledger_entries` query, not recomputed); a non-participant stranger and the organizer themselves both real-404 trying to pay; a participant who already paid real-409s on a second attempt; the split bill correctly stayed OPEN after only one of two shares was paid and flipped to SETTLED with a real `settledAt` timestamp the moment the second was; a real non-divisible 1,000 RWF bill split 3 ways live-produced exactly two 333.33 shares and one absorbing 333.34 share, summing to exactly 1,000.00; a participant not actually a member of the target group real-400'd before any row was created; insufficient funds real-422'd with the payer's wallet balance provably untouched afterward (confirmed via direct MySQL, no partial debit); and a duplicate `POST .../conversations/{id}` call with the same `Idempotency-Key` correctly replayed the exact same split bill id rather than creating a duplicate or posting a second chat message. **Honestly, explicitly deferred, matching the design doc's own scoping (at original ship time)**: KakaoPay's randomized "사다리타기" ladder-game split mode (closed 2026-07-25), the up-to-5-tracked-settlement-rounds mechanic, scheduled reminder nudges for unpaid participants (closed 2026-07-27, see below), and photo receipt attach (closed 2026-07-28: `SplitBillService.attachReceipt`, organizer-only, a URL not a binary upload since this backend has no file-storage layer -- live-verified end-to-end, a real organizer attached a real receipt URL to a real split bill, confirmed it persisted on read, confirmed a non-organizer participant real-404'd trying to attach one, confirmed a blank URL real-400'd) were all named follow-ups; the up-to-5-tracked-rounds mechanic was the one still open, closed 2026-07-28: new `SplitBillService.requestNextRound` lets the organizer explicitly escalate to a new settlement round (distinct from the automatic 24h reminder nudge), capped at KakaoPay's own real 5-round ceiling (`docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` Section 6/7, cross-verified across 5 outlets), re-announcing to the group thread with the real round number visible. New `SplitBill.currentRound` (migration V149). Unit-verified (`:splitbill:test`, 25/25 passing): round increments 1->2, a non-organizer real-404s, every-participant-already-paid real-409s, an already-SETTLED bill real-409s, and hitting round 5 real-409s. Live-verified end-to-end against itunda.duckdns.org: a real organizer escalated round 1->2->3, a non-organizer real-404'd, and after the sole participant paid in full the bill real-409'd as already-settled -- every one of KakaoPay's four named 정산하기 differentiators is now real. No client UI yet (bank-mfe/Android/iOS composer + bubble rendering) -- backend-first, matching this project's own established multi-pass precedent (Partner SDK, Toss Certificate, and 1:1 Messaging itself all shipped backend-then-UI across separate passes). **Android client wired 2026-07-22**, closing that "no client UI yet" gap for this platform: a real receipt icon in `GroupThreadView`'s top bar (`SuperAppTabs.kt`) opens a new `GroupSplitBillsView` listing every real split bill for that group (organizer, total, per-participant share/status) with a real create form (amount, description, tap-to-select participants from the group's real member list) and a real "Pay my share" action for the caller's own pending shares. bank-mfe and iOS remain open. **Android leave-group/add-member wired 2026-07-22**, closing the last named Android gap in this row: `POST`/`DELETE .../groups/{id}/members` were real on the backend from the start but had zero UI anywhere. New `GroupManageMembersView` (a real people icon in `GroupThreadView`'s top bar) lists current members, a real "Leave group" action, and a real add-member picker sourced from the caller's own Talk contacts, filtered to exclude people already in the group. **bank-mfe client wired 2026-07-22 for both split-bill and leave-group/add-member**, closing this row's remaining named client gaps on web: a new receipt icon and a new people icon in `GroupThread`'s header open `GroupSplitBillsView`/`GroupManageMembersView`, mirroring the Android port field-for-field. **iOS client wired 2026-07-22**, closing the last remaining client gap for this row: a real receipt icon and a real people icon in `TalkScreen.swift`'s `GroupThreadScreen` header open the same `GroupSplitBillsView`/`GroupManageMembersView` flows; `BUILD SUCCEEDED` against a real iPhone 17 Simulator. **Real KakaoPay 송금봉투 (money envelope) themed presets added 2026-07-26, closing a gap `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` itself named** (story.kakaopay.com/support.kakaopay.com: "themed presets ([축하해요]/[내마음]/[행운만땅]/[정산해요]) carry occasion/message instead of free text"). New optional `GiftTheme` enum (migration `V114`) on the existing `Gift` entity -- exactly these 4 real, sourced presets, nothing invented, since `DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s own research notes "a complete, verified catalog... wasn't found." Additive alongside the pre-existing free-text `note`, never replacing it -- a theme communicates occasion, the note still carries an actual message, matching how KakaoPay's own envelope shows both together. The chat message `GiftService` composes now leads with the real Korean preset label + a matching emoji (e.g. "🎉 축하해요 (Congratulations)") when a theme is chosen, falling back to the original plain "🎁" prefix when it isn't -- fully backward compatible, verified live. Live-verified against the deployed cluster: a themed gift with a note produced the exact real chat message `"🎉 축하해요 (Congratulations) Sent a gift: 3,000 RWF — \"You did it!\""`; an untouched, theme-less gift sent in the same conversation produced the exact original unchanged format, `"🎁 Sent a gift: 1,000 RWF — \"just because\""`. **Real Kakao-style group per-message read-receipt countdown added 2026-07-26**, closing a gap `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s own Talk section explicitly flagged as still open ("Direct messages now show a Kakao-style pending-read `1`... Group receipts still need a true per-member model") — and which `GroupConversationMember`'s own doc comment had explicitly deferred as "a genuinely separate, later feature if ever needed." Turned out the existing single per-member `lastReadAt` cursor (added for `unreadCount` at the conversation-list level) was already sufficient: a message's real remaining-unread count is exactly how many OTHER members (excluding the sender) have a cursor still earlier than that message's `sentAt` — no new per-message-per-member table needed. New `GroupMessagingService.getUnreadCounts`, surfaced as `unreadCount` on every message in `GET /api/v1/messages/groups/{id}/messages`; a new `RealtimeMessagePublisher.publishGroupReadReceiptChange` push (WebSocket `type: "group_read_receipt"`) fires every time a member's cursor advances, so an open thread's countdown can decrement live instead of only on next refetch, matching every other real-time push in this interface. 2 new unit tests (`GroupMessagingServiceTest`); also found and fixed a real, unrelated pre-existing test gap while running the suite (`userRepository.findAllById` was never stubbed in the "member sends a message" test, silently relying on the mention-parsing call never actually executing -- it does, every send, mention or not). Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a fresh 3-member group's message started at `unreadCount: 2`, dropped to `1` after one member opened the thread, to `0` after the second did too, and a second message sent by a different member correctly counted 2 remaining (the two non-senders), confirming the countdown reflects exact real cursor state, not an approximation. Backend-only this pass -- no client UI touchpoint yet. **Real group-chat pin added 2026-07-26**, closing the "pin" half of a Talk long-press-menu gap `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` had named (1:1 conversations already had this via `Conversation.pinnedMessageId`; `GroupConversation` had no equivalent field at all). New `pinned_message_id` column (migration `V119`) plus `GroupMessagingService.setPinnedMessage`/`getPinnedMessage`, mirroring 1:1 pin's exact shape: any real member can pin/unpin (a shared conversation has one shared pin, not a per-owner setting), a cross-group message reference real-404s the same IDOR-safe way. Also corrected two other stale doc claims found while researching this: `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` said "no mention parsing exists anywhere" for group chats, but that shipped 2026-07-25 (`GroupMessagingService.parseMentions`, real push wired in 2026-07-28 -- deliberately mention-only, not every ordinary group message, matching the same notification-spam discipline real Slack/KakaoTalk already establish by pushing only for an explicit @mention; unit-verified, `:messaging:test` 33/33 passing, and live-verified end-to-end against itunda.duckdns.org: mentioning a real group member by their real firstName produced a real GROUP_MENTION push alongside the in-app notification, while an ordinary no-mention message correctly sent no push at all) -- and its own Marketplace/Jobs/Property wishlist recommendation was already fully built (`ListingFavoriteService`/`JobPostFavoriteService`/`PropertyListingFavoriteService`, all controller-wired) but never marked done. 2 new unit tests (pin/unpin plus cross-group rejection). Live-verified end-to-end: a fresh 2-member group's message started unpinned, a non-sender member pinned it and both members saw the same real shared pin, unpinning cleared it for both. **bank-mfe UI wired 2026-07-26**: real per-message read-receipt countdown (`m.unreadCount`, shown next to the timestamp on a sender's own messages exactly like 1:1's own "1" indicator, disappearing at 0) and a real pin/unpin banner in `GroupThread`, both mirroring `ConversationThread`'s already-proven 1:1 UI shape field-for-field. The live `group_read_receipt` WebSocket push triggers a real thread refetch so the countdown updates live as members read, not just on the next 4s poll. Verified via a clean `tsc -b && vite build` plus directly exercising the pin/messages endpoints against the live backend to confirm response shapes (including the new `unreadCount` field) match the updated TypeScript interfaces exactly. Android/iOS still open. **Real KakaoTalk Emoticon Store added 2026-07-26**, sourced from Kakao Corp's own official emoticon-business product page and Inquivix's coverage of its real scale (cumulative sends past 300 billion as of Nov 2025). A server-seeded catalog of purchasable artist sticker packs, sent as real first-class message content -- structurally distinct from the existing toggleable emoji-reaction row (reacting to someone else's message, not sending a purchasable sticker as your own). Purchase is a real one-time wallet debit through the exact same `LedgerService.postLedgerTransaction` call shape every other purchase in this backend uses, crediting a new `EMOTICON_REVENUE` ledger account (itunda's own direct product revenue, not an escrow hold like Gift/Ride/Booking money-in-flight). Sending reuses `MessagingService`/`GroupMessagingService`'s own already-tested `sendMessage`, adding `emoticonId` as one more optional trailing parameter alongside the existing `imageUrl` precedent -- ownership is verified by the new `EmoticonService` before either method is ever invoked, so neither already-tested method needs to know about the Emoticon repositories at all. Honestly scoped at the time: no Emoticon Plus monthly-subscription tier and no gifting-a-pack-to-another-user, both real Kakao features and each its own well-scoped follow-up. **Real gifting-a-pack-to-another-user closed 2026-07-28**: `EmoticonService.giftPack` resolves the recipient by phone number (the same real convention `P2pService.sendDirect` already establishes), reuses `purchasePack`'s exact ledger movement but credits the *recipient's* `UserEmoticonPack` with `source=GIFTED` (a schema value that had existed since day one with no code path producing it until now), and notifies the recipient. Live-verified end-to-end: a real 5,000 RWF-funded giver gifted the seeded pack to a second real account (201, `source: "GIFTED"`, correct recipient id), the recipient's own owned-packs list showed it, re-gifting the same pack to the same recipient real-409'd, and gifting to a phone number with no itunda account real-404'd. Emoticon Plus's monthly-subscription tier remains the one still-open follow-up. New `EmoticonPack`/`Emoticon`/`UserEmoticonPack` entities, migration `V122`, one demo pack seeded via `SeedDataRunner` (server-managed catalog, not user-generated). 9 new unit tests. **Live-verification of the new send path surfaced a real, separate, pre-existing bug affecting three already-shipped features** (composer photo send, message forwarding, @mentions): `imageUrl`/`forwardedFromMessageId`/`forwardedFromType`/`mentionedUserIds` were real columns on `Message`/`GroupMessage`, but neither the `GET .../messages` history endpoint nor the live WebSocket push ever included them in their response -- only the sender's own immediate `POST` response carried the rich content, meaning a recipient never actually saw a photo, a forwarded-message label, or a mention on their own screen, either live or on refetch. Fixed in the same pass (both REST controllers, both WebSocket push payloads). Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: catalog list/detail correctly public-readable to any authenticated user; sending an unowned emoticon real-403s; purchase real-debits the exact 500 RWF price and credits ownership; a second purchase real-409s; the owned-pack send succeeds and — after the same-pass bug fix — the *recipient's own refetch* correctly shows the real `emoticonId`, not just the sender's own response **Real KakaoTalk-style 선물하기 기프티콘 (mobile gift voucher) added 2026-07-26**, distinct from the money-gift feature this row already covers -- sourced from real gifticon mechanics (huffingtonpost.kr/imaeil.com's 2016 coverage of Kakao's own validity-extension policy change, cs.gifticon.com's own FAQ): a voucher redeemable at ONE specific real itunda merchant, either for a specific `MerchantProduct` (snapshotted at purchase, e.g. "Iced Latte") or a flat cash-equivalent amount, never landing in the recipient's own wallet the way a money gift does. New `GiftVoucher` entity (migration V127) + `GiftVoucherService`/`GiftVoucherController` in the existing `:gift` module: the purchaser's money moves into a new `GIFT_VOUCHER_HOLDING` escrow account immediately, and only reaches the merchant's wallet when the MERCHANT's own authenticated account redeems it (`POST /api/v1/gift-vouchers/{id}/redeem`, mirroring `MerchantService.collect()`'s real "merchant collects" pattern -- never a recipient self-serve redeem that could be faked), charged the same real flat 1.5% platform fee at redemption time. Itunda's own honest scoping choices, not claimed reproductions of any one real merchant's policy: a 180-day default validity (the middle of Kakao's own published 60-365 day range), one real one-time extension (+90 days, only within 30 days of expiry -- scoped down from Kakao's real "unlimited within 5 years"), and a real 90% cash refund on expiry with the remaining 10% forfeited as real fee revenue (not silently dropped). 13 new unit tests. **A real bug was found and fixed during live verification**: `redeemVoucher`'s confirmation message initially tried to post as the merchant owner into the purchaser↔recipient 1:1 conversation the merchant was never a participant in, real-500ing via `MessagingService`'s own `requireParticipant` check every single time -- fixed by posting as the recipient's own message instead (the party actually present at redemption), with a new test asserting the correct sender going forward. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a purchaser's balance dropped exactly 3,000 RWF on purchase; a stranger's redeem attempt real-404s; the real merchant's redemption credited exactly 2,955 RWF (3,000 minus the real 1.5% fee) and flipped the voucher to `REDEEMED`; re-redeeming the same voucher real-409s. **Real split-bill scheduled reminder nudges added 2026-07-27, closing this row's own last-remaining named split-bill follow-up** (KakaoPay's own real 정산하기 reminder feature -- a real chat nudge when a named participant hasn't paid). New `SplitBillParticipant.lastReminderSentAt` (migration V143, `null` means never reminded -- a fresh participant is immediately due for their first real nudge, the same "null means immediately eligible" trick `AutoSaveScheduler`/`AutoTopUpScheduler` already established, so live verification never needs to wait out a real 24 hours). New `SplitBillService.sendDueReminders` + `SplitBillReminderScheduler` (`@Scheduled(fixedDelay = 30000)`, same demo-speed-poll-over-a-real-business-cadence shape as those same two schedulers) sends a real chat-embedded nudge -- `groupMessagingService.sendMessage` posted as the organizer, into the same group thread the original request lives in, not a separate push channel -- to every real still-PENDING participant whose last reminder (or creation, if never reminded) is 24+ real hours old. Resilient per-participant: one bad row never blocks the sweep for every other real due participant. 3 new `SplitBillServiceTest` cases. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster with zero manual trigger calls: created a real split bill with one named participant, waited ~9 real seconds (well within the scheduler's own 30-second poll), and the real reminder message appeared in the group chat sent as the organizer; the participant then paid their real share, and a second real scheduler poll ~40 seconds later correctly sent no further reminder (message count unchanged) since they were now PAID. Real mobile push notification for 1:1 direct messages closed 2026-07-28: `MessagingService.sendMessage`'s existing quiet-room mute-preference check now also gates a real `PushNotificationService.sendToUser` call (not just the in-app `Notification` row), so a muted conversation stays muted for push too; live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster with two fresh accounts -- a real 1:1 message produced a real `NEW_MESSAGE` push-backed notification for the recipient. Real mobile push notification closed 2026-07-28 for `EmoticonService.giftPack`'s existing EMOTICON_PACK_GIFTED notification, the recipient's real gift-arrived moment. This closes the entire push-notification rollout undertaken this session: every real `Notification(` call site found via `PushNotificationService`'s own doc comment plus every later grep sweep is now wired, except `AuthService`'s OTP-code notifications, deliberately left alone since those are already delivered via SMS. 17/17 `:messaging:test` passing (existing `EmoticonServiceTest.kt`, 1 new push assertion). |
| Offline | Low-connectivity use | Demo/fallback and pending actions | real (backend + Android + iOS) | Backend built and live-verified 2026-07-13: new `POST /api/v1/actions/batch` + `GET /api/v1/actions/types` (new `offline` module) accepts a batch of client-generated pending actions and dispatches each independently through its real domain service via a new `BatchActionHandler` plugin point in `:core` (implementations in `bills`: `BILL_PAY`/`BUY_AIRTIME`, and `savings`: `SAVINGS_DEPOSIT`). Each action reuses the exact same `IdempotencyService.replayOrExecute` path its single-action endpoint already uses, so resubmitting a batch after a partial network failure replays already-completed actions instead of double-executing them — live-verified with a fixed idempotency key across two full submissions, identical resulting transaction id both times. One action failing never fails the batch: live-verified a single 3-action batch that real-200'd a real airtime purchase, real-400'd an unrecognized action type, and real-404'd a deposit to a nonexistent goal, together in one response; also live-verified a real end-to-end `BILL_PAY` and a real `SAVINGS_DEPOSIT` correctly 422ing with `INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS`. Deliberately excludes wallet transfer — `WalletService`'s quote/confirm split has a real 60-second quote expiry, so queuing a transfer confirm offline would be confirming a stale or nonexistent quote by replay time. **Android client built and live-verified same day**: new `OfflineActionQueue` (plain SharedPreferences + Gson, not Room — this repo has no Room/KSP/kapt toolchain anywhere and the Gradle graph already carries real documented version-alignment fragility, so a new annotation-processing pipeline for one small queue wasn't worth the risk) and `ConnectivityObserver` (real `ConnectivityManager.NetworkCallback`), wired into `MainViewModel.depositToSavingsGoal` — scoped to `SAVINGS_DEPOSIT` only, matching the backend's supported types and the same transfer-exclusion reasoning. Live-verified on a real emulator with real connectivity toggling (`svc wifi`/`svc data disable`): a deposit attempted while genuinely offline was durably queued (survived a full app-process kill and relaunch while still offline, confirmed via UI), then automatically replayed the moment connectivity was restored — the real backend's `SavingsGoal.currentAmount` moved from 0 to 200 with zero further user action, confirmed both in the app UI (wallet balance 5,000 → 4,800) and directly against the backend (`GET /api/v1/savings/goals`). A real, pre-existing crash bug was found and fixed along the way (blocking verification, though unrelated to the queue itself): `MainViewModel.fetchData()` didn't catch `HttpException` around `getInterestJar()`, so any account with no interest jar yet (every brand-new account) got a real `404` that crashed the entire app on first launch — fixed by treating that specific 404 as "no jar yet," not a fatal error. **iOS client built and live-verified same day**: new `OfflineActionQueue.swift` (plain `UserDefaults` + `Codable`, same "no new persistence framework for one small queue" reasoning as Android's) and `ConnectivityObserver.swift` (real `NWPathMonitor`), wired into `TransferViewModel.depositToSavingsGoal` with the identical `SAVINGS_DEPOSIT`-only scope. Building this surfaced a real design gap neither platform's initial pass caught: `NWPathMonitor`/`ConnectivityManager` only observe the *device's* network interface, not whether *this app's specific backend* is reachable — confirmed live by killing the local dev backend process on the host Mac while the Simulator's shared network interface never changed state, so the interface-level signal alone never fires in that real, common failure mode (a backend outage with the device's own network fine). Fixed with a second, real trigger: `BankViewModel.load()` opportunistically retries the queue after every successful fetch, plus a genuine periodic retry timer (10s) as the durable fallback once the first opportunistic attempt lands too early (a real outage doesn't resolve in the 1.5 seconds before that first retry). Also found and fixed the same class of pre-existing crash-adjacent bug Android had: `BankViewModel.load()`'s `getInterestJar()` call wasn't scoped to catch a 404 for a brand-new account, silently skipping the savings-goals fetch that followed it in the same `do` block. All three real behaviors were independently confirmed live, verified directly against the running backend's API, not just visually: (1) a deposit attempted while the backend was genuinely down (killed, not merely simulated) produced the real "Saved offline" confirmation and was durably queued; (2) that queue survived a full app-process kill and relaunch while still offline; (3) once the backend came back, the periodic retry replayed it for real with zero manual interaction — a goal's `currentAmount` moved from 0 to 5,000, confirmed via a direct `GET /api/v1/savings/goals` call. Honest caveat: a single fully-automated, fully-green run of the XCUITest covering this end-to-end was not achieved — the host Mac was running two Multipass VMs plus an Android emulator alongside Xcode/the Simulator, and under that real memory pressure the app was repeatedly jetsam-killed by the OS mid-run (confirmed via `xcrun simctl spawn ... log show` showing a real SIGKILL from `runningboardd`, not a Swift-level crash) — a resource-contention artifact of this specific environment and orchestration approach (an external harness racing a Gradle/JVM backend restart against the test's own timers), not a defect in the app code, which is why the behaviors above were each confirmed via direct backend-state checks rather than relying on one clean automated pass. The test (`OfflineQueueLiveTests.swift`) is left in the repo as real, valid coverage for a less resource-constrained rerun |
| Device Security | Device-bound sessions (Toss's own real Gateway/Passport architecture) | Self-hosted device binding with zero third-party identity/SMS dependency | real (backend: device recording, step-up re-verification, self-service device management, enforced on every money-moving endpoint) / real (bank-mfe UI: step-up prompt on a real 403, a real Devices tab) / real (Android + iOS: same real deviceId-at-login, step-up dialog on Transfer/Savings-deposit/Interest-claim, Devices list+revoke in Settings) | New, 2026-07-20, at the user's direct request for "a clear and secure way to verify our users without depending on third party since bank needs high security" -- researched Toss's own real, published security architecture (toss.tech's Gateway article, support.toss.im/security) rather than guessing. The one real, novel, self-hosted-friendly gap it surfaced: Toss's gateway issues a real "Passport" token carrying both user AND device identity, checked on every request -- itunda's own JWTs never carried any device binding at all, meaning a leaked or stolen access token would work from any device, forever, until natural expiry. New `TrustedDevice` entity (migration V53, `rw.itunda.auth.DeviceService`): the device used to register is auto-trusted (it just proved password ownership in that same request); a login from an unrecognized device still succeeds (real bank UX -- you can sign in and look around) but is recorded untrusted until a real step-up re-verification (password re-entry) explicitly trusts it. Real anti-spam limit on the step-up endpoint itself (5/minute per user). New `DeviceVerificationFilter` enforces this by reusing the real `Idempotency-Key` header every money-moving endpoint in this backend already requires -- no hand-maintained second list of "sensitive" URL patterns that could silently drift out of sync as new money-moving features are added. Deliberately, honestly backward-compatible: a session with no `deviceId` claim at all (an older, not-yet-updated client, or a token minted before this feature existed) is untouched by the filter -- forcing every existing session to suddenly fail would be a real regression, not a security improvement, so each client opts in only once it starts sending a real `deviceId` at login, the same additive rollout shape this whole project uses for every other optional capability. New endpoints: `GET/POST /api/v1/auth/devices`, `POST /devices/verify`, `DELETE /devices/{id}` -- list/verify/revoke, the same self-service device management Toss's own security settings page offers. `:auth:test` grew by 9 real cases (auto-trust on registration, untrusted-on-first-login plus a real notification, no-op for a device-less registration, correct password verifies, wrong password real-400s, a device-less session real-400s before ever checking a password, revoke works, revoke of a nonexistent device real-404s). Live-verified end-to-end against a real running backend + MySQL, covering the entire loop: registered device_A (auto-trusted), sent a real P2P transfer from it successfully; logged in from a brand-new device_B, confirmed it could log in and read its own profile but got a real 403 `DEVICE_NOT_VERIFIED` attempting to send money; verified device_B with the wrong password (real 400) then the correct one (real 200, now trusted); confirmed the identical P2P send now succeeds from device_B; confirmed a client sending no `deviceId` at all still works with zero regression (the honest backward-compat case); listed both devices with the correct trusted/untrusted status; revoked device_A and confirmed it disappeared from the list. `bank-mfe` UI shipped the same day: `login()` now sends a real, stable per-browser `deviceId` (persisted in `localStorage`) plus a real browser/OS label derived from `navigator.userAgent`; `TransferFlow` catches a real 403 `DEVICE_NOT_VERIFIED` and shows an inline step-up prompt instead of a generic error; a new Devices tab lists every real device on the account with a real "Remove" action. Live-verified in a real headless-browser pass against the real running backend: registered via a seed CLI device (not the browser), logged in fresh (a genuinely new, untrusted device), attempted a transfer and saw the real step-up prompt, verified the device, retried -- the transfer succeeded -- then confirmed the Devices tab correctly showed both the seed device and "(this device)" as trusted, screenshot-confirmed, zero unexpected console errors (the one 403 logged is the expected rejection itself, the same honest pattern this file already documents elsewhere). `tsc -b`/`vite build` both clean. **Android and iOS ported 2026-07-21**, closing the mobile half of this row's own named follow-up: both platforms persist a stable per-install deviceId (Android SharedPreferences, iOS UserDefaults, matching bank-mfe's localStorage choice -- not a secret, so no Keychain/EncryptedSharedPreferences needed) sent at login/register, catch a real 403 `DEVICE_NOT_VERIFIED` on `sendTransfer`/savings deposit/interest claim with a step-up dialog (password re-entry -> `POST /devices/verify` -> retry the original action), and list/revoke real devices in Settings. iOS-specific: rather than widening `NetworkError.httpError`'s arity (which every existing catch site across the target would need updating for), added a purely additive `.deviceNotVerified` case, detected only when a call carries an `Idempotency-Key` -- the same real signal `DeviceVerificationFilter` itself gates on. Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin`/`:features:payments:impl:compileDebugKotlin` clean; iOS `xcodebuild -scheme ItundaApp` real `BUILD SUCCEEDED` (required installing `tuist` and registering the two new source files, since `ios/*.xcodeproj/` is gitignored and normally regenerated via `tuist generate`). No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] -- the underlying endpoint was already fully live-verified server-side and via bank-mfe one day earlier; this is a straight port of already-proven logic onto an already-verified endpoint. **Gift/Commerce/Eats/Stocks closed 2026-07-21, across all three platforms.** Scoped by checking which calls actually carry an `Idempotency-Key` (the exact signal `DeviceVerificationFilter` gates on) rather than assuming every catch site in these large files was in scope -- most weren't (post/listing/offer CRUD never sends one). The real gap was 5 flows: Gift send/claim, Commerce checkout, Eats checkout, Stocks buy/sell. bank-mfe: same `needsDeviceVerification`/`DeviceStepUpPrompt` pattern added to `StockDetailSheet`/`ConversationThread`/`EatsCheckoutView`/`MultiCartView` (the last stops its per-merchant order loop at the first device-not-verified rather than collecting N duplicate failures, since one checkout shares one device/session). Android: extracted `isDeviceNotVerifiedError` + a new `DeviceStepUpHost` composable so the pattern didn't need hand-duplicating a fourth time. iOS: added the SwiftUI equivalent `DeviceStepUpHost` -- `NetworkClient.swift` itself needed zero further changes, since the additive `.deviceNotVerified` case already fires for every idempotency-keyed call through the shared `authenticatedPost`, not just the original three. Verified real: bank-mfe `tsc -b && vite build` clean, Android `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean, iOS `xcodebuild -scheme ItundaApp` real `BUILD SUCCEEDED`. **Checked bill pay specifically, since it was named above as unchecked: it doesn't need this fix at all yet** -- `POST /bills/pay` has zero client call sites on any platform (Android/iOS/bank-mfe), confirmed by grepping each network layer; the Bills row above is real on the backend only, no client UI exists to wire a step-up dialog into. **Push wired into the real NEW_DEVICE_LOGIN security alert, 2026-07-28** -- same real urgency FraudReviewService's confirmed-fraud alert already established: a compromised account's real owner needs to know the instant an unrecognized device signs in, not next app-open. Unit-verified (`:auth:test`, 10/10 passing, including a case confirming push does NOT fire for an already-known device). Live-verified end-to-end against itunda.duckdns.org: logging in with two different real deviceIds each produced a real NEW_DEVICE_LOGIN notification (confirmed via GET /api/v1/notifications) naming the correct device. **Still honestly open: any future money-moving flow (including a real Bills client UI, whenever built) will need this wiring individually -- there's no single choke point that would auto-cover a brand-new endpoint.** **merchant-mfe ported 2026-07-28** -- the last real client gap this row's own "still honestly open" note implicitly covered (rider-app/merchant-app native apps still don't send a deviceId at all, a real, separate, not-yet-started follow-up). Same exact pattern bank-mfe already shipped: `lib/device.ts` (per-browser UUID in localStorage, `itunda_merchant_device_id` key), `login()` now sends `deviceId`/`deviceName` (merchant-mfe never calls account registration -- a merchant registers their business only after already being an itunda user elsewhere, so this app's own device is genuinely untrusted the first time, exactly correct behavior for a device the backend has never seen), a shared `DeviceStepUpPrompt` component wired into both real money-moving call sites (`chargeCard` in `CollectScreen`/`PosScreen`, `runPayroll` in `PayrollScreen`), and a Devices list/revoke card added to `SettingsScreen`. `tsc -b` and `vite build` both clean; no interactive click-through this pass, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] and the exact precedent the Android/iOS port itself set -- a straight port of already-proven logic onto an already-live-verified backend endpoint. **Native MerchantApp ported 2026-07-28 (item 97), closing the "rider-app/merchant-app native apps still don't send a deviceId" follow-up merchant-mfe's own entry named** -- checked first rather than assumed: native `RiderApp` (Android + iOS) has zero `Idempotency-Key`-carrying endpoints (confirmed via fresh grep), so device binding is genuinely moot there. Native `MerchantApp` -- a third, separate app from both merchant-mfe (web) and the main itunda app, with its own standalone `NetworkClient`/`TokenStore`, no dependency on `:core:network` (Android) or the main app's device layer (iOS) -- does have real money-moving endpoints: Android has `chargeCard` (`PosScreen.kt`) and `moveToBusiness`/`moveToPersonal` (`BusinessAccountScreen.kt`); iOS has only `chargeCard` -- `moveToBusiness`/`moveToPersonal` doesn't exist on iOS at all, a real, separate, pre-existing platform-parity gap not addressed here. Same exact pattern as every prior port: Android gained `DeviceStore.kt` (unencrypted SharedPreferences, `itunda_merchant_device` key), `LoginRequest` gained `deviceId`/`deviceName`, `isDeviceNotVerifiedError` on `retrofit2.HttpException`, and a self-contained `DeviceStepUpDialog` composable wired into both `chargeCard` and `moveToBusiness`/`moveToPersonal`'s catch blocks. iOS gained `DeviceStore.swift` (UserDefaults, `itunda_merchant_device_id` key), `LoginRequest` gained the same two fields, a purely-additive `.deviceNotVerified` case on this app's own `NetworkError` (detected only when a call carries an `Idempotency-Key`, same signal as every other platform), and a SwiftUI `DeviceStepUpDialog` wired into `chargeCard`'s catch block. Deliberately, honestly out of scope: neither native app has an existing Settings screen, so no Devices list/revoke UI was added to either -- a scoping decision, not a silent omission. Android `:merchantapp:compileDebugKotlin` clean (first pass genuinely failed on an unresolved `rememberSaveable` reference -- merchantapp's Compose dependency set doesn't include the saveable-state artifact the main app pulls in; fixed by switching to plain `remember`, matching every other state variable already in this module). iOS `xcodebuild -workspace Itunda.xcworkspace -scheme ItundaMerchantApp -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator'` real `BUILD SUCCEEDED` (required a `tuist generate` first pass to pick up the two new source files via the target's `MerchantApp/Sources/**` glob, since the checked-in `.xcodeproj` predated them). No interactive on-device/Simulator click-through this pass, matching [[feedback_verification_pace]] -- straight port of already-proven, already-live-verified logic. |
| Rideshare | Kakao T-style ride-hailing (택시 호출) | Real passenger trip request + driver dispatch, backend-only this pass | real (backend: driver registration, real trip request/dispatch/accept/decline/start/complete/cancel lifecycle, real distance-based fare, real escrow-held payment, real acceptance-rate-filtered + distance-ranked dispatch, real single-trip-at-a-time enforcement) | New `rw.itunda.rideshare` module (migration `V115`, `RideDriver`/`RideTrip` entities), the "big swing" round-2 candidate deliberately deferred until every smaller sourced gap from the same research batch was closed first. Sourced directly from Kakao Mobility's own official page (kakaomobility.com/contents/taxi-dispatch): real dispatch ranks candidate drivers by acceptance-prediction, daily completion volume, rating, real acceptance rate, and ETA among qualifying candidates; a real, documented finding that cancellation rate jumps from 9.8% to 28.8% once a wait crosses 15-18 seconds; AI dispatch cut average wait time ~39% and grew daily riders by ~250,000. **Honest v1 scope**: no real ML acceptance-prediction model exists (Kakao's own sourced >90% ROC-AUC figure describes a trained model this repo has no data or infrastructure to build) -- the honest, rule-based proxy for the same real signals is a real, computed acceptance rate (`RideDriver.totalOffers`/`totalAccepted`, an exact ratio from a driver's own actual accept/decline history, not a prediction) used as a real minimum-quality filter (excluding drivers below a real 30% rate, but only once they have a real statistically meaningful sample of 5+ offers, so a brand-new driver is never unfairly excluded for lack of data), then real `GeoUtils.haversineKm` distance as the tiebreaker among qualifying candidates -- structurally the same "coarse filter, real rank" shape `EatsOrderService.rankNearbyRiders` already established, reused for a genuinely distinct domain. **The real 15-second offer window isn't an invented number -- it's directly Kakao's own sourced cancellation-surge threshold.** **Real push wired into the ride-offer notification, 2026-07-28** -- the single most time-critical notification site found across this whole push-notification rollout: unlike every other in-app-only notification in this backend, a driver who misses this one within the real 15-second window doesn't just see it late, they lose the offer entirely to the next-ranked driver. Unit-verified (`:rideshare:test`, 8/8 passing): the offered driver's own `pushNotificationService.sendToUser` is asserted alongside the pre-existing in-app `Notification`. Fare = a real itunda-scoped base fare (1,000 RWF, reflecting a taxi meter's flag-fall convention) + the exact same real per-km rate `EatsOrderService.perKmDeliveryRate` already established (250 RWF/km, reused rather than inventing a second one, since Kakao's own page discloses no fare/surge mechanics to reuse instead), with a 1,500 RWF floor. Fare escrow/payout/refund mirrors `EatsOrderService`'s own delivery-fee-holding pattern exactly: held in a new `ride_holding` clearing account from request time, paid to the driver net of a 1.5% platform fee at `COMPLETED`, refunded in full if `CANCELLED` while still `REQUESTED` (before any driver has committed) -- the same safest, simplest real cancellation scope `EatsOrderStatus.CANCELLED`'s own doc comment already chose, no invented cancellation-fee policy. A real single-trip-at-a-time guarantee was built in from the start this time (not found as a live bug the way Eats' own equivalent was) -- a driver can only ever carry one active trip, real-409 (`RIDE_DRIVER_ALREADY_ON_TRIP`) otherwise, and the dispatch candidate pool excludes busy drivers too. 6 new unit tests (`RideTripServiceTest`) cover fare computation + escrow + dispatch, insufficient-funds rejection, the single-trip guard, payout math, cancellation refund, and the acceptance-rate filter skipping a closer-but-unreliable driver in favor of a farther, reliable one. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster with real accounts: a passenger requested a real 1.068km trip, correctly computed and escrowed the real 1,500 RWF fare floor (wallet 30,000 → 28,500), the one online driver received a real dispatch offer with the correct fare in the notification body, accepted it (`totalOffers`/`totalAccepted` both correctly incremented to 1), started and completed the trip -- the driver's wallet correctly credited the exact real net-of-fee payout (0 → 1,477.50, i.e. 1,500 − 22.50). Separately verified: an explicit decline correctly excluded the driver and left the trip openly `REQUESTED` with no other real candidate available; cancelling that same still-`REQUESTED` trip correctly refunded the full fare (wallet returned to exactly 28,500); with the driver mid-trip on a third real request, a fourth real trip request correctly found no dispatchable candidate (busy-exclusion working inside the ranking, not just at accept time), and the busy driver's explicit accept attempt on it correctly real-409'd. **Honestly scoped**: backend-only this pass, no Android/iOS/bank-mfe UI touchpoint yet, matching this project's own established "backend-first, UI as a named follow-up" precedent (`WeeklySavingsService`, `MarketplaceEscrow`, `MerchantCoupon`/`MerchantAd`/`MerchantBookingReview` all shipped the same way). No real road-distance routing (self-hosted OSRM) used for fare/ranking -- straight-line `GeoUtils.haversineKm` only, the same honest simplification `GeoUtils.kt`'s own doc comment already names for every other distance-based feature in this backend. No real ML acceptance-prediction, no surge pricing, no scheduled/multi-stop rides, no driver ratings UI -- all real, named, deliberately deferred follow-ups. **bank-mfe UI wired 2026-07-26**, closing the "backend real, no client" gap named above -- new `Rides` tab with a `Get a ride`/`Drive` toggle: passengers search a pickup/dropoff address (reusing `MapsController`'s own `/api/v1/maps/search`) and request a trip with a live-polling status card (cancel while not yet in progress); drivers register, go online/offline (auto-sharing their real browser geolocation on going online), and accept/decline/start/complete real trip requests, all against the exact same endpoints as above, zero new backend surface. Full trip lifecycle (request → auto-dispatch offer → accept → start → complete, real fare paid to the driver net of platform fee) re-verified end-to-end against the live deployed cluster using raw coordinates. **Found live while verifying this pass, not caused by it, and fixed the same day**: itunda's self-hosted Nominatim/OSRM/tile/glyph stack briefly appeared down cluster-wide right after wiring this UI (search/reverse/directions/tiles all real-empty or 404) -- root-caused and fixed as two config bugs, not a lost service; see the Maps row's own full account for the real story (nothing here needed a code change once that landed). **Android client added 2026-07-28 (item 109)** -- new `RideScreen.kt`: passenger request/cancel (one-tap "use my location" for pickup via the shared `rememberRealLocationRequester` helper already proven in the Hood feature; manual dropoff address/lat/lng entry this pass, no autocomplete search integration -- an honest, named v1 scope-down), driver register/go-online/accept/decline/start/complete, both with live 4-second polling matching bank-mfe's own cadence. `:core:network:compileDebugKotlin` clean; a full `:app:compileDebugKotlin` still hits the known pre-existing `react-native-safe-area-context` issue (see [[project_itunda_android_build_env]]) before ever reaching this file, so verified via that clean compile plus a manual cross-check. **iOS client added 2026-07-28 (item 110), closing this client thread entirely** -- new `RideScreenView.swift` (same passenger/driver flows as Android, one-tap pickup location via a new private `CLLocationManager` wrapper matching `HoodScreen.swift`/`MapScreenView.swift`'s own established per-file pattern), wired into `BenefitsShopAllScreens.swift`; `NetworkClient.swift` gained the full matching endpoint/DTO set. | Agent Network | MTN MoMo/M-Pesa-style cash agent network | Real cash-in/cash-out at a physical agent till, with a real per-transaction commission | real | A mature `rw.itunda.agents` module (float management, receipt tracking, daily limits, till reconciliation) existed from earlier in this session with no dedicated row in this file until now -- adding one to correctly track it. **Real agent commission added 2026-07-27, closing a real gap found by re-reading this already-shipped code**: a real human could operate a till (accepting/paying out cash, `AgentService.cashIn`/`cashOut`) but earned literally nothing for providing the service, unlike every real mobile money agent network this module is modeled on. Sourced from MTN Mobile Money's own real, published agent commission mechanic (momo.mtn.com/agents, momo.mtn.com/pricing): a flat fee for small transactions, a percentage-based commission that increases by real transaction band for larger ones, rewarding agents who handle higher transaction values -- a concrete real rate card was found for MTN MoMo Ghana specifically (jbklutse.com/mtn-momo-fees-2026), not Rwanda's own, which doesn't appear to be published anywhere. Honest scope: the real STRUCTURE (flat-then-tiered-percentage, real per-transaction cap) is sourced; the exact RWF amounts/bands (`AgentCommissionSchedule`: flat 50 RWF up to 5,000; 1% up to 20,000; 1.5% up to 100,000; 2% above that, capped at 3,000 RWF per transaction) are itunda's own honest adaptation for its own Rwanda-modeled network, not presented as copied from a real published Rwanda table. Commission is credited to whichever real operator actually handled the transaction (`acceptedByUserId`/`paidByUserId`, resolved to their own real MAIN wallet) as two extra legs folded into the SAME ledger transaction as the underlying cash-in/cash-out -- one atomic settlement, not a second call -- debiting a new dedicated `AGENT_COMMISSION_EXPENSE` ledger account and crediting the operator's wallet; honestly skipped (never blocking the real customer's own cash-in/cash-out) if the operator has no real wallet of their own, which shouldn't happen for a real registered operator but isn't worth risking real customer money on that assumption. **A real, costly bug found live during this pass's own verification, worth recording in detail**: the very first real cash-in against the deployed cluster real-500'd with `IllegalStateException: Unknown ledger account agent_commission_expense`. Root cause: adding a new `LedgerAccountType` enum value is not enough on its own -- `LedgerAccountType` is a pure Kotlin enum, but the actual DB row a ledger leg references comes from a separate seed list, `LedgerAccount.SEED_IDS` (inserted idempotently at boot by `SeedDataRunner`), and this pass added the enum value without also adding it there. **The same exact latent bug already existed for the immediately-prior overdraft item's own `interest_income` account** -- it simply hadn't been caught yet, because that account's own real-time interest-accrual scheduler had never actually fired against the live cluster during that item's own verification pass (only unit-tested). Both missing rows were added to `SEED_IDS` in the same fix, redeployed, and re-verified. **Reusable lesson for future ledger work**: a new `LedgerAccountType` enum value needs BOTH the enum entry AND a matching `SEED_IDS` row before its first real use, or a real live 500 follows the first time -- and any freshly-added clearing account that hasn't yet been exercised by a live code path (like `interest_income` at the time) should be treated as unverified even after the enum change compiles and unit tests pass, since neither catches a missing seed row. 6 new `AgentServiceTest` cases (tiered commission crediting as extra ledger legs, the honest no-wallet skip, and the exact tier-boundary/cap math in isolation). Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster across two redeployed images (the second fixing the `SEED_IDS` bug): a real cash-in of 15,000 RWF credited the customer's wallet with 15,000 and the operator's own wallet with exactly 150 (the 1% tier-2 band); 60,000 RWF credited 900 (1.5% tier-3); 150,000 RWF credited exactly 3,000 (2% tier-4, landing exactly at the real cap); a further 200,000 RWF (whose uncapped 2% would be 4,000) correctly credited only 3,000, proving the real per-transaction cap actually applies -- the operator's wallet correctly accumulated to exactly 7,050 RWF across all four real transactions. Real mobile push notifications closed 2026-07-28 for both `cashIn` and `cashOut` -- the most fundamental notification in real mobile money (instant confirmation a customer's cash was just added or withdrawn at a physical agent till), matching the exact MTN MoMo/M-Pesa real-time SMS-confirmation pattern this whole module already mirrors. Also fixed a real gap this pass found while wiring it: `AgentService` is constructed across 6 separate test files (not just the obviously-named `AgentServiceTest.kt`), all updated for the new constructor param. |

New `WalletType.MINI` (confirmed zero blast radius first -- no exhaustive `when (WalletType)` exists anywhere in this codebase) + `MiniWalletService`/`-Controller`: `openMiniWallet` is idempotent (an already-open Mini wallet just returns itself, same `KeywordAlertService.addAlert` discipline), `deposit` is a real self-funded `MAIN`-to-`MINI` `WALLET`-to-`WALLET` ledger transfer (the exact real movement `StocksService.fundInvestmentWallet` already proved live) that validates all three real caps before ever posting the ledger entry -- a rejected deposit never touches the ledger at all. **Honestly, deliberately scoped down from the real product, per explicit user approval**: no real age-eligibility gate (`User` has no birthdate/age field anywhere in this backend -- adding one would mean a materially bigger registration-flow change than this pass) and no merchant-category restriction (blocking specific harmful-to-minors merchant categories needs a real card-transaction-category classification this codebase doesn't have yet); both named here as real, still-open gaps rather than silently absent. 8 new unit tests. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster: a real account opened a real zero-balance Mini wallet, deposited a real 50,000 RWF within all three caps, a further 500,000 RWF deposit correctly real-422'd as `MINI_WALLET_BALANCE_CAP_EXCEEDED` (would have pushed the real balance to 550,000, over the 500,000 cap), and a further 260,000 RWF deposit correctly real-422'd as `MINI_WALLET_DAILY_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` specifically -- not the balance cap -- since the resulting 310,000 RWF balance sits under the 500,000 cap but the real 310,000 RWF deposited that same day exceeds the real 300,000 daily limit. **Real age-eligibility gate added 2026-07-28 (item 98), closing this row's own previously-named "no age-eligibility gate" scope-down** -- confirmed via hankyung.com's and sedaily.com's own coverage of KakaoBank's real 2023 age-lowering ("가입 연령 7∼18세로 확대"), extending the heraldcorp.com source this row already cited for the age-lowering-to-7 fact: the real product's eligible window is 만 7세~18세 (age 7-18 inclusive, 19+ ineligible). New `User.birthDate` (migration V150, nullable, opt-in -- same shape as `neighborhood`/`profilePhotoUrl` on this same entity) set once via new `POST /api/v1/auth/profile/birth-date` (`AuthService.setBirthDate`, rejects a future or implausible date). `MiniWalletService.openMiniWallet` now real-422s `MINI_WALLET_BIRTH_DATE_REQUIRED` if no birth date is on file, and `MINI_WALLET_AGE_INELIGIBLE` if the computed age falls outside 7-18 -- both checked before ever touching the MAIN-wallet lookup. **Still honestly out of scope**: the real product's under-14 legal-guardian-consent requirement (a genuine guardian-approval workflow this codebase has no concept of yet) and merchant-category restriction (needs real card-transaction-category classification this codebase doesn't have) -- both real, separate, not-yet-started follow-ups, named here rather than silently absent. 5 new `:wallet:test` cases (no birth date, too young, too old, exact boundary ages 7 and 18 inclusive) plus 3 new `:auth:test` cases for `setBirthDate` itself -- 12/12 and 33/33 respectively. Full monorepo `clean compileKotlin compileTestKotlin` + `test` sweep clean, zero failures across every module. Live-verified end-to-end against the deployed cluster with a fresh real account: opening a Mini wallet with no birth date on file correctly real-422'd `MINI_WALLET_BIRTH_DATE_REQUIRED`; setting a birth date making them 5 years old then correctly real-422'd `MINI_WALLET_AGE_INELIGIBLE` on the same open call; setting a birth date making them 15 correctly real-201'd a genuine new Mini wallet. **First client UI added 2026-07-28 (item 99)** -- found the feature had zero client anywhere on any platform despite being real and live since its original 2026-07-28 launch. New `MiniWalletCard` on bank-mfe's Home tab (`lib/miniWallet.ts`'s `openMiniWallet`/`depositToMiniWallet`, `lib/neighborhood.ts` gained `setBirthDate`, `Wallet`'s type union gained `'MINI'`): opening inline-collects a birth date only if the real backend 422s `MINI_WALLET_BIRTH_DATE_REQUIRED`, shows a real "ages 7-18" message on `MINI_WALLET_AGE_INELIGIBLE`, and otherwise renders the real balance + an "Add money" deposit form once open -- the exact same "collect only when the backend actually asks for it" flow this codebase already uses elsewhere, not a speculative upfront form. `tsc -b` and `vite build` both clean (one cosmetic `INEFFECTIVE_DYNAMIC_IMPORT` warning matching this app's own pre-existing pattern).
